# pqdaw architecture

This document fixes the module layout and the interfaces between modules so that several agents can work in parallel without colliding. [SPEC.md](../SPEC.md) says *what* to build. This file says *where it goes and how the parts talk*. If an interface here has to change, change this file in the same commit and log it in `docs/PROGRESS.md`.

## 1. Modules and dependency direction

```
                    ┌──────────┐
                    │   app    │  pqdaw.exe: wires everything together, CLI flags, main loop, TransportPort impl
                    └────┬─────┘
        ┌────────────────┼──────────────────┬─────────────┐
        ▼                │                  ▼             ▼
   ┌─────────┐           │            ┌──────────┐   ┌────────┐
   │  views  │ (T3.7)    │            │  engine  │   │  io    │ (inside engine/ for now: audio + MIDI devices)
   └──┬───┬──┘           │            └────┬─────┘   └────────┘
      │   └──────────┐   │                 ├────────────┬──────────────────┐
      ▼              ▼   ▼                 │            ▲                  │
   ┌─────────┐     ┌──────────┐            │        ┌───────┐              │
   │   ui    │     │ document │            │        │  jit  │              │
   └────┬────┘     └────┬─────┘            ▼        └───┬───┘              │
        │               │              ┌───────┐        │                  │
        │               └─────────────▶│ graph │◀───────┘                  │
        │                              └───┬───┘                           ▼
        │                      ┌───────────┴────────┐                 ┌─────────┐
        │                      ▼                    ▼                 │  media  │ (T5.1: decoding, sample pool, streaming)
        │                 ┌────────┐           ┌────────┐             └────┬────┘
        │                 │ tuning │           │  dsp   │◀─────────────────┤
        │                 └───┬────┘           └───┬────┘                  │
        ▼                     ▼                    ▼                       ▼
   ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │                                    core                                   │
   └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   ┌───────────┐
   │  library  │ (T5.9: browser index, watcher, search, tags) ──▶ document, media      (views ──▶ library since T5.10)
   └───────────┘
```

Arrows point at dependencies. A module may only include headers from modules below it. **No cycles.** `jit` sits above `engine` (T1b.1): it produces `engine::ProgramImage`s, so it includes engine headers; the engine never includes `jit` — whoever owns the compiler worker (the app) hands compiled images to the engine (QUESTIONS Q63). `compile` (T1.x-arch2) sits above `engine` + `jit`: the one file → Schedule → image pipeline and the process's JIT compiler, used by `tools/pqc` and `app`. `ui` does not depend on `document` or `engine`. `views` (T3.7) composes `ui` + `document` (+ `tuning`, `core`) and never depends on `engine`: it reaches the transport through the plain-data `TransportPort` interface, which `app` implements (`ProjectLink` over the engine since T3.17; the wall-clock `SimulatedTransport` remains only as a fallback when playback cannot start). `app` depends on `views` + `engine` + `compile`. `document` depends on `graph` for the project → graph lowering (T3.17, `pq/document/lower.h`); it never includes `engine`. `meta` (TMM.3) sits above `graph` (+ `dsp`, `core`) and below `document`: the meta-module types and their expansions to graph patches; `document` (state in the model, the lowering) and `compile` (the prelude that makes `meta_<type>(…)` calls of a standalone file expand with the default state) depend on it, `views` depends on it directly since TMM.4 (the faces read types, laws and the prelude), and `engine` never includes it (it sees only `dsp` tables through `Resources`). `media` (T5.1) depends on `core` + `dsp` only; `engine` depends on it (the RT read side of streaming is its header-only `pq/media/stream.h`; `pq/engine/wav_file.h` forwards to `pq/media/wav_file.h`), and `views` depends on it since T5.5 (waveform overviews; `ui` stays independent of `media`: `ui::drawWaveform` takes the overview as plain spans, `pq/ui/waveform.h`). `library` (T5.9) depends on `media` (probes) + `document` (the stock device catalog for the Instruments / Audio Effects categories); nothing below it includes it, `views` does since T5.10 (the browser panel, `pq/views/browser/`; the drop code classifies files with `library::indexedKind`), and it never touches the audio thread. Headers that most view code includes (`view_context.h`, `view_scenes.h`, the arrangement's) do not include `pq/library/*`: the namespace name `library` collides with local helpers of that name in tests that say `using namespace pq;`.

| Module | CMake target | Namespace | Contents |
|---|---|---|---|
| `src/core` | `pq_core` | `pq` | RT-safe primitives: SPSC/MPSC queues, pools, arena, epoch reclamation, allocation trap, denormal guard, aligned buffers, fast math approximations, `Ticks`/time types, stable IDs, non-RT logging plus an RT log ring, voice taps (`pq/core/voice_tap.h`, TMM.2: per-voice-group lossy record rings a meta module's face reads its dots from — 8 sampled values per lane per record taken every `stride` frames of the group's own count, the position, the increment, the lane mask the image host sets; written only while a `VoiceTapReader` is registered; the reader keeps per voice slot the newest record, activity and a sampled-value history), analysis taps (`pq/core/tap_ring.h`, T4.6: the audio thread copies a signal into a lossy ring that one analysis thread reads; nothing is copied while no reader is registered; TX.spectrum: param-rate channels repeated, `constMask`), probe levels (`pq/core/probe_level.h`, T4.5: last / min / max of a block, one writer, any reader) |
| `src/tuning` | `pq_tuning` | `pq::tuning` | EDO, MOS (port of mos-explorer), Scala, JI (ratios, monzos, naming), pitch identities, resolution to Hz, the tuning-as-signal coefficient form |
| `src/dsp` | `pq_dsp` | `pq::dsp` | Native leaf-atom kernels (scalar + SIMD), the curve model + evaluator + `curve` atom (GU.1, `pq/dsp/curve.h`; see **Curves**), table resources (`pq/dsp/tables.h`; T4.12: built-in band-limited waveform tables, `builtinWavetable(k)`, read by a wavetable atom whose `table` is `kBuiltinWavetableBase` + k), FFT (`pq/dsp/fft.h`, T4.6: radix-2 real FFT on split arrays), the spectrum analyser (`pq/dsp/spectrum.h`, T4.6: PQ Meter's spectrum behaviour, reimplemented), the transfer-function estimate (`pq/dsp/transfer.h`, TX.spectrum: H1 = Σ S_xy / Σ S_xx per display point from a device's input and output, Welch averaging with a long window below 400 Hz, coherence- and level-gated confidence), the analysis-tap sink `signal_tap` (TX.spectrum, `Resource::SignalTap` = a 4-channel TapRing: input L/R, output L/R), analysers ported from PQ Meter (spectrum grid, NSDF pitch, LUFS…), sample-rate conversion (`pq/dsp/resample.h`, T5.4: polyphase windowed-sinc, Kaiser, 64 taps × 256 phases with linear interpolation between phases, 32.32 positions, stretched by tables per q = 256 / stretch for rates above 1, AVX2; `gainCopy` for rate 1; a 16-tap draft quality), frame tables (TMM.1, `pq/dsp/frame_table.h`: `FrameTable` = F dense frames × mip levels by the "sr − 20 kHz" rule, one level per lane from a bucket lookup of the increment, rows 4× oversampled at 8·(7-smooth) samples and read by a 6-point 5th-order interpolator, ≥ 130 dB image rejection, harmonics pre-divided by its response; RT readers `readFrameTableL1` / `readFrameTableL8` (phase, position Spl or per sample, `FrameReadState` = previous phase and delta) for TMM.2's `wavetable_pos` kernel to wrap, plus a double reference; TMM.12-fix1: a table whose every frame is DC + harmonic 1 carries {dc, amplitude, θ} per frame (`FrameTableView::sine`, builder step 6) and the readers compute it as the `sine` atom does whenever every lane rests on one frame for the block (`frameTableSineCoefs` gives the lanes' coefficients); `pq/dsp/frame_table_build.h`: the off-thread `FrameTableBuilder` (keyframes → spectral morph / crossfade per gap → baked warp / fold / sync with analytic discontinuity corrections → levels, mixed-radix double FFT, arenas reused: no allocation once sized), `frameTableSpecHash`, `basicCycle`; `pq/dsp/wave_warp.h`: the one warp / fold / sync formula set shared by the bake, the live path and tests), live frame-table slots (TMM.2, `pq/dsp/frame_table_slot.h`: `FrameTableSlot` = one atomic pointer to an immutable entry {view, owner, generation, the entry it replaced} plus the VoiceTap its readers write; `publish` unlinks and retires through an EpochReclaimer only entries two or more back that were superseded ≥ 0.5 s ago (AU.fold; at most 64 kept); the `wavetable_pos` kernel (kernels_frame_table.cpp) does one acquire load per call and on a generation change crossfades over `kFrameTableFadeSeconds` (25 ms) from the table it played, finding it on the chain (`FrameTableSlot::find`); a publish during a fade waits for it to end, then the fade goes on to the newest (`frameTableFade`), so a knob drag's stream of tables is one continuous crossfade, never a jump; `voice_tap` is the generic sink), the Wavetable's live path and Shape mode (TMM.8, `pq/dsp/wave_live.h`, kernels_wave_live.cpp: `wavetable_live(phase, position, warp, fold, sync; table, mode, corr)` applies sync → warp → fold per sample with the wave_warp.h formulas (param-rate controls ramp over the block), reads each tap at a level pair chosen from its instantaneous speed × `liveLevelScale` (the region factor's headroom kLiveBandScale ½ on BASE-rate speeds from the base mip level's representative increment `liveRepIncrement` (TMM.8-fix: factor-independent, the same rule as the live-band bake), × the fold's spread 1 + θ) and crossfaded by a weight linear in the speed (`FrameTableView::bucketRich` / `bucketTop` / `bucketInvSpan`), and with `corr` corrects every sync reset, cycle wrap and warp kink with band-limited step / ramp residuals (Kaiser-windowed sinc, half-width 4: the output is delayed 4 samples), J and K evaluated in double (`liveValueAtPsi`); `wavetable_shape(x, position; table)`: the table as a transfer curve of x ∈ [−1, 1] (clamped; T(1) = T(0)), first-order ADAA from the table's antiderivative arrays (`FrameTableSpec::antiderivative`: P rows c_h / (2πih), per-frame DC, per-level edge values), the level from a held |Δu| (50 ms release); both swap and tap like `wavetable_pos`, 1 lane == each of 8 lanes; private reader steps shared through `frame_table_simd.h`), the oversample region's rate converters (TMM.6, `pq/dsp/oversample.h`: polyphase IIR half-band cascades ×2 … ×16 designed for ≥ 116 dB over everything that folds below 20 kHz at 44.1 kHz and above, unwrapped phase interpolation, bool hold / any; kernels `oversample_*` whose frames are the oversampled count, fully unrolled per factor, AVX2 over the voice lanes), warp engines later |
| `src/media` | `pq_media` (+ `pq_media_codecs`: the third-party decoders, warnings off) | `pq::media` | T5.1: probing (`pq/media/probe.h`: `SampleInfo` from headers: container, rate, channels, exact frames, bit depth, acid/smpl/INST tempo, root and loop, file-name BPM, NTFS identity; MP3 Xing/LAME gapless), decoding (`pq/media/decoder.h`: one `Decoder` interface, planar float32; own PCM reader for WAV/RF64/W64/AIFF/AIFC, dr_wav for ADPCM/A-law/µ-law, dr_flac, dr_mp3's frame decoder with our own frame table for exact seeks, stb_vorbis; files memory-mapped; third-party heaps capped), PCM conversion kernels (`pq/media/pcm.h`, AVX2), the sample content hash (`pq/media/hash.h`: bit for bit `doc::fileIdentity`'s hash, Q145 item 8, checked by a test), WAV read/write for goldens and renders (`pq/media/wav_file.h`, moved from engine). T5.2: the sample pool and disk streaming (`pq/media/sample_pool.h`: `SamplePool`, `PooledSample`, residency and budget, the disk thread; `pq/media/stream.h`: `StreamPool` slots and `SampleCursor`, the RT read side, header-only). T5.x-perf-audio: mips (`SamplePool::buildMips(sample, levels)`: 2×, 4×, 8× decimated copies of a fully resident sample, `dsp::decimate2`, built on demand off the audio thread, counted in the budget, retired by a demotion; `PooledSample::mips()` one acquire load; streamed samples have none, QUESTIONS Q159) and `SampleCursor::swap`. T5.5: waveform overviews (`pq/media/waveform.h`: `WaveformOverview` = per channel int16 min / max at 256 frames per bucket + ×4 mips to one bucket, allocated whole and filled progressively by `OverviewBuilder` (published every 2^20 frames: `builtFrames` release / `validBuckets` acquire, so readers never see a bucket being written); `buildOverview(Decoder&)`; the disk cache `<dir>/<hash>-<size>.pqwf` (`saveOverview` via `.part` + rename, `loadOverview`, `trimOverviewCache` LRU); `readSampleWindow` for deep zoom; `AnalysisWorker`, one background-mode thread (THREAD_MODE_BACKGROUND_BEGIN); `loadOrBuildOverview`) |
| `src/meta` | `pq_meta` | `pq::meta` | TMM.3 (docs/plans/MM.md): meta modules — subcircuits with their own faces whose state lives in the document. The type registry (`pq/meta/registry.h`: `meta::Type` by id, `registerType`, the reserved call prefix `meta_`, instance names, name paths), each type's controls / inputs / outputs / resources (`pq/meta/type.h`: ParamSpec with its face law Linear / Exp / Exp0, SettingSpec, InputSpec, ResourceRequest), the deterministic, hash-stable EXPANSION (`expand(type, ExpandContext{form Nested / ChainNotes / ChainAudio / ChainVoice (TMM.10-fix1: chosen by what reaches the chain input, never by the type), voice, chainInput, settings, modulated params, slots})` → a `graph::Patch` with fixed NodeIds per port and type-chosen ids per body node + resource requests; `expansionKey`), the PRELUDE (every type's default expansion `meta_<id>` and its signature: parse externals for the loaders), state DATA (`pq/meta/data.h`: an immutable value tree — numbers, strings, float arrays, lists, records — with deep equality, hash and base64 float32; never part of an expansion), and the built-in types (GU.4: the Phase accumulator and the VCA are no longer types — they are group presets of atom and value blocks, `pq/document/modules.h`; GU.f7: a file still holding one is refused on load, as any unknown meta type; `pq/meta/wavetable.h`, TMM.5: the Wavetable — inputs `x` (default 0 — TMM.10-fix1: no implicit note; `meta::Reads::Phase`: at a chain's note source it reads the note's phase `phasor(voice.hz)`) and `pos`, params phase / position / fold / warp / sync, settings mode / warpMode / os (TMM.8), data = keyframes with 2048-sample cycles, gap modes, dense frames, removeDc, normalise (`WavetableData`, `wavetableData`, `parseWavetableData`); fold / warp / sync are `bakeable`: static they are baked into the table; TMM.8: modulated (`ExpandContext::modulated`) in Phase mode the expansion is the LIVE PATH, `oversample(n, phase: ph) { out = wavetable_live(…) }` with the modulated controls wired (a live warp takes sync along) and the others baked (`TableSource::modulated` tells the table job which not to bake; `wavetableOversampling`: the `os` setting, auto ×8 for fold, ×16 with a live warp or a modulated sync); Shape mode (setting `mode` 1) expands to `oversample(n) { out = wavetable_shape(x + phase, pos, table) }` (auto ×2) over a table with its antiderivative arrays and refuses modulated fold / warp / sync (`Type::bakedOnly`); TMM.8-fix: Phase mode bakes with the LIVE band limit (`FrameTableSpec::liveBand`: each mip level is the live read at the level's representative increment, so baked and live agree ≤ −80 dB), and `Type::tableVariant` (which controls are live, Shape mode) rides in the slot index's upper bits so a switch gets its own slot, built before the recompiled image plays (`ProjectLink` waits on the compile path); its chain input (`Type::chainInput`) is x. `pq/meta/envelope.h`, TMM.11: the Envelope — inputs `in` (default 1, `InputDefault::One`: wired, a VCA) and `gate` (a Bool input, `InputSpec::kind`; default voice.gate, `InputDefault::VoiceGate`), params attack / decay / sustain / release (times on `@curve(exp0, log2 60001)`), acurve / dcurve / rcurve (−1 … 1) and amount; its expansion is stdlib msenv's arithmetic (mode None, soft start) as atoms with each segment generalised by its curve, bit-identical to msenv with every curve 0; `Type::voiceEnd` — the body calls `Builder::done(x)`, the framework uses it as the ChainNotes `done` and adds an output `done` in the Nested and ChainVoice forms; its tap carries the level and whether the note is released (`decodeEnvelopeTap`); `envAttackLaw` / `envFallLaw` / `envAttackLevel` / `envFallLevel` / … are the continuous-time shape the face draws. TMM.5's off-thread TABLE BUILDER (`pq/meta/table_builder.h`): `Type::frameTable(TableSource{data, dataHash, params, settings, sampleRate}, index) → TableJob` (an owned `dsp::FrameTableSpec` + an identity key; one keyframe builds one dense frame), and `TableBuilder` (one below-normal worker, newest wins per target, a weak cache by key so identical specs share one table, the dsp builder's arenas kept; TMM.9 **preview builds**: a slow job — a bake, or more than 128 dense frames — is first built as a preview and delivered under the job's key, uncached, and its full build waits until no newer job has come for that target for `kPreviewSettle` = 0.1 s, so a baked knob or a drawn stroke is heard per step instead of a full table every ≈ 65–300 ms; `waitIdle` runs deferred full builds at once; TMM.13-fix1 H4: `waitFor(targets, timeout)` waits only until each named target's newest job has delivered a table (a preview counts), building those targets before any other — the compile path waits so for its NEW frame-table slots only (not for the whole builder: a drag on another instance neither delays a compile nor is forced into full builds); `forget(target)` also drops the target's queued job (L18). TMM.16: a preview is FOCUSED — the last table delivered for the target cloned (`dsp::FrameTable::clone`) with only the frames that matter rebuilt (`dsp::FrameTableBuilder::buildFramesInto`, ≤ `kPreviewFocusFrames` = 8): the four around `TableJob::focus` (the position the instance is read at; not part of the key) and the two around each keyframe whose cycle changed since that table; so where voices read, a preview equals the full table. Without a compatible earlier table (first build, another frame count / rate / mode, or after `forget(target)`, which the link calls when it frees a slot id) it is TMM.9's `kPreviewFrames` = 8 dense frames. The link publishes a delivered table whose key is among the slot's recent submissions (`MetaSlot::recent`), not only the newest: deliveries arrive per slot in submission order, and dropping every table older than the newest job left a drag whose steps outpaced its builds silent until it stopped). `Builder::bind` names a body node (a value read twice, a sink: the printed expansion parses back equal). Depends on `graph` + `dsp` + `core`; `document` and `compile` depend on it; `engine` never includes it. `Builder::region` / `inRegion` (TMM.8) put body nodes into an `oversample(n, phase: …)` region. `Builder::region` / `inRegion` (TMM.8) put body nodes into an `oversample(n, phase: …)` region. The faces live in `views` (TMM.4: `pq/views/meta/`) |
| `src/graph` | `pq_graph` | `pq::graph` | Patch IR, patch-language parser/printer, inference (rate/kind/poly/width), optimiser, scheduler, `Program` description (the interpreter backend is in `engine`: it builds a ProgramImage), the project graph: per-track compile units (partition, composition, unit hash; T1.x-arch1), circuit-diagram layout (T4.3: layered auto-layout, Manhattan channel router, `pq/graph/layout.h`), inspection (T4.5, `pq/graph/inspect.h`: probe targets through infer / optimise / schedule, the ops' cost weights), structural constants of a patch as written (T4.15, `pq/graph/structure.h`: read-only, for the circuit editor), oversample regions (TMM.6, PATCH_LANGUAGE §2.3: `Patch::regions()` + `Node::region` in source patches; TMM.12: `OversampleRegion::factorFrom` — `oversample(hq)` names a `real const` input whose value, resolved per instance by infer() from the literal the call passes, is the factor; infer() flattens them into `TypedPatch::oversample` + `NodeOrigin::region` and inserts the rate converters on every audio-rate wire across a boundary; the optimiser keeps regions apart (CSE, fusion, `sr` = n·sr, durations); the scheduler emits Block / Loop regions with a `factor` and n×-long buffers) |
| `src/jit` | `pq_jit` | `pq::jit` | LLVM IR generation from a schedule into an `engine::ProgramImage` (depends on `engine`), ORC LLJIT, the compile cache (T1b.4, `pq/jit/cache.h`: code shared in memory, a disk cache, read-only AOT seed directories) and the baseline-CPU target the AOT seed is compiled for |
| `src/compile` | `pq_compile` | `pq::compile` | T1.x-arch2 (Q73 B): `SourceFile` (a .pqp with its imports), `compilePatch` (infer → optimise → schedule), `buildImage` (interpreter or JIT), the process's `jitCompiler()` (its cache: `setJitCache`, T1b.4) and `jitUnitBackend()` / `jitUnitBackend(compiler)` (`pq/compile/compile.h`). Depends on `engine` + `jit` |
| `src/engine` | `pq_engine` | `pq::engine` | Audio thread runtime: ProgramImage ABI, the shared image host both backends run (`pq/engine/image_host.h`, T1.x-arch2), interpreter backend, Program execution, voice allocation, transport, hot swap + state transfer, the per-track project runner + compile driver (T1.x-arch1), parameter blocks, taps for meters, audio device I/O (RtAudio), MIDI I/O, offline renderer; the browser's preview player (`pq/engine/preview_player.h`, T5.10: a one-slot `ClipPlayer` over a one-clip image, commands through an SPSC ring, sleeps when stopped) |
| `src/document` (headers `pq/document/`) | `pq_doc` | `pq::doc` | Immutable project model (tracks, clips, notes, device chains, tunings, automation), commands, undo/redo, serialization (JSON + `.pqp`), project → graph lowering, the device library + catalog (T4.1, `devices.h`) |
| `src/library` | `pq_library` | `pq::library` | T5.9: the browser index (`pq/library/index.h`: places, entries as columns grouped by directory + one string pool, kinds and categories; `scan.h`: rebuilds that copy unchanged directories and relist only dirty ones, probing only new or changed files (headers only, never decoding), renames matched by NTFS file ID; `index_file.h`: `index.pqi` snapshot + `index.pqj` journal in %LOCALAPPDATA%\pqdaw\library), the indexer thread (`indexer.h`: background priority, one recursive overlapped `ReadDirectoryChangesW` per local place root, 100 ms coalescing, no wakeups while idle; T5.x-index-progressive: rebuilds publish the tree first with new files unprobed, a parallel probe pass fills them in with a publish about every second, `Indexer::progress()` feeds the browser's indexing line; the indexing probe is `media::probe` with `ProbeOptions::headersOnly`), search (`search.h`: fuzzy subsequence scorer behind a 64-bit character-set prefilter, filters, keystroke narrowing, the `Searcher` worker), tags and colour collections (`tags.h`: `tags.json`, keyed by file ID, path + size fallback). The browser panel is T5.10 (`views`) |
| `src/ui` | `pq_ui` | `pq::ui` | Window (SDL3), Vulkan renderer, draw lists (ImDrawVert-compatible), text (FreeType/HarfBuzz), widget toolkit, theme tokens, offscreen screenshot path; drag sessions (`pq/ui/drag.h`, T5.7: payloads Files / BrowserItems / Clips / Device, `Widget::dragOver` / `dragLeave` / `drop` / `autoScrollDrag`, `UiTree::beginDrag` for internal drags and `dragEnter` / `dragMove` / `dragExit` / `dragDrop` for OS drags, a ghost painted over the tree) |
| `src/ui/scenes` | `pq_ui_scenes` | `pq::ui` | Named test scenes (`pq/ui/scenes.h`: `--screenshot`, goldens, ui benchmarks, `renderSceneImage`). Linked by `pq_app`, tests and benches; not part of the toolkit. The fonts, theme and textures they paint with (`pq/ui/scene_textures.h`) stay in `pq_ui` |
| `src/views` | `pq_views` | `pq::views` | The DAW's views (T3.7+): `ViewContext` (owns the `DocumentStore`, global actions, view state), `TransportPort`, the main-window shell (control bar, track-area/detail/browser slots, info view, status line), arrangement, mixer, piano roll, MOS wheel, preferences window, circuit canvas + patch viewer (T4.2), circuit editor + text pane (T4.4; one subtree each; T3.18's `AudioPrefsPort` is the plain-data audio-device interface the app implements), view scenes for screenshots. device panels + the Device tab (T4.6, `pq/views/devices/`), the project patch view (T4.13, `pq/views/project_patch/`). Depends on `ui`, `document`, `meta` (TMM.4: the faces), `graph` (the circuit view draws Patches), `dsp` (the spectrum analyser behind the device displays; graph already depends on it). T5.7: `FileProbes` (`pq/views/file_probes.h`: dragged files probed and hashed by a small worker pool while they hover, handed to the app's sample preloader), the drop rules (`pq/views/sample_drop.h`: plan + one-Batch edit), the arrangement's `FileDrop` (ghost clips). T5.10: the browser (`pq/views/browser/`: `Library` = places / index / tags / preview port in the ViewContext, `BrowserPanel` in ShellSlot::Browser, `PreviewPort` implemented by the app); depends on `library` since T5.10. T5.11: the User Library's files (`Library`: save / default presets / user devices / tunings / clips, `presetPatch`, the hot-swap request), loading library items (`pq/views/browser/library_load.h`: catalog devices with their default preset, presets, user devices, tunings, clips; hot-swap), the browser's hot-swap mode, device tree, filter / tag chips, tag editing and width handle; the Device view's Save Preset / Save as Default Preset / Save as New Device and preset slot; the tuning chips' and cards' Save to Library / Hot-Swap. T5.12: arrangement clips leave the arrangement as a `Clips` payload (`packClipRef`); the browser saves dropped clips as .pqclip (Alt: WAV through `Library::setClipRenderer`) |
| `src/app` | `pq_app` (lib) + `pqdaw` (exe: `main.cpp` only, so tests can link the CLI/commands) | `pq::app` | Main, CLI, the window loop, `ShellApp` (views in the window: SDL file dialogs, text input, title, idle/first-frame measurement), `TransportPort` implementations, the playback link (`ProjectLink`: document → engine, transport, meters; T3.17), the audio output (`AudioOutput`) and the audio device manager (`AudioDeviceManager`, T3.18: preferences, user settings file, rate changes, hot-plug), `--render`, the JIT cache configuration and the AOT seed (`pq/app/jit_cache.h`, T1b.4: `--jit-aot`); T5.7: our OLE drop target on the window (`pq/app/os_drop.h`: SDL's drop events are turned off, see Q157); T5.10: `PreviewService` (`pq/app/preview_service.h`: the views' PreviewPort over the engine's PreviewPlayer, the link's sample pool and AudioOutput's cue source), the browser's indexer / tags / places wiring (`libraryDataDir`, `userLibraryDir`, the settings' "library" section); T5.12: `ProjectLink::renderClip` (one clip through its own offline clip player: the engine's code, blocks aligned as in a project render), `pq/app/clip_export.h` (clips as 32-bit float / 24-bit WAV files), the OLE drag source (`pq/app/os_drag.h`: an IDataObject offering CF_HDROP, files produced on the first GetData; `DoDragDrop` when an internal drag leaves the window, `UiTree::setDragOutHandler`); T5.x-bugreport: F12 bug reports (`pq/app/bug_reporter.h`: the frame read back with `Swapchain::readLastFrame`, the context, the writer thread; files, panel and recent-input ring in `pq/views/bug_report/`; CLAUDE.md "Bug reports") |
| `tools/` | `pqc` (exe) + `pq_pqc` (lib: everything but `main`, for tests) | `pq::pqc` | Patch compiler CLI (T1.10, `tools/README.md`): check / dump-ir / dump-schedule / render (interpreter or JIT). The AOT seed is `pqdaw --jit-aot`, not pqc: cache entries are valid only for the binary that wrote them (T1b.4, Q116). Depends on `compile` (its pipeline) |
| `stdlib/` | – | – | Stock subcircuits and devices as `.pqp` text: `filters/` (onepole, svf, svf_fast, biquad + RBJ designers, allpass, dcblock, comb), `envelopes/` (adsr; msenv: multi-stage with loop modes, T4.12), `control/` (lfo, slew, smooth), `dynamics/` (envfollow), `util/` (crossfade, oversample: polyphase half-band stages, T4.9), `instruments/` (basic_synth), `devices/` (utility, saturator: T4.9; eq_eight: T4.10; operator: T4.12, an instrument); callers `import` the file (PATCH_LANGUAGE §3.1). Each subcircuit has a hand-written C++ reference in `tests/stdlib/` (T1b.3; devices: `device_reference.h`, cost in `bench/jit/bench_devices.cpp`; Operator: `operator_reference.h`, its SPEC §13 calibration in `bench/engine/bench_render.cpp` `operator/stock-*`) |
| `tests/` | `pq_tests_<module>` | – | doctest unit tests per module, golden renders in `tests/golden/` |
| `bench/` | `pq_bench_<suite>` + `pq_bench_support` | `pq::bench` | Benchmarks; the T1.11 runner (`pq_bench.h`: named benchmarks, regex filter, warmup/repeats/median/p99, CSV) and `tools/bench/append_results.py` append results to `bench/RESULTS.md` |

## 2. Threads

| Thread | Realtime? | Owns | Talks via |
|---|---|---|---|
| UI / main | no | document snapshots, UI state, Vulkan | posts `EngineCommand`s (SPSC), receives `EngineReport`s (SPSC, lossy) |
| Audio | **yes** | the current `Program`, voice state, param blocks | lock-free only |
| Compiler worker | no | graph lowering, inference, optimisation, JIT | publishes `ProgramHandle` (atomic pointer swap + epoch) |
| Disk | no | sample streaming (T5.2: `SamplePool`'s disk thread, above normal priority; T5.4: `ProjectLink` owns the pool, engages the thread while the transport plays and prefetches on locate), sample loading (T5.4: the link's loader thread, `SamplePool::load`), recording writer | one ring per stream slot: the consumer claims / reads / releases lock-free; the disk thread polls the slots every 2 ms while engaged or while a stream is claimed and otherwise blocks on an event only non-RT code sets |
| Analysis | no | analysers (spectrum, scope, LUFS), transient/tempo detection | reads tap rings filled by the audio thread |
| File probes (T5.7) | no | `views::FileProbes`' workers (2–8): `media::probe` + the content hash of dragged audio files | results under a mutex, one coalesced post to the UI thread (`ViewContext::uiPoster`) |
| Preview loader (T5.10) | no | `app::PreviewService`'s thread: `SamplePool::load` of the browser's selection (head + stream source, or resident data) | results under a mutex; a play waiting for its load is posted to the UI thread; the audio thread reads only the image `PreviewPlayer::play` hands over through its command ring |
| Library indexer + search (T5.9) | no | `library::Indexer` (background priority: the browser index of the places; blocks on its change handles, no wakeups at idle; T5.x-index-progressive: its probe pass runs on a pool of 8 below-normal-priority threads, normal I/O priority, that wait on a condition variable when idle), `library::Searcher` (one search worker; a newer query cancels the one in flight) | publish immutable `shared_ptr<const Index>` / `SearchResult` snapshots (a mutex around the pointer) and call an on-change callback; never touch the audio thread |

## 3. Key interfaces (sketches; the real headers are authoritative once written)

```cpp
// core: realtime-safe single-producer/single-consumer ring (power-of-two capacity; the producer- and consumer-written
// index blocks are kHotLineSeparation = 128 bytes apart, outside each other's adjacent-line prefetch pair)
template <class T, std::size_t HotLineSeparation = kHotLineSeparation> class SpscRing {
public:
  explicit SpscRing(uint32_t capacityPow2);  // allocates: construct off the audio thread only
  bool tryPush(const T&) noexcept;
  bool tryPop(T&) noexcept;
};

// core: allocation trap. In tests and debug builds, any heap allocation while an RtScope is active fails the test.
struct RtScope { RtScope() noexcept; ~RtScope() noexcept; };   // marks the current thread "realtime"

// core: RT log ring. One RtLog per realtime thread (SPSC of POD records: static "{}" format, code, ≤ 4 numeric
// args, rdtsc, sequence); RtLogger drains every 10 ms on its own thread, formats, writes lines; drops are counted
// exactly and reported as a line plus a sequence gap.
class RtLog { public: explicit RtLog(uint32_t capacityPow2); template <class... A> bool log(uint32_t code, const char* fmt, A...) noexcept; };
class RtLogger { public: void add(RtLog&, std::string name); void start(); void stop(); size_t drainOnce(); };

// tuning: everything resolves to float32 Hz in the end
namespace pq::tuning {
  struct PitchId;                                  // Degree | Ratio | Cents | Harmonic (SPEC §3.1)
  class Tuning;                                    // EDO | MOS | Scale | JI, with reference pitch (default C3 = 261.626 Hz)
  float resolveHz(const PitchId&, const Tuning&, const ResolveContext&) noexcept;
  class MosLinearTable;                            // per chain length n: (k_i, m_i) for every constant-order interval of g
  MosLinearSpan MosLinearTable::lookup(double g, double P) const noexcept;  // cents_i = k_i·g − m_i·P (+ equave·P), SPEC §3.3
  // T2.6 (pq/tuning/tuning_signal.h): an EDO / MOS tuning's per-interval coefficients rotated to its mode, plain data
  // for the tuning node's table (Scale, MODMOS and generator sequences have none: they play static pitches)
  Result<LinearTuningForm> linearTuningForm(const Tuning&);
}

// graph: the compiler pipeline
namespace pq::graph {
  class Patch;          // IR (pq/graph/patch.h): flat node/port/wire arrays, dense NodeIndex + stable NodeId,
                        // params with attrs, in/out/export declarations, subcircuit nodes, solve blocks, layout;
                        // validate() for structure + declared types; operator== is structural (ignores NodeIds/order)
  struct PatchFile;     // a .pqp file: imports + patches (pq/graph/patch_file.h)
  class AtomRegistry;   // leaf atoms + operators: name → signature (pq/graph/atoms.h)
  // pq/graph/parse.h. A file holds several patches. Options: externals (prelude signatures), an ImportLoader for
  // `import`s (graph does no file I/O), validate, previous (T1.x-text-ids: the previous parse of this text, whose
  // NodeIds the result inherits). parseDetailed() returns every Diagnostic (message, file:line:col, byte span) and,
  // with `previous`, ParseResult::idMatch; parse() formats them `file:line:col: error: …` + source line + caret.
  Result<PatchFile> parse(std::string_view source, std::string_view fileName, const ParseOptions& = {});
  ParseResult parseDetailed(std::string_view source, std::string_view fileName, const ParseOptions& = {});
  std::string print(const PatchFile&);             // parse(print(f)) == f
  // pq/graph/id_match.h (T1.x-text-ids, Q68): structural diff of a re-parsed patch against the previous one; paired
  // nodes take the previous NodeIds (names, then structure down inputs / up unique consumer edges, then Merkle-style
  // input hashes, then weak pairs whose atom/literal/ports changed), the rest get ids above every previous id. Dense
  // order is kept (it is text order: signatures and output order depend on it), so ids may become unsorted:
  // Patch::setNodeIds keeps an id → index table then (indexOf stays O(log n)). Patches pair by name.
  IdMatchStats inheritNodeIds(PatchFile& next, const PatchFile& previous);
  // T4.3, the stored layout (PATCH_LANGUAGE §2.2): layout lines name anonymous nodes by key (`y.in.a`: consumer key +
  // input; `_e<k>` roots under solve equations), so placing a node never changes how the rest prints.
  // ParseOptions::lenientLayout drops stale entries (ParseResult::layoutDropped) instead of failing; ParseResult::patchSpans
  // locate each patch's layout block. printLayout(patch) = the block print() writes; spliceLayout(source, parsed, laidOut)
  // = the source with only the layout blocks replaced (comments and formatting kept; byte-stable; line endings followed).
  std::string printLayout(const Patch&);
  Result<std::string> spliceLayout(std::string_view source, const ParseResult& parsed, const PatchFile& laidOut);
  // pq/graph/layout.h (T4.3): world units = logical px at zoom 1; inputs on a node's left edge, outputs on its right, pin
  // offsets from the caller's LayoutGeometry (views/circuit/symbols.h; defaultGeometry() for tools/tests).
  // autoLayout ("tidy"): feedback cut (per delay in a loop: its loop inputs or outputs, the fewer; then DFS) → layers
  // (longest path, then as late as possible; ins first, outs last) → shared dummy chains per output pin (forward) and per
  // reversed net → barycentric crossing minimisation (exact bilayer counts, best of N sweeps) → vertical placement by
  // isotonic regression per layer (dummy–dummy links ×4) → channel routing: one group per net per channel, groups ordered
  // by pairwise stub-crossing cost, packed into tracks keeping that order; writes NodeLayout and every wire's via points.
  // completeLayout: place unplaced nodes (auto positions moved off placed ones) + routeUnrouted (Z / detour, no
  // obstacle avoidance). carryLayout(next, previous): positions by NodeId after a text edit (T4.4).
  AutoLayoutStats autoLayout(Patch&, const LayoutGeometry&, const AutoLayoutOptions& = {});
  uint32_t completeLayout(Patch&, const LayoutGeometry&, const AutoLayoutOptions& = {});
  uint32_t routeUnrouted(Patch&, const LayoutGeometry&, float stub = 12);
  uint32_t carryLayout(PatchFile& next, const PatchFile& previous);
  // T4.4 (pq/graph/edit.h): the circuit editor's IR edits — connect (replaces the input's driver; a declared bool ↔
  // real mismatch is refused; a wire that would close a delay-free cycle runs through an inserted z1: the patch
  // language's rule is unchanged, the editor makes the delay explicit), addNode from a spec (literal with unit, atom,
  // operator, callee, `param` / `in` / `out` / `export` declaration), rename (declarations: the callers' signature copies
  // follow), constToParam (a param pin in place of a constant), makeSubcircuit (selection → new patch after the edited
  // one: ins, param pins from the parent's params, outs), refreshCallers (call nodes rebuilt by port name after a
  // signature change), anonymize (the printer's `_n<digits>` names treated as none). Parser support: ParseOptions::
  // deferMissingInputs (a missing call argument is a validation error, so unwired graphs print and parse back),
  // ParseResult::lowered (the file is whole; diagnostics are validation errors; NodeIds are inherited then too),
  // PatchTextSpans::begin (a patch's `patch` keyword), `export e` without a value; spliceLayout accepts a lowered parse.
  // T2.6 voice tuning: a `tune_voices(hz, cents)` node (no outputs, removed by infer) rebinds voice.hz (and, with
  // `cents` connected, voice.cents) in every subcircuit instantiated below its patch to those inputs; the binding
  // patch's own reads stay the engine's (the tuning node's fallback); the nearest binding ancestor wins.
  // pq/graph/infer.h (T1.3). Flattens subcircuits per call site (callees from InferOptions::library) and infers
  // kind/rate/poly/width with declared types as upper bounds. TypedPatch = { Patch patch (flat: no Subcircuit nodes,
  // inserted voice_sum reducers, bool eq/ne rewritten, TX.bool-real: a gate_to_real in front of every port where a
  // bool reaches a real input — before its voice_sum —, only real into bool is a kind error), types[] (inferred, parallel to patch.portTypes()),
  // stateKey[] (per node: hash of the NodeId call path, the hot-swap key), origin[], instances[] }. inferDetailed()
  // returns every Diagnostic, located at root-patch nodes via ParseResult::nodeSpans. T1.3-fix1 (Q29 B): free-running
  // sources (stateful, inputs global and ≤ param rate) that feed voice processing are per-voice
  // (InferOptions::freeRunningPoly = Voice, decided after the fixpoint; per-voice phasor/sine_hz get reset =
  // voice.trigger); `shared(expr)` in text sets Node::shared (an attribute, no node) and keeps its cone global.
  Result<TypedPatch> infer(const Patch&, const InferOptions&);
  // pq/graph/optimise.h (T1.4). Durations → samples (`· sr` inserted, TypedPatch::durationsInSamples), then a
  // fixpoint of constant folding (by the atom's own dsp kernel as Control regions run it: 8 lanes, lane 0; never
  // params), exact algebraic simplification, select sinking (pure ops below a select/mux when the copies drop below
  // audio rate), CSE (literals keyed by unit) and DCE; then rate refinement (pure atoms = max of their inputs' rates)
  // and a·b + c → muladd fusion at equal rates. Stateful nodes keep stateKey, atom and types; replacements never
  // change kind/width/poly (T1b.x-perf, Q132: a constant select / mux whose picked arm is a literal of another poly or width becomes a literal of the select's own type, so a structural switch's off section is dead). Errors: a folded channel index out of range; an `smp` literal through arithmetic into
  // a duration input. OptimiseOptions: sampleRate (sr folds when known), maxPasses, fuseMulAdd.
  Result<TypedPatch> optimise(TypedPatch, const OptimiseOptions& = {}, OptimiseStats* = nullptr);
  // pq/graph/schedule.h (T1.5). Tarjan SCCs on wires + implicit equation→unknown edges of solve blocks; feedback SCCs
  // become Loop regions with z1/delay split into read (in place) + write (loop end), run per sample or, when every
  // cut is a delay line with a constant lower bound, in sub-blocks of the shortest tap age (T1b.3: z1s are not cut
  // when cutting the delay lines alone breaks every cycle, so they keep the sub-blocks); a delay-free cycle is an
  // error (text) or gets a z1 + warning (ScheduleOptions::visualMode, Schedule::rewritten). Stages alternate global
  // (even, lanes 1) / voice (odd, lanes 8, run per voice group); a stage = Control region (param/const ops once per
  // block on 8-float patterns, param-rate solves) + Block/Loop regions. Global audio read by voice ops is broadcast;
  // voice reducers accumulate in the voice stage. Flat output: ops {kind, dsp kernel, lanes, Spl mask, operands
  // (Buffer | Param slot | Const | External), state entry, resource}, regions, stages, solve blocks, buffers (interval
  // reuse, in-place element-wise ops; the 100-node test chains need 1–2), params, externals, outputs, StateLayout. dump() is
  // the `pqc dump-schedule` text. Liveness counts reads after an op (solver lhs/rhs/unknown reads after a body,
  // engine voice-output reads at a stage end, a loop's next iteration) as keeping the value past that op;
  // ScheduleOptions::shareBuffers = false gives each value its own buffer (the replay checker, sched_replay.h, compares
  // the two). Loop sub-block bounds are evaluated in float32 like the runtime.
  // TX.smooth (schedule.h "Param ramps"; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.smooth-a): every param is smooth when it changes, in every
  // device, by one mechanism at the param → audio boundary. After outputs() (values still virtual), insertRamps() finds
  // each Spl input of a Kernel op in a Block / Loop region (not a solve body) whose operand is a real pattern (Param,
  // pattern Buffer, param-rate External; never a Const) where dsp::rampableInputs(kernel) allows it (real inputs
  // that can be audio rate, minus choices and counts: mux / demux index, counter / clockdiv n, pulse period, timer
  // duration, tuning_hz entirely (pitch identities, and tuning automation is already per sample); minus state-init and required-Spl inputs) and the kernel
  // exists for Blk; per (stage, factor, source) one `param_ramp` op (dsp::Atom::ParamRamp, 8 lanes in voice stages)
  // goes into a Block region right after the stage's Control region, with a state entry (key: a structural hash of
  // the source expression, unit-independent) and a Block buffer whose liveness runs to its last reader (uses_ entries:
  // ordinary buffer reuse). Schedule::ramps / rampOf (parallel to operands) say which op inputs read which ramp; the ops
  // keep their Spl operands and masks. ScheduleOptions::paramRamps = false: none (references, A/B).
  //   The ramp (kernels_ramp.cpp): linear over dsp::kParamRampSeconds = 20 ms from where it is to a changed input
  // (bitwise), restarted from where it is by a later change; a first call (or a voice lane after its reset) starts at
  // its input; non-finite values jump. It writes its block only in a call where some lane moves (ParamRampState::
  // active); dsp::paramRampHold fills it with the resting value when a reader needs the block anyway.
  //   Run time, both backends, the same bits: an op with a moving ramp among its inputs runs with every ramped input
  // as a block (findKernel without those Spl bits; resting ramps held), else as scheduled — the interpreter per record
  // (InterpretedImage::prepareRamps: pool cells swapped, relocated in loops), the JIT per call (pointer / kernel /
  // mask selects in emitCallHalf) and, for inline code, per stage: the units (regions, chains) that read a ramp exist
  // twice, as scheduled (hoisted patterns, every specialisation) and in ramp mode (ramped operands read per frame
  // from their blocks; fused runs not interleaved): per span of such units one cold branch per block calls the span's
  // ramp-mode copy, a noinline function `<stage>_ramp<k>`; `<stage>_ramp0`, called after the Control region, runs
  // the param_ramp regions and holds every resting ramp inline code reads. PF.3: units that read no ramp are emitted
  // once and run in both modes (codegen.cpp emitUnits; the ramp-mode copy of the whole stage was ~45 % of a unit's
  // IR); the copies stay separate functions because one function holding both took longer in machine code. A pair function stages its voice state after that branch when the regions before it
  // touch none (so nothing staged is live there); a stage with ramps pairs only up to chain cost 256 (the Operator's
  // pair frame grew past 4 KiB at 320). Smoothed gains: their `g` is ramped like any input; in ramp mode an inline
  // gain runs the kernel's audio-rate path (gainAudioPrologue; per frame g + residual), whose target is stored before
  // the frames (a later op of the run may take the ramp's buffer). A held ramp gives the param path's bits, so the
  // JIT's per-stage choice and the interpreter's per-record choice agree.
  //   Host: validateRamps; dormant only once every global ramp has settled; idleAfterBlocks covers the longest global
  // ramp (project_compile.h globalRampFrames). The sleep plan ignores param_ramp state for dormantCapable; ramp
  // regions never sleep (one compare per ramp and block).
  //   TX.smooth-fix1 (Q?-TX.smooth-fix1-a; supersedes the per-block rest checks, the rest summary and most of the
  // pair bail above): zero at rest BY CONSTRUCTION. (1) Memory: param_ramp states live in a ramp side table
  // (StateEntry::side; StateLayout::sideGlobalBytes / sideVoiceBytes; StateArena::sideGlobal / sideVoice /
  // sideVoiceStride; ImageHost's own allocation; state transfer, VoiceEngine lane reset and lane move cover it) and
  // the ramp outputs' buffers come after every other buffer (allocateBuffers' second pass), so the arenas, the voice
  // stride and every buffer are the ramp-free schedule's (tests/graph test_schedule "leave the ramp-free schedule's
  // buffers and state as they are"). (2) Code: one entry per stage with a `ramp` argument (StageFn); ramp off it
  // runs the ramp-free code (no checks, no ramp state touched) plus one never-taken branch per span of ramp-reading
  // units (PF.3; before, a second entry `<stage>_rampmode` repeated the whole stage); pair functions are ramp-free
  // only (ramp 0).
  // (3) Choice, outside the stage code: Schedule::rampStages (per stage: has ramps, `checked`, the params and global
  // param-rate externals its static ramps follow, the ramp length in frames). ImageHost::decideRamps (begin()) sets
  // a stage's frames-left counter to the ramp length when one of those params / externals changed bitwise (the param
  // pass already computes paramChanged_; the externals' lane 0 is compared) and in the first block; run() passes
  // `ramp` = counter > 0, or for a voice group a lane reset this block (VoiceGroupIO::startMask, set by
  // VoiceEngine::resetLaneState) to the backends, which run the ramp mode (JIT: the stage function's `ramp`
  // argument; interpreter: InterpretedImage::rampMode_, which otherwise skips the param_ramp regions and
  // prepareRamps). A group in ramp mode never pairs. Static sources (Ramp::staticSource: params, constants, note-on-static voice signals, global param-rate
  // engine sources and root inputs such as transport.bpm, through stateless ops) cannot move otherwise, so held
  // automation, a released knob or a held note costs nothing. Only ramps of other sources (a stateful Control op, a
  // per-voice signal that moves) keep a per-block check after the Control region (`checked`; the stock Operator has
  // none). SleepStats::rampRuns counts ramp-mode stage runs.
  // TX.uniform-branch (schedule.h "Uniform branches"; Q?-TX.uniform-branch-a): a select in a Block region whose
  // condition is a pattern defined before the region (param, constant, param-rate external, a Control value; never a
  // ramped input) has one condition for the whole block. uniformBranches() (after insertRamps, values still virtual)
  // walks the ops backwards and gives each op the innermost guard that covers all its readers (a branch select read
  // through input 1 / 2 counts as that side): Schedule::branches (Branch {op, region, parent guard}, outer first) and
  // Schedule::guardOf (per op: kNone or 2·b + side). Only stateless kernels without a resource in the select's region
  // are guarded, and only when every reader is in that region; outputs, probes, ramp sources, solves and reducers pin a
  // value, so stateful ops (delays, filters, phasors, smoothed gains) and what they read always run. Nothing else of
  // the schedule changes (ScheduleOptions::uniformBranches = false: the same ops, operands and buffers, no tables;
  // CompileOptions::uniformBranches, `pqc --no-branches` for A/B). Run time (both optional: computing everything is the
  // same output): per Block region, per block and voice group, each branch's state from the sign bits of its
  // condition's lanes (8; a JIT pair's 16; the scalar baseline's one) — ALL, NONE or MIXED; side 0 runs unless NONE,
  // side 1 unless ALL, inside the parent guard; the select runs as select(c, x, x) of the chosen side (bit for bit
  // its value, the skipped side never read) or as itself when MIXED. Interpreter: InterpretedImage::runBranched (a
  // guard byte per record, the select's other input cell swapped for the call). JIT: analyseBranches takes a branch
  // when one of its sides guards ≥ kMinBranchCost (opCost units); runs split at guard changes and around a taken
  // select (values crossing are stored, as between runs); emitBranchStates at the region's start, each guarded part
  // behind one branch, the select in a three-way branch (emitBranchSelect); chained regions (emitChain) ignore guards.
  // tests/graph sched_replay.h replays every branch state (a skipped value read would fail "before anything wrote it in
  // this block"); sched_exec.h's executor poisons skipped buffers with NaN.
  ScheduleResult scheduleDetailed(const TypedPatch&, const ScheduleOptions& = {});  // + warnings in the Schedule
  Result<Schedule> schedule(const TypedPatch&, const ScheduleOptions& = {});
}

// jit (pq/jit/compiler.h, T1b.1): the LLVM backend: same ProgramImage ABI and results as the interpreter, bit for bit
// with the default options (T1b.1-fix1: no fast-math flags; CompileOptions::contract opts into fmul+fadd contraction,
// which a chaotic feedback loop turns into O(1) differences). One generated function per stage; element-wise runs
// fused into loops (8 frames per <8 x float> for global ops, one frame of 8 voices for voice ops; latency-bound runs
// interleave up to 8 independent chunks per iteration, capped by code size, and runs split past a size cap);
// per-sample loops, Control regions and solve bodies inline, including the simple stateful atoms; delay lines,
// wavetable, noise, shaper, sample_* and global stateful atoms in block regions call the dsp kernels; exact
// transcendentals call the kernels' own SVML functions (1 lane = lane 0 of the 8-lane call, Q94). Host CPU target
// (AVX-512 where present), a light IR pipeline by default, machine code at CompilerConfig::codeGenLevel
// (src/jit/src/codegen.h, compiler.cpp). CompileStats reports the object size and whether any stage needs stack
// probes (it must not: a frame > 4 KiB touches guard pages on the audio thread).
// Voice stages (T1b.2, CompileOptions::voiceCode, Q111): a voice value is one vector across voice lanes. The
// simple stateful atoms are inline in voice block regions too, and consecutive voice regions that run frame by
// frame (inline Block regions + per-sample loops) are fused into one loop over frames ("chains"); T1b.x-perf (Q122):
// global stages too, from the first to the last of at least two per-sample loops, with cheap Block regions between
// them (scalar per frame), one sleep segment, and at most 20 invariants + state words (more spill the recursions):
// independent recursions (EQ Eight L/R, M/S) overlap and cascades pipeline across frames. Every voice stage
// without sends or per-frame calls also gets a PAIR function (StageFn with group1/vstate1) that runs two occupied
// groups as <16 x float>: one zmm per value on AVX-512 hosts (VoiceCode::Pairs512, the Auto choice there), two ymm
// halves otherwise (Pairs256); a lone group runs the 8-lane function. Its stage-local voice values have a second copy
// for the second group (HostLayout::altOff), kernel calls and reducers run once per half (second-group reducer
// records: ImageHost::reduceInfoB), exact transcendentals are one 8-wide SVML call per half: every mode is
// bit-identical to the interpreter (tests/jit voice-code cases). VoiceCode::ScalarLanes is the benchmark baseline
// (one voice lane per call, scalar code; refuses voice stages with calls). CompileStats::pairedStages.
// Smoothed gains (T4.x-fix-dsp): a global gain / gain_add with a param target is generated inline, so the device
// mixes it scales (EQ Eight's sections between their SVF loops, the Saturator's oversampled samples, Utility's
// matrix) stay in their chain / fused run. Before a run's (chain's, per-sample loop's) frames: per gain, if it is not
// steady (started, same target and time constant), a call to dsp::gainPrologue (the kernel's prepare / retarget);
// then one branch per block: every residual 0 → the loop with each gain as fma(x, target, acc) (a target of 0: acc /
// 0), else the loop with the kernel's per-frame definition (kernels_gain.cpp). The 1-lane kernel computes the same
// frame by frame, so interpreter == JIT bit for bit however the frames are split. Voice gains and audio-rate targets
// stay kernel calls. TX.smooth: a gain's target is ramped like any param-rate input (see "Param ramps" above); in a
// stage's ramp-mode function its inline form is the kernel's audio-rate path; settled it is unchanged.
// T1b.x-perf (Q126): a global Control region of param / constant math only (the interpreter's memo rule) runs
// behind a flag JitImage::process sets when the params equal the previous block's (ImageHost::paramsChanged, the
// host's param pass, which the interpreter reads too; HostLayout::memoOff), so a
// settled mixer costs no dB / pan math on the JIT either.
// T5.x-perf-audio: the host's param pass itself is skipped when the caller vouches the params are the previous
// block's (ProcessContext::paramsUnchanged: ProjectProgram, no project param written since the packet's params were
// refreshed, the idle-unit generation) and that block changed none (ImageHost::begin; paramsChanged() stays false).
// TX.vca-knob2: ProgramHost (offline renders, previews) vouches too, from a compare with the packet's previous block.
// In a block whose params changed, a memo region whose ops read different params runs only the runs of ops whose param
// mask (graph::Schedule::memoMask: bit p mod 64 per param p the op depends on through the region) meets the changed
// params (ImageHost::paramsChangedMask, HostLayout::memoOff + 2); the scheduler gives every value read across a mask
// boundary a buffer of its own. A VCA's knob automated every block no longer re-places its markers.
namespace pq::jit {
  class Compiler {                                 // one per process; owns LLJIT; images may outlive it; PF.4: compile()
                                                   // from any number of threads at once (see "JIT threads" below)
  public:
    static Result<std::unique_ptr<Compiler>> create(const CompilerConfig& = {});
    Result<std::unique_ptr<engine::ProgramImage>> compile(const graph::Schedule&, const CompileOptions& = {},
                                                          CompileStats* = nullptr);  // not RT-safe
    void setCache(const CacheConfig&); CacheCounters counters() const;          // T1b.4
  };
}

// jit compile cache (pq/jit/cache.h, T1b.4; QUESTIONS Q116/b): compile() looks the machine code up before running
// LLVM — (1) a live image with the same key in this Compiler shares its code (one JITDylib per code object, released
// with its last image), (2) CacheConfig::dir (read-write; pqdaw: %LOCALAPPDATA%\pqdaw\jit-cache), (3) each
// CacheConfig::readOnly dir (pqdaw: <exe dir>/jit-aot, the AOT seed) under the host key, then under the
// Target::Baseline key — and compiles only on a miss, storing the object in `dir`. A hit links the stored COFF object
// (no IR, no passes, no instruction selection) and rebuilds the image's memory and pointer table from the current
// schedule (buildHost), so it is bit-identical to a fresh compile. Key = xxh3-128 of what codegen reads (schedule
// minus identities: state keys, noise seeds, node indices, diagnostics; T5.x-perf-audio: constants no operand reads, e.g.
// a clipPlayer's slot, so audio tracks share one code; PF.1: an op's resource slot only as "has a resource" — codegen
// reaches a resource through its pointer-table slot, L.opInfo, which is keyed — so tracks of one instrument share one
// code; the HostLayout incl. T1b.2's pair layout; compile options incl. the resolved VoiceCode) + target
// CPU/features + machine-code level + LLVM version + entry format + a hash of the executable's file (generated code
// reaches kernels through the pointer table, stored as offsets into this binary; codegen itself is in it; PF.1: hashed
// once per process on a background thread started by Compiler::create / compile::setJitCache, jit::startBinaryIdentity).
// PF.1: the machine-code pipeline runs without Loop Strength Reduction (-disable-lsr, set once in compiler.cpp); the
// light IR pipeline (optLevel 1) runs LSR itself on functions of <= 2000 IR instructions (small voice loops gain from
// it, the long global stages paid ~15 % of their compile for nothing). Code is
// shared/stored only when every table slot codegen added points into the executable (else `uncacheable`). Entries:
// 64-byte header (magic, format, key, payload size + hash) + stage names, table description, stats, object; written to
// a temporary file and renamed (MoveFileExW); readers share delete; invalid entries are deleted and recompiled;
// `dir` is bounded (maxBytes, least recently used out to 3/4). CacheConfig::verify (tests): every hit regenerates the
// IR and compares its hash. Static asserts on the Schedule structs' sizes make a new field fail to compile in
// src/jit/src/cache.cpp until the key covers it. PF.4: an entry holds one object per part (format 4).
//
// JIT threads (PF.4, survey #5/#7): compiles run concurrently on one Compiler. Shared: the LLJIT (ORC's session and
// linking layer are thread-safe; each code object has its own JITDylib) and the session state (live codes, counters,
// cache config: short locks). Per compile: an LLVMContext and module, and a TargetMachine leased from a pool (the IR
// pipeline's target info and instruction selection: orc::SimpleCompiler → object → addObjectFile, as a disk hit). A
// compile of a key another compile is running waits for it and shares its code (in-flight set), so 64 tracks of one
// instrument still compile once. Code is released only while no compile runs (a shared/exclusive gate: compiles hold it
// shared; a release that finds a compile running parks the code for the last one out, never waits: M1 review L6).
// A module of >= 3000 generated IR instructions with two functions or more is split by function into 2 parts
// (largest function first to the lighter part; local functions made external), each handed to a fresh context as
// bitcode, optimised and compiled to its own object, both linked into one JITDylib; the parts run on a helper thread
// while no other compile runs (an edit), one after the other otherwise. The split depends on the module only, so the
// code is the same bytes whatever the thread count (tested on the disk entries). The functions' machine code is
// unchanged by the split (bit-identical output). Rejected: FastISel for quick first images (survey #8): bit-identical,
// −16 % on the largest unit's quick image, but Operator, EQ Eight and Saturator then need stack probes (frames > 4 KiB).
namespace pq::jit {
  struct CacheConfig { path dir; vector<path> readOnly; uint64_t maxBytes = 512 MiB; bool verify = false; };
  struct CacheCounters { compiles, memoryHits, diskHits, aotHits, stores, storeErrors, rejected, uncacheable, evicted…,
                         verified, verifyMismatches, codesReleased, releasesDeferred; };   // "no JIT compile" = compiles unchanged (the test hook)
  // T1b.x-perf (M1 review L6): an image's code is released with its last image without waiting for a compile in
  // progress: freed at once when the compiler's lock is free, else parked and freed by the lock's holder as it lets go.
  enum class Target { Host, Baseline /* x86-64-v3 */ };    // CompilerConfig::target
}

// engine image host (pq/engine/image_host.h, T1.x-arch2, M1 review M15): everything an image does around its op code,
// once for both backends: schedule validation, the memory layout (buffers + backend scratch, aux patterns, state
// arena with StateInit), ImageInterface / StateLayout / StateArena, params (clamped, splatted), engine sources
// (pointer table [ext · groups + group]), the special ops (broadcast, reducers, sends as KernelFns with a record
// per op), resources (+ built-in defaults), and the stage loop with reducer init, RunCtx and output copies:
//   host.build(schedule, {who, backend, maxBlockFrames, maxVoiceGroups, resources, extraBufFloats});
//   process: if (host.begin(ctx)) host.run(ctx, global(k), voice(k, group));   // the backend's two callables
//   (T1b.2) host.run(ctx, global, voice, pair(k, a, b)) runs two occupied groups per call in the stages marked by
//   host.enablePairs(stages, alt): the second group's reducers read their own records (reduceInfoB, RunCtx runB),
//   each reducer still combines a before b (bit-identical); stages with sends are never paired
// The interpreter's records and the JIT's stage functions are what remains backend-specific; ImageInterface::backend
// says which one built an image.
namespace pq::compile {   // pq_compile (T1.x-arch2, Q73 B): the one file → image pipeline (pqc, pqdaw --play)
  Result<std::unique_ptr<SourceFile>> SourceFile::load(path);           // + imports, kept alive
  Result<Compiled> compilePatch(const SourceFile&, size_t patch, const CompileOptions&);  // every stage kept
  Result<std::unique_ptr<engine::ProgramImage>> buildImage(const graph::Schedule&, Backend, const ImageOptions&);
  Result<jit::Compiler*> jitCompiler();                                 // the process's one LLJIT (thread-safe)
  void setJitCache(const jit::CacheConfig&);                            // T1b.4: its cache (pqdaw: app::configureJitCache)
  engine::UnitBackend jitUnitBackend() noexcept;                       // ProjectCompiler → the JIT
  engine::UnitBackend jitUnitBackend(jit::Compiler*) noexcept;         // … through another compiler (tests, AOT seed)
}

// engine Newton (pq/engine/newton.h, T1b.1-fix1): what both backends do for a solve block, in one place so they
// agree bit for bit: forward-mode tangents through the body (one evaluation per iteration; tangentOp is one template
// instantiated on scalar floats by the interpreter and on IR by the JIT; planTangents says which tangents are
// structurally zero), finite differences with a step relative to the unknown (2^-8 · max(|u|, 1e-6), one-sided where
// a side leaves the domain) for bodies without tangent rules (table reads) and lanes whose tangent Jacobian is not
// finite, the exact cos/log both backends call per lane (cosExact, logExact), and the n > 1 update both call
(linearUpdate: Gaussian elimination per lane in double, T1.x-arch2).

// engine: what the audio thread runs (pq/engine/program_image.h, interpreter.h, image_runner.h; T1.7)
namespace pq::engine {
  class ProgramImage {                             // interpreted or JIT-compiled, same ABI
  public:
    virtual void process(ProcessContext&) noexcept = 0;   // one block; no allocation, no locks
    virtual StateLayout const& stateLayout() const noexcept = 0;  // for state transfer by NodeId
    virtual const ImageInterface& io() const noexcept = 0;  // slots: root ins, outs/exports (global, voice), params
    virtual StateArena stateArena() noexcept = 0;            // global arena + one block per voice group
    virtual uint64_t rejectedBlocks() const noexcept = 0;    // blocks above io().maxBlockFrames (outputs zeroed)
  };
  // The interpreter backend lives in engine (graph may not include engine): flat records {KernelFn, KernelArgs}
  // with every pointer resolved here (allocation happens here, never on the audio thread).
  Result<std::unique_ptr<ProgramImage>> buildInterpreted(const graph::Schedule&, const InterpretOptions& = {});
  class ImageRunner;  // AudioCallback (BlockAdapter's engine side) → ProgramImage::process, for one global patch
  class VoiceEngine;  // note events → voice lanes, voice.* buffers, mixing, stealing, voice end (T1.8, voices.h)
  class ProgramHost { // T1.9 (program_host.h): owns the running image; hot swap with state transfer + crossfade
  public:
    Result<PublishInfo> publish(std::unique_ptr<ProgramImage>, const SwapPolicy& = {}, const VoiceSlots& = {});  // non-RT
    size_t collect();                          // non-RT: frees retired images (EpochReclaimer)
    void process(const AudioBlock&) noexcept;  // audio thread (an AudioCallback); swaps at image-block boundaries
  };
  class ProjectCompiler; // T1.x-arch1 (project_compile.h): ProjectGraph → per-track units → UnitImages; update() per edit
  class ProjectProgram { // T1.x-arch1 (project_program.h): N units in dependency order through bus buffers
  public:
    Result<PublishInfo> publish(uint32_t unit, UnitImage, const SwapPolicy& = {});  // non-RT: swaps only that unit
    void process(const AudioBlock&) noexcept;  // audio thread
  };
  class Engine {   // T3.17 (pq/engine/engine.h): Timeline + ProjectProgram + param/audition rings + idle gate
  public:
    ProjectConfig projectConfig();                       // hook = the timeline, event sources = slots (+ auditions)
    void setProgram(std::unique_ptr<ProjectProgram>);    // then publish / publishTopology on program()
    bool post(const TransportCommand&); bool setParam(uint32_t slot, float); bool audition(slot, hz, vel, frames); bool releaseAuditions();
    void wake(); void setWakeHook(fn, user);             // UI / compile threads
    void process(const AudioBlock&) noexcept;            // audio thread (device or offline loop)
    Result<RenderStats> renderOffline(frames, AudioSink&, blockFrames);  // same path, deterministic
  };
  // T1.10 (pq/engine/offline_render.h): runs any image through a ProgramHost (T1.x-arch2; before, an ImageRunner)
  // (+ VoiceEngine + an EventSource such as NoteListSource, pq/engine/note_list.h) at a fixed block, chunk by chunk
  // into an AudioSink (BufferSink, or a WavWriter-backed sink), FTZ/DAZ + an RtScope around each chunk's block loop.
  // The second form renders a caller's ProgramHost with a non-RT callback between chunks (publish there: swaps
  // offline are the live protocol). One loop (src/engine/src/render_loop.h) for both and for Engine::renderOffline.
  Result<RenderStats> renderOffline(const RenderRequest&, AudioSink&);
  Result<RenderStats> renderOffline(ProgramHost&, const HostRenderRequest&, AudioSink&);
}
```

### Audio device I/O (engine, T0.6)
```cpp
namespace pq::engine {
  struct AudioBlock { const float* const* in; uint32_t numIn; float* const* out; uint32_t numOut; uint32_t frames; int64_t frameTime; };
  using AudioCallbackFn = void (*)(void* user, const AudioBlock&) noexcept;    // once per block, planar float
  class AudioDevice {                       // NullDevice (tests/headless), WasapiDevice (own WASAPI: exclusive + shared,
                                            // output only), AsioDevice (own ASIO host, duplex), RtAudioDevice
                                            // (RtAudio WASAPI shared, for comparison and input)
    virtual Result<std::vector<DeviceInfo>> enumerate();                       // non-RT virtuals
    virtual Result<StreamInfo> open(const StreamConfig&, AudioCallback);
    virtual Status start(); virtual Status stop(); virtual const CallbackStats& stats() const noexcept;
  };
  class CallbackRunner;   // the one per-callback path every backend calls: RtScope + DenormalGuard + rdtsc stats
  class BlockAdapter;     // device buffer of any size → engine at a fixed internal block (direct when divisible, else FIFO +block latency)
  class UnderrunTracker;  // per device wake: lateness vs the wall-clock schedule, empty shared buffer, device clock past
                          // frames written → CallbackStats::outputUnderflows (one per episode) + recordWake (lateness, queue);
                          // the ASIO host feeds it each buffer switch against the driver's sample position for
                          // lateness only (M0 review L2; ASIO underruns stay position jumps)
  void planarToInterleaved(...);  // planar float → interleaved float32/int32/24-in-32/int24/int16 (AVX2 mono/stereo)
  ToDeviceFn asioToDeviceFn(type); FromDeviceFn asioFromDeviceFn(type);  // per-channel ASIO conversions (AVX2)
}
```
`DeviceChangeNotifier` (wasapi_device.h, T3.18) is an `IMMNotificationClient`: endpoint added / removed / state / default changes call a function on a system thread (event driven, no polling); the DAW uses it as its generic hot-plug signal (USB interfaces with ASIO drivers have WASAPI endpoints too).
`Backend::Wasapi` is our WasapiDevice (the default); `Backend::RtAudioWasapi` keeps RtAudio's WASAPI for comparison and input. WasapiDevice's audio thread joins MMCSS "Pro Audio" once, waits on the device event, and per event runs the callback once per period on planar scratch, then converts once into the device buffer (no ring).
`Backend::Asio` is AsioDevice (T0.6-fix2): a clean-room host of the ASIO driver ABI (`asio_abi.h`; no Steinberg SDK). A control thread per device (COM STA, hidden window, message pump; it polls the request flags every 50 ms while the stream runs, 250 ms while a driver is loaded but stopped) makes every non-audio driver call and handles driver requests (reset / buffer-size / rate change → stop, dispose, release, reload, recreate, restart; latencies re-queried) that the RT-safe message callback only flags. The driver's thread calls bufferSwitch(TimeInfo) → convert inputs → CallbackRunner → convert outputs into the driver's half (float32 channels zero-copy) → outputReady(). One driver per process (the ABI's callbacks carry no context). AU.stream: before a reset's stop it calls the owner's `setBeforeDriverStopCallback` hook (AudioOutput fades out there); the driver's halves are zeroed at every start.

**Stream trace** (AU.stream, `pq/engine/stream_trace.h`): a process-wide flight recorder of the audio stream: 8192 fixed slots (384 KiB), any-thread RT-safe `push` (one fetch_add + a seqlock'd slot, ≈ 20 ns), overwriting the oldest; `read(cursor)` off the audio thread. AsioDevice records open / start / started / stop / stopped / close, every bufferSwitch (half index, the driver's sample position, filled by us or not, output 1's peak / first / last sample), every asioMessage selector and value with our answer, sampleRateDidChange, a reset's reopen and its result, latency re-queries; WasapiDevice the same per period (no positions) and its underrun restarts; AudioOutput its suspend / resume, pre-roll begin / end, fades. `describeStreamTrace` lists the notable ones (first buffers after a start, a repeated half from a driver that alternates, a position step ≠ the buffer, an interval < ¼ or > 1.75 periods, not filled, a jump in output 1). AudioOutput's control thread prints new notable events to stdout (`pqdaw: audio trace: …`); the F12 report's Context has the last 30 s.
The engine's process entry will be an `AudioCallback` behind a `BlockAdapter`; `ProcessContext` frame counts are therefore always the fixed internal block on the realtime path.

### ProgramImage ABI (shared by interpreter and JIT)
- `ProcessContext` carries: frame count (≤ max block), sample rate, transport snapshot, pointers to input/output channel buffers, the parameter block (`float*`, indexed by `ParamSlot`), the event list for this block (sorted, preallocated), and the voice table.
- Concretely (pq/engine/program_image.h, T1.7): global signals planar (`frames` floats per slot: root `in`s, `out`s/`export`s, track.in, sidechain.in, send bus channels); voice signals per 8-lane group in the kernel layout (`frames · 8`, voices interleaved): `VoiceGroupIO { laneMask, signals[VoiceSignal] (voice.*), in[], out[], gain }` (T1.8-fix1: `gain` = the VoiceEngine's per-lane per-sample gain, frames · 8, or null; audio-rate `voice_sum` and voice sends add `select(gain ≠ 0, x, 0) · gain`, `voice_last` reads its lane only where gain ≠ 0, other reducers keep laneMask: Q61 option A), `VoiceTable { groups, numGroups, lastGroup/lastLane }`. Bools are lane masks everywhere. Param-rate inputs are read at frame 0. The image clamps params to their declared ranges every block (Q44). Slot meanings come from `io()` (`ImageInterface`: global/voice in and out slots with node, channel, rate; `ParamSlot`s; sample rate; maxBlockFrames; maxVoiceGroups). process() runs global stages once and voice stages once per group (`numGroups`) **whose `laneMask` is non-zero** (T1.8: a group without voices is skipped: its state holds, its voice outputs are not written; the JIT must do the same, so voice cost is per occupied group); reducers combine the groups' active lanes (per sample through `gain` when given); callers run it under `DenormalGuard` (CallbackRunner does). voice.* buffers as the VoiceEngine fills them: `Hz`, `Cents` (float) and `Gate`, `Trigger` (masks) are audio rate (`frames · 8`); `Velocity`, `Release`, `Ratio`, `Harmonic`, `UnisonCount`, `Index`, `Degree`, `Equave` (T2.6), `Tuning`, `NoteHz` and `NoteDegree` (T4.17: the note's Hz and its absolute degree in its own tuning at its note-on, held: an Instrument Rack's key zones read them) are param rate, 8 floats only (frame 0). `ProcessContext::events` (`pq/engine/note_events.h`) is never read by an image.
- **Clip players** (T5.4): `ProcessContext::clipIn` (`clipIn[slot · kClipChannels + c]`, `numClipSlots`) carries the audio the timeline's clip player rendered this block for each clip slot; a `clipPlayer(clip: k)` atom is an engine external (`graph::External::clip` = k) that both backends read through the host's pointer table (a slot without audio points at zeros), so interpreter and JIT agree bit for bit and units differing only in their slot share JIT code. `ImageInterface::clipSlots` lists the slots an image reads (the runner's idle-unit test).
- **Oversample regions** (TMM.6, PATCH_LANGUAGE §2.3; `graph::Region::factor`, `Buffer::factor`, `StateTag::factor`): a Block / Loop region with factor n runs every op on `frames · n` samples (a Loop's sub-blocks count those, Blk operands are offset by t · lanes as usual) and its buffers hold n samples per frame (the host sizes them maxBlock · lanes · n; the JIT's pair copies too); the rate converters (`oversample_*`, pq/dsp/oversample.h) are ordinary kernel ops of the region whose base-rate side reads or writes a factor-1 buffer, and read n from their constant second input. The host validates every op's operand factors (ImageHost::validateFactors). The interpreter multiplies a region's frames by its factor; the JIT emits the region's loops over `frames · n` (`regionFrames`) and never chains regions of different factors. Stateful ops inside get StateInit::sampleRate = n·sr and their StateTag carries n (a changed factor starts their state fresh on a hot swap); such regions never sleep, values inside are not probed, and reducers / sends / engine sources stay outside (infer() puts converters around them). TX.group-oversample: an inferred probe (InferOptions::probes) on a node outside a region whose value is computed inside reads it after the converter that brings it there (`locateProbes`: a group's Out terminal over a member inside an oversampled group gives the decimated value); `oversample_up` / `oversample_down` have response rules (`dsp::os::halfBandResponse`, the cascades at the base ω).
- All Program state lives in **one arena** allocated when the image is built (plus one block per voice group for voice state). The state layout (`graph::StateLayout`, T1.5) maps `(TypedPatch::stateKey, channel, arena) → (offset, size, type tag, StateInit)`; the tag is (kernel, lanes, ring frames for delay lines). Hot swap copies state for every key whose tag and size match (SPEC §7.5). Given the previous layout, `schedule()` keeps every unchanged entry at its old offset (new entries fill holes, then append; compacted when holes exceed 2× the live size + 4 KiB), so the transfer is a few coalesced memcpys.
- Parameter values live in a double-buffered block written by the engine from `EngineCommand`s. Programs read them and never write them.
- **Inspection: live probes and cost meters** (T4.5, SPEC §7.3, §7.6, §11.1; `pq/graph/inspect.h`, `pq/engine/inspect.h`, `pq/core/probe_level.h`; QUESTIONS Q134). The general tap mechanism for any reader of a running program's values (the circuit editor's probes and LED trace now; T4.7's modulated-knob ring and T4.13's project patch view next). *Which value*: a `graph::ProbeTarget { path (NodeIds of the subcircuit calls from the root), node (NodeId), output }` names a node output as written; `InferOptions::probes` → `InferResult::probeSources` (the flattened output carrying it, through subcircuit boundaries, `in` / `param` pass-throughs, inserted reducers), `OptimiseOptions::valueMap` (where each flattened value ended up: CSE / folding forward it, a value fused into another op — a mul inside a muladd — or a dead one has none), `graph::probePoints(optimised, sources, valueMap, ids)` → `ScheduleOptions::probes` → `Schedule::probes` (`ProbeRef { id, value operand, poly, kind, stage (voice: the stage position), region (of the op that writes it), voiceSum }`).
