A probe only extends its value's liveness (a global value to the end of the block, a voice value to the end of its stage) and marks it stored in generated code; ops, regions, the sleep plan (value-based) and the state layout are those of the schedule without probes, so the audio is bit-identical with and without them (tests: every node output of the corpus probed, interpreter and JIT; interpreter == JIT probe streams). *Copies*: the shared image host copies each BOUND probe after the voice group of its stage (voice) or after the last stage (global) into its `TapRing` (the full signal; one relaxed load when nobody reads it) and/or `ProbeLevel` (last / min / max of the block; bools decoded to 0 / 1), between stages, never inside generated code; a voice value is the newest voice's lane (`VoiceTable` voice_last; 0 while none sounds) or the sum over the sounding lanes (`voiceSum`; bools: any); a global value whose region is asleep (T1.x-silence) reads 0. `ProgramImage::bindInspect(InspectBinding { ProbeTap { id, ring, level }[], CostMeter* })` (non-RT, before publish; images without a host return false). Cost: no probe compiled = the plain program plus an empty-list test per block; compiled but unread = one load per probe per block (bench/RESULTS.md T4.5). *Cost meters*: `engine::CostMeter` (owned by whoever publishes, outlives the images bound to it): while enabled and the image is the metered one (`wants(key)`; T4.x-fix-engine, M4 review L5: an image becomes the metered one at its first block — `CostMeter::activate`, keys only move forward — not when it is bound, so a bound image waiting for its publish does not take the meter from the one playing), `begin()` raises a flag word in aux (`Layout::meterFlagOff`), both backends read the TSC around every region they run — the interpreter around its region loops, the generated code around each region or fused voice chain behind the flag loaded once per stage function (`codegen.h`) — adding cycles and a timing count per region into buf (`Layout::meterOff`), and the host adds the block (and its total) to the meter after the last stage; disabled: one load per block and one predictable branch per region per block. `CostMeter::read` gives ns per frame per region and in total since the previous read of the same image (a new image takes a baseline first), less the measured cost of the TSC reads (`timerCost()`: per timing, from the region and from the total), with `units` (per region, the first region of its timing unit: a JIT chain is one) and the publisher's `tag` (`setTag`, e.g. the schedule generation). `graph::opCostWeight` splits a unit's time among its ops (an estimate where a region mixes subcircuits; exact per region). Measured: the meter's total within a few % of a benchmark of the same image (JIT; bench/RESULTS.md).
- **Silence and sleep** (T1.x-silence, SPEC §7.7; `graph::SleepPlan` in pq/graph/schedule.h, the host's "sleep" in pq/engine/image_host.h). *Declarations*: every atom says what silent inputs (exactly ±0) do to it (`dsp::silenceInfo`: Any / All / Clip / MulAdd, Memory with `dsp::silenceTail` = 1 for z1 and edge detectors, floor(max) + 3 for a delay line, Smoother for gain / gain_add), checked against its kernel (tests/dsp/test_silence.cpp); subcircuits inherit them through their atoms. *Plan* (`schedule()`, ScheduleOptions::sleep): Block / Loop regions of global stages are SLEEPABLE when, under H = "the segment's sources silent, its waking params unchanged, its state quiescent", every stateful op keeps its state (memory with a Z input, a settled z1 of a param-derived value, a settled smoother) and every value read outside its SEGMENT (the connected sleepable regions, joined also through always-running stateless ops) is Z (a greatest fixpoint, so feedback loops qualify); sources are root inputs, track/sidechain inputs and values of earlier stages (voice reducers), so the decision is made when the stage starts; each region lists the buffers it fills while asleep. *Run time* (the shared host, so both backends decide identically): at each global stage's start, per segment, scan its sources (an early-out backward scan; `ProcessContext::inSilent` answers for silent buses), compare its waking params, and, awake with silent sources, check its state — memory words ≤ ε, the frames a delay line wrote since the last look (once the whole reach after a swap) ≤ ε for its tail, smoother residuals exactly 0 — then flush the ≤ ε residue to 0 and sleep (ε = 2^-30 ≈ −180 dBFS on the state, ≤ −120 dBFS at the output through 60 dB of resonance / feedback gain: a feed-forward tail ends exactly, a feedback tail is cut there). The interpreter skips a sleeping region's records and zero-fills its fill buffers; the generated code branches once per region per block on the segment's flag word in aux (`Layout::sleepOff`) to the same fills (the constant-output kernel). Sources silent → it stays asleep, untouched; anything else wakes it for the whole block, so the first sample after silence is computed from the flushed state: identical to a run that never slept for feed-forward segments, within the −120 dBFS cut for feedback ones, identical to the Shadow mode (`SleepMode::Shadow`: same decisions, every region runs) in both cases, and interpreter == JIT bit for bit (tests/engine/test_silence.cpp, tests/jit/test_jit_silence.cpp). *ABI*: `ProcessContext::inSilent` (in), `outSilent` per global out slot, `tailsDone` (every segment asleep) and `dormant` (tailsDone, outputs silent, no voice group ran, nothing else in the image keeps state: `SleepPlan::dormantCapable`) (out); `ImageInterface::voiceTailFrames`; `ProgramImage::sleepStats()`. Images built with `SleepMode::Off` (InterpretOptions / jit::CompileOptions::sleep) never sleep.

### Voices and note events (engine, T1.8: `pq/engine/note_events.h`, `voices.h`)
- **Events**: `NoteEvent { frame, kind (NoteOn | NoteOff | Pitch | AllNotesOff | AllSoundOff), noteId, hz (float32, resolved upstream), velocity (note-on; release velocity for note-off), ratio, harmonic, unisonCount, unisonIndex, rampFrames, degree, equave (T2.6: a Degree note's identity for the tuning node, NaN = none), tuning (T2.6-fix3: which tuning node of the track patch the note follows, voice.tuning; 0 = none), noteDegree (T4.17: voice.noteDegree, the note's absolute degree in its own tuning, NaN = unknown), flags (`kNoteEventRetune`: a Pitch event that also replaces the note's identity) — 52 bytes }`. `EventBuffer` (preallocated; RT-safe `push` keeps frame order, ties in push order; `clear`; when full it drops the newest NoteOn, then Pitch, and as a last resort merges offs into an AllNotesOff/AllSoundOff, never losing a note-off: T1.8-fix1) exposes an `EventList { events, count }` for `ProcessContext::events`. Producers (T3.16 sequencer, MIDI, JI objects) push events with frame offsets inside the block; a frame ≥ the block length is clamped. `Pitch` moves a note to a new Hz as a step or as a glide linear in cents over `rampFrames` (the hook for T2.6 and pitch curves).
- **VoiceEngine** drives one ProgramImage: `prepare(image, VoiceConfig)` off-thread; `process(ProcessContext&)` (RT-safe) allocates voices from `ctx.events`, renders the voice.* buffers, fills `ctx.voices`, runs the image, mixes each voice `out` slot as `Σ lanes select(gain ≠ 0, out) · gain` (`mix(i)`; an idle lane's NaN never gets in), ends voices and compacts. `VoiceConfig { maxVoices (polyphony; lanes above it are fade headroom: voiceGroupsFor(n) = ceil(n/8) + 1 groups), steal (Oldest | Quietest | None), retriggerSameNote, resetOnNoteOn, compact, stealFadeMs, silenceDb/silenceMs, releaseTimeoutMs, referenceHz (voice.cents), monitorMask, doneSlot (findVoiceSlot(io, patch, "done")), maxEventsPerBlock (NoteOn + Pitch per block; offs always apply), moveBudgetFrames (delay-ring frames compaction may move per block) }`. Inspection: laneStatus/laneOf/liveVoices/activeGroups/groupsRunLastBlock, signal(g, VoiceSignal), gain(g), stats().
- **Sample accuracy without sub-blocks**: an event at frame k changes the per-sample voice.* buffers at k (gate, trigger, hz, cents) and the lane gain (0 before the onset); param-rate voice signals take the lane's latest value for the whole block. A lane starting in a block has its state reset to StateInit at the block start (generic: per-lane words of every voice `StateEntry`, from a template built with `dsp::initState`; noise counters and delay-line headers are shared and kept; a delay line's ring is reset in O(1): `DelayState::start[lane]` = writePos, older taps read 0) and reads hz 0 until the onset, so its output from k on does not depend on block boundaries. A free lane reads hz = referenceHz (finite). Oversize blocks (> maxBlockFrames, refused by the image) still apply their events and voice ends.
- **Allocation**: best fit (a free lane of the fullest non-full occupied group, else the lowest empty group), plus compaction (`VoiceConfig::compact`, default on): at a block end, when the occupied groups exceed ceil(occupied lanes / 8), the least occupied group's voices move to free lanes of other groups (per-lane state words copied; the reachable part of a delay ring, min(history, ⌊maxDelay⌋ + 2) frames, re-aligned to the destination group's write position, at most moveBudgetFrames per block, else the group is not emptied; noise state stays with the group). This relies on the voice state layout rule of `atom_state.h` (per-lane fields are [8] arrays, word ≡ lane mod 8, first in each struct), which every backend's StateArena must keep; which bytes of each atom's state are per lane is declared per atom by `dsp::laneStateInfo` (M1 review L7: shared words are the noise counter, the delay header and phasor incScale/halfSr; a stateful atom without a declaration fails `tests/dsp/test_lane_state.cpp`, which also checks every declaration by running the kernel on lane-permuted inputs). Stealing above maxVoices: released before held, then oldest or quietest (peak of the monitored outs over the last block; voices younger than `quietestMinAgeMs`, still in their attack, only when every candidate is, M1 review L9); the victim fades (raised cosine) over stealFadeMs in its lane (engine-mixed outs and, through `gain`, internal voice_sum / sends) while the new note starts in a free lane; with no free lane it waits for the fade (deferred start at the next block's frame 0). Same-note retrigger (equal Hz) takes over only a released voice that started before this block and was released before the note-on's frame (a visible gate edge), without a reset; held voices are never merged (unison). NoteOff finds the newest held voice of the id, Pitch the newest held, else released; voice_last moves to the newest held/released voice when its lane frees or is stolen.
- **Voice end** (checked at block end): a `done` voice out/export (true or > 0.5 at the last frame), the monitored outs below silenceDb for silenceMs after release, or releaseTimeoutMs after release when there is neither. T1.x-silence: the image's `voiceTailFrames` (its voice stages' longest delay line) is the voice's tail: the silence window is max(silenceMs, tail), and a `done` with a tail starts the tail instead of ending the voice (it ends after its outs have been silent that long; without monitored outs, the tail's length after done), so an echo inside a voice is not cut. Internal `voice_sum` / sends / `voice_last` see the per-sample gain; voice_highest / lowest / loudest the block lane mask: QUESTIONS Q61.
- `ImageRunner::setVoices(VoiceEngine*, EventSource, capacity)`: the runner calls the event source per image block, then the VoiceEngine; an image without global `out`s plays the voice mixes (`pqdaw --play <instrument> --notes "60 64 67"`).
- **Image bindings (T1.9)**: everything image-dependent (voice out slots, mix buffers, lane reset/move spans, the image's voice state) is a `VoiceBinding`. `prepareLanes(io, cfg)` sets up lanes and voice.* buffers for a geometry (maxVoiceGroups, maxBlockFrames, sample rate); `makeBinding(image, doneSlot, monitorMask)` (non-RT) binds any image of that geometry; `attach(current, fadingOut)` (RT) switches at a block boundary, so lanes, notes and allocator state survive a program swap. While a crossfade runs, `process(ctx, fadeCtx)` runs both images on the same voice table, lane resets and compaction moves apply to both, `fadeMix(i)` holds the old image's mixes. `prepare(image, cfg)` = prepareLanes + makeBinding + attach.

### Hot swap (engine, T1.9: `pq/engine/program_host.h`, `state_transfer.h`)
- **ProgramHost** owns the running ProgramImage (any backend: it uses only the ABI). `publish(image, SwapPolicy{mode: Auto | Instant | Crossfade, fadeMs}, VoiceSlots)` (non-RT, internally serialised) builds everything that allocates — the transfer plan against the previously *published* image, the param remap (new slot ← old slot with the same `ParamSlot::key`), output scratch, the VoiceBinding — and pushes a packet through an SpscRing. The audio thread pops at most one packet per image block, never during a crossfade, so packets become current in publish order and each plan's source is exactly the image it replaces. Images must have the host's sample rate and maxBlockFrames (and voice geometry); `lastPublished()->stateLayout()` is the next compile's `ScheduleOptions::previous`. Device mapping as ImageRunner (one rule, `mapChannels`: input c → global root in c; output c ← c-th global `out`, mono on all, else the voice mixes). `HostConfig::externalVoices` runs the images through a caller's prepared VoiceEngine (renderOffline's); `takePending()` makes the first published image current before the first block (so its params can be set).
- **State transfer** (`buildTransferPlan` off-thread, `executeTransfer` RT): a new `StateEntry` takes the old entry with the same (stateKey, channel, arena) when tag and size match; else it keeps StateInit (`TransferPlan::matched/fresh/mismatched/dropped`). Ranges contiguous in both arenas are coalesced (with T1.5's stable offsets an edit is usually one range per arena). It runs on the audio thread at the swap, right before the new image's first block, from the old image's current state; voice ranges are copied per group, only for occupied groups when `resetOnNoteOn` (free lanes are reset at note start anyway), and not at all by a host without voices (`executeTransfer`'s `maxGroups` 0, M1 review L8). `ProgramImage::stateLayout()`/`stateArena()`/`io()` must be callable from the publishing thread while the image runs (they return immutable data / fixed pointers). State keys come from NodeIds: edits made on the Patch IR keep them; edited TEXT keeps them when it is parsed with the previous parse (`ParseOptions::previous`, T1.x-text-ids: ≥ 99 % — measured 100 % — of the corpus's state entries match after an added export, inserted lines, renamed bindings, reordered lines or a reprint); a plain re-parse renumbers nodes (declarations first) and loses the match (Q68). Every caller that re-parses text for a hot swap (pqc, a text editor view) should pass the previous parse; the document lowering builds IR with document-derived NodeIds instead (T3.17-fix2).
- **Swap at the block boundary**: audible (Auto) = transport playing, or any voice lane in use, or the last block's played output above `HostConfig::silenceDb` (−90 dBFS; read from the old image's scratch, no per-block metering). Audible → **linear** crossfade over fadeMs (clamped 5–20 ms): both images run on the same inputs/events/voice table, `out = old + g·(new − old)`, g = (k+1)/N, exactly `new` from g = 1 (bit-exact where old == new: an unchanged path passes untouched; linear because the two programs are correlated, equal-power would bulge up to +3 dB). Silent → instant. Params carried, VoiceEngine re-attached.
- **Reclamation**: the audio thread only advances an atomic "retired through" sequence (instantly, or when the fade ends) and calls `EpochReclaimer::quiescent` at the end of every `process()`; `collect()` (and `publish()`) retire those packets to the reclaimer, which frees them after the next quiescent state. The audio thread never frees. `audioOffline()/audioOnline()` while the stream is stopped.

### Per-track compile units (T1.x-arch1: `pq/graph/project.h`, `pq/engine/project_program.h`, `project_compile.h`)
SPEC §15 (2026-10-21, M1 review H6): the compile unit is the track, not the project. A project is N units, each with its own Schedule, ProgramImage, state arena, swap slot and compile-cache key; an edit recompiles and crossfades only its unit.
- **Project graph** (`graph::ProjectGraph`, plain data; T3.17 lowers the document into it, graph never includes document): `ProjectTrack { id (stable, ≥ 1), name, patch (the track's device chain as one Patch; devices are subcircuits from `library`), role (Instrument | Audio | Group | Return | Master), eventSlot }`, `ProjectWire { from (track, out/export), to (track, in/param), kind (Audio | Sidechain | Modulation) }`, `output` (the master's out), `library`, `taps` (T4.7: out/export ports that get a bus with no reader, after the output's bus; analysis taps). Several wires into one port are summed (an `add` chain in wire order). Cross-track signals are global: a per-voice source is summed over voices where it leaves its track (an identity subcircuit with a global bound), whichever unit the consumer is in.
- **Project graph** (`graph::ProjectGraph`, plain data; T3.17 lowers the document into it, graph never includes document): `ProjectTrack { id (stable, ≥ 1), name, patch (the track's device chain as one Patch; devices are subcircuits from `library`), role (Instrument | Audio | Group | Return | Master), eventSlot, maxVoices (T4.12-fix1: an Instrument's polyphony, from its instrument device's `voices` setting; 0 = the compiler's default — the unit's VoiceConfig::maxVoices and voice groups follow it; a change restarts that unit with a new VoiceEngine) }`, `ProjectWire { from (track, out/export), to (track, in/param), kind (Audio | Sidechain | Modulation) }`, `output` (the master's out), `library`. Several wires into one port are summed (an `add` chain in wire order). Cross-track signals are global: a per-voice source is summed over voices where it leaves its track (an identity subcircuit with a global bound), whichever unit the consumer is in.
- **Partition** (`partitionProject`): one unit per track; Sidechain/Modulation wires merge their tracks (union-find, SPEC §15), except when that would put two instruments in one unit or close a cycle through two (one voice table per image; the wire stays a feed-forward bus, which is just as sample-accurate; a note is recorded: QUESTIONS Q84); cycles between units (Tarjan on the unit graph) merge (SPEC §7.4), and the scheduler handles the loop (an explicit delay, or a z1 + warning in visualMode); a cycle through two instruments is an error. Units come in dependency order (Kahn, ties by lowest track index). A bus per distinct (track, port) read by another unit, plus the output.
- **Composition** (`composeUnit`): the unit's Patch has one subcircuit node per member track whose **NodeId is the track id** (named `t<id>`), so every state key in a track is the same in any unit (merges/splits keep keys; a per-unit and a whole-project program agree on noise seeds); a root `in bus<k>` (producer's kind/width, always audio-rate global, so a producer's rate change never recompiles consumers) per bus read, a root `out bus<k>` per bus written; instrument members export their voice outs as `vmon_<port>` (VoiceEngine monitors) and `done`. `wholeProject()` composes every track as one unit (the reference for tests; M12's background whole-project re-optimisation starts from it). `unitHash` = the compile-cache key (optimised unit + sample rate, block size, voice groups).
- **Compile driver** (`engine::ProjectCompiler`): `build(graph)` compiles units in dependency order (consumer input types from producers' bus types) through a `UnitBackend` (function pointer; null = interpreter, the JIT plugs in without the engine including jit) and returns the `ProjectLayout` + one `UnitImage` per unit (T4.4-fix1: `ProjectCompileOptions::inspect` compiles an inspected track's unit with probes and its instance tree, `onBind` sees every image `bind()` makes and returns an owner the image keeps alive (T4.x-fix-safety: the meter / probe-tap leases it writes): see "User devices") (image + bus bindings by node name + voice slots + param links + the owners of the resources it reads: `ProjectCompileOptions::keepAlive`, T2.6-fix4, kept with the image until the program frees it); `update(graph, tracks[, backend])` recompiles only the units holding those tracks against each unit's previous StateLayout (stable offsets), skips units whose key is unchanged, and refuses topology changes (partition, bus kind/width: rebuild); `bind(unit, image)` binds another image of the unit's current schedule (the background JIT image after the interpreter one: T1b.5).
- **Background compiler** (T1b.5, `engine::BackgroundCompiler`, `pq/engine/background_compile.h`): PF.4: a pool of worker threads (`BackgroundCompilerConfig::threads`, default `engine::defaultCompileThreads()` = min(6, cores − 2), ≥ 1; `PQ_JIT_THREADS` overrides; `LinkConfig::jitThreads`; the patch preview uses 1) at `THREAD_PRIORITY_BELOW_NORMAL` that build images of unit schedules with a `UnitBackend` (the JIT: `compile::jitUnitBackend()`) and hand each to the caller's `deliver` on the worker that built it (concurrently: `deliver` must be thread-safe; ProjectLink's runs under its compile mutex). `want(jobs)` replaces the queue (one job per schedule; a schedule never runs on two workers; schedules new to the queue — the unit just edited — first, refinements last; a running build is never interrupted, its image may come back stale); `beforeBuild` is a test hook (simulated slow compile); `waitIdle()`. Bounded: at most `threads` compiles at a time whatever the edit rate; the UI and the audio thread (MMCSS) preempt them. Cold open of the 30-track fixture (release): every unit on a JIT image 3.5 s → 0.86 s, every final image 7.3 → 1.7 s at 6 workers; peak +128 MB over one worker (bench/RESULTS.md, PF.4). T1b.x-perf (Q111 option C): with `UnitBackend::quick` (`compile::jitUnitBackend(true)`: jit::CompileOptions::quickFirst) a job delivers a quick FIRST image (`BackgroundJob::first`: the JIT's voice groups, no pair functions, not stored on disk) when the cache has no final image and pairs would add code, and queues a refinement (`refine`: the final image) at the end of the queue, so every unit gets a JIT image before any unit's pair image is compiled; both swaps are Instant (bit-identical).
- **Runner** (`engine::ProjectProgram`): buses are planar channels owned by the program at fixed addresses; a unit's image writes its buses directly (its global out slots point at them) and later units read them (root in slots), so feed-forward between units costs nothing and adds no latency. process() per image block: the BlockHook once (transport + project params), then per unit in order: at most one packet popped (none while fading), param links copied, one `ProgramImage::process` (or `VoiceEngine::process` for an instrument unit with its own VoiceEngine and EventSource = `ProjectConfig::eventSources[eventSlot]`, e.g. `Timeline::eventSource(k)`); then the output bus to the device. Per-unit hot swap = T1.9's protocol per slot (transfer plan against the unit's last published image, param remap by key, VoiceBinding, linear crossfade old + g·(new − old) per produced bus channel when the unit is audible: transport playing, a voice of the unit, or its buses above −90 dBFS), so only the edited unit runs twice and every other unit is bit-identical through the swap. One EpochReclaimer for the project; the audio thread never frees. Not yet: device inputs into buses, parallel units across cores.
- **Topology swaps** (T3.17, `ProjectProgram::publishTopology`, `ProjectCompiler::rebuild`): a track added / removed / re-routed changes the partition. `rebuild(graph, dirty)` re-partitions and REUSES every unit whose member tracks, bus connections (by bus key `"<track id>:<port>"`, `ProjectLayout::busKey`), input bus types and param links are unchanged and none of whose tracks is dirty: no compile (adding a track compiles the new unit plus the buses it feeds, e.g. master and the returns). `publishTopology(layout, units)` keeps the storage of every bus whose key and width are unchanged (so a continued unit's running image needs no rebinding), and the audio thread switches at one block boundary — run order, buses and every new image (T1.9 swaps: state transfer, crossfade when audible) at once, after the involved units' queued packets and fades are done. Removed units run on as zombies (in an order that still feeds their readers' old images) until every reader has finished the crossfade to its new image, so a removed track fades out instead of cutting off; then they are freed off the audio thread. Unit indices are those of the last published layout; `publish()` refuses while a topology is pending (`canPublish()`). Units whose member set changed (a merge, a split) start fresh.
- **Idle units** (T3.17-fix2, M3 review H1 "silence is free"; the unit-level start of T1.x-silence): `ProjectCompiler` proves per unit (`engine::idleAfterBlocks`) that its outputs are exactly zero while its input buses are silent and no voice sounds (zero propagation over the optimised patch) and that its only global state is smoothed gains with constant times; `UnitImage::idleAfterBlocks` = the blocks of unchanged params after which those gains have snapped onto their targets. The runner then skips such a unit in a block where every bus it reads is silent, it has no live voice and no note event, it is not fading and its linked params have been unchanged that long: bit-exact (running it would change nothing; tested against `idleUnits = false`), its bus channels are zeroed once and marked silent so readers can go idle too. A silent 64 × 8 project costs ≈ 0.4 % of a core (interpreted). The interpreter also skips a global Control region that reads only params and constants into buffers nothing else writes while the image's params equal the previous block's (the settled mixer's dB / pan math). **T1.x-silence** generalises the skip: a unit is also skipped while its image reported itself `dormant` after the last block it ran (every sleepable region asleep — the reverbs' and delays' tails done —, outputs silent, nothing else keeping state) and every bus it reads is silent, no voice, no event, no linked param change, no fade (bit-exact for the same reason; tested against `idleUnits = false`). T5.4: a unit that reads clip slots (`ProjectConfig::clipIn` / `clipActive` from the timeline's clip player; `idleAfterBlocks` treats `clipPlayer` as a zero source) is quiet only in blocks where none of its slots has audio, so an audio track whose clips are not under the playhead sleeps like an empty MIDI track. A bus is marked silent by its producer's `outSilent` report (every slot writing it ±0; never during a fade; T5.x-perf-audio: computed while the image copies the out, one pass) as well as by an idle producer, and the program passes the flags to its readers as `ProcessContext::inSilent`, so a silent project sleeps unit by unit downstream. `quiet()` also requires every live unit's `tailsDone` (no tail in progress). The idle 30-track stdlib reverb/delay project (tests/engine/idle_project_fixture.h) then costs bench/RESULTS.md's `pq_bench_silence` rows. T4.x-fix-engine (M4 review L1): the linked params are compared only in blocks after a project param may have changed — a generation counts writes through `projectParams()` (the engine's param pops), an adopted larger param block, and the BlockHook's own counter (`BlockHook::paramWrites`: the timeline bumps it only when an automated value changes) — so a silent project with linked device params costs one generation compare per unit, not one per link (bench `idle/30 tracks <backend> links`). **Param block growth** (M4 review M4): `ProjectProgram::reserveProjectParams(n)` (non-RT) makes a larger block; the audio thread adopts it at its next block, or at once when an installed image or a popped write needs it, copying the values (one memcpy; old blocks kept until the program goes); `Engine::growParamRing(n)` (UI thread) chains a larger param ring behind the current one (the audio thread drains the old ring first, so writes stay in order; `collect()` frees left rings). ProjectLink starts at 16,384 slots and doubles them when all are in use (up to 2^24; past that the status line says so).
- **Bus meters**: `ProjectLayout::busMetered` buses get peak and mean square of channels 0–1 per block on the audio thread (one AVX2 pass); `readMeters(buses, levels)` reads them since the previous call (T3.17's track meters: the `"<id>:out"` buses).
- **Plugin-delay compensation** (T6.2; `pq/graph/latency.h`, `pq/engine/latency_plan.h`, ProjectProgram "Plugin-delay compensation", ProjectCompiler's file comment): units REPORT latency, READERS delay their inputs. `graph::pathLatency(typed)` gives every node of an optimised patch the longest sum of reported latencies from a source (AtomSignature::latency rules: the `latency(x, frames)` atom — the delay line's kernel at a constant time, a kernel_map alias — reports frames / its region's factor; `oversample_up` / `_down` half of `dsp::os::roundTripLatency(factor)` each; z1 / delays / filters 0). `CompiledUnit::outLatency` (per written bus key) and `compensatedKeys` (buses read through Audio / Sidechain wires; Modulation reads are never delayed, SPEC §15 2026-10-24) are derived, never part of the cache key (PF.1). `ProjectCompiler::latencies()` → `planCompensation(layout, units, unitOffset)` in unit order: `A_u = max L(read bus)`, input delay `round(A_u − L)`, `L(produced) = A_u + out + offset`, total = `round(L(output))`; O(units + bus reads). `ProjectProgram::setCompensation(plan)` (non-RT; a ring like topologies, taken at a block boundary once the program runs the plan's topology): per (reader unit, bus) a preallocated `DelayLine` (power-of-two ring ≥ delay + block, `width` channels) runs in the READER's slot before its image (push the bus block, read the delayed block: one copy each per channel), the image's `ProcessContext::in` points at it (`Packet::inLive`); no new edge between units (PF.5-safe). A delay change crossfades old → new tap over 10 ms; a dropped line fades to 0 then is bypassed; lines carry over between plans (a larger ring copies the history once); a line whose bus has been silent for delay + block reads silence at no copy (idle units stay idle; `quiet()` waits for held audio and notes). A voice unit with A_u > 0 delays its note events by A_u (`EventDelay`, preallocated FIFO); automation lanes of a compensated unit's params carry `AutomationLaneDesc::shiftFrames` (the link republishes them). A MERGED unit aligns the audio wires into each member track at compose time (`composeUnit`'s `wireDelay` puts `latency` nodes on early wires; found by a first front end with probes, a second one only when misaligned). No latency anywhere: no plan is made, no line exists, bit-identical output (tested). `ProjectProgram::outputLatency()` is the total for views, recording and the metronome. Not delayed yet: a compensated unit's own clip players and a merged member's own sources against a later sidechain (QUESTIONS Q?-T6.2-a).

### Playback link (T3.17: `pq/document/lower.h`, `pq/engine/engine.h`, `pq/app/project_link.h`, `src/app/src/audio_output.h`)
- **Lowering** (`doc::lower`, document; IR with stable ids since T3.17-fix2, M3 review H1/H2): per active track a `graph::Patch` built directly (no generated text; `doc::patchText` prints one for debugging) whose NodeIds come from the document: fixed ids for the role's own nodes (ports, mixer stage, the instrument call), `doc::nodeIdFor(role, entity id, k)` for nodes that exist per document entity (a tuning node per tuning id, a send input per sending track id; `NodeRole::Device`: T4.1 device chains — a device instance = a subcircuit call `d<id>` with `nodeIdFor(Device, device id, 0)`, T4.7 cross-device wires = the modulation math feeding those calls' param pins, see "Cross-device wiring", so a chain edit keeps the state of every untouched device; see "Device chains"). State keys therefore follow document entities, never construction order. A lowering shares the previous patch object of a track whose IR is equal (`patchHash` with ids + `operator==`), which is how the link sees which tracks changed. MIDI = the default instrument (`stdlib/instruments/basic_synth.pqp`, Q15 A; pitch = `voice.hz`) → `voice_sum` → three `gain` stages (pq/dsp/atoms.h: a param gain at block rate, smoothed per sample only while it moves, settled = one multiply): `out` (constant-power pan · volume · gate · unmuted), export `post` (pan · volume · sendGate · unmuted) and export `pre` (pan · sendGate · unmuted); groups take `in` (balance pan) and returns the sum of their sends; the master `in` → balance · volume → `out`. **Sends live in the return** (the return set is not part of a track's patch): one `in s<k>` + `send<k>` param + `gain_add` per sender (a send at −∞ is a settled gain_add at 0: the accumulator passes through, in place), wired from the sender's `post` or `pre` export; so adding a return compiles only the return and the master. Return → return sends are lowered only while stored (Live disables them until used), and a send that would close a delay-free loop (a return loop; a return routed into a group that sends to it) only while stored, with an automatic `z1` on it (SPEC §15 2026-10-24; `LoweredProject::delayedSends`, `doc::delayedSends`; the link names it in the status line); `doc::conditionalSendsKey` tells the link when a send level edit changes the graph. Routing: a deactivated target group plays nothing (L1); a return may output to a group (L2). Mute/solo → `computeAudibility(p, {ignoreMute})` → the `gate`/`sendGate` params, and the mute is its own param `mute` (≥ 0.5 muted), so a mute lane plays (M3). Params carry stable keys (`"<id>:volume"`, `":pan"`, `":gate"`, `":sendGate"`, `":mute"`, `"<sender>:send:<ret>"` on the return's patch), mapped by the app to project param slots that never move. `lowerParams` recomputes values without lowering the graph. **Tuning signal (T2.6-fix1)**: a MIDI track whose track-level effective tuning is a main tuning with a linear form (`doc::trackTuningSignal`: EDO, MOS without MODMOS / generator sequence) gets `tun = tuning_hz(voice.degree, voice.equave, voice.hz, voice.cents, tgen, tper, tst, tref, table: k)` + `tunv = tune_voices(tun.hz, tun.cents)` before the instrument (EDO: `0, tper, 0, tref`, tper = the equave); the params' keys are per tuning (`"tuning:<id>:reference"`, `":equave"`, `":generator"`, `":period"`, `":stretch"`, `paramKey` of the tuning `ParamRef`s, so tuning automation lanes render into them; every following track links the same slot), their text defaults are fixed, and `k` comes from `LowerOptions::tuningTableOf` (unset: no nodes, the T3.17 behaviour; `lowerParams(p, true)` matches a lowering with it). **T2.6-fix3**: one node per tuning signal the track's clips follow (`doc::trackTuningSignals`: the track-level one, then clip overrides to other main tunings by id), chained (`tun<i> = tuning_hz(…, tun<i−1>.hz, tun<i−1>.cents, …, select: voice.tuning, node: i)`, params `tref<i>` …); Degree notes, Ratio notes anchored (through notes) to them, and reference-relative Ratio / Cents notes follow their clip's node (`doc::PitchFollow`); private, Scala and MODMOS tunings play static Hz (QUESTIONS Q105). **Cost** (PF.6, `pq_bench_lower`): linear in the project — each plan node reads only its own wires (`WirePlan::into` / `outOf`, the input sums grouped by target pin once), the wires by target device, the devices by id and the literal value blocks' reach to a choice (one backward pass) are built once per lowering, a callee's call signature once per callee; release: 64 default-instrument tracks 112–232 → ≈ 20–40 ms, 30 tracks × 8 modules (12.9k blocks, 19k wires) 535–980 → ≈ 45–60 ms, output unchanged (the bench's lowering digest).
- **Engine** (`engine::Engine`): owns the Timeline and the ProjectProgram; a UI → audio SPSC of param writes and one of audition notes (mixed into the instrument slot's events); an idle gate (asleep after 250 ms of quiet: transport stopped, `ProjectProgram::quiet()`, output ≤ −120 dBFS; woken by any command, write, audition or publish) that answers blocks with silence without running anything. T3.17-fix1: `quiet()` is a flag the program computes inside `process()` before its reclaimer hears the audio thread is quiescent, over the live slots only (M3 review C1: it read freed zombie units); the engine keeps its own monotonic time base (`time()`; a device block's frameTime is ignored, so a reopened device cannot move audition note-offs or the units' sample time back, M15b); `audioOffline()` ends sounding auditions; `audioStopped()` tells the compile path not to wait for an audio thread that is not there.
- **Lowering** (`doc::lower`, document; IR with stable ids since T3.17-fix2, M3 review H1/H2): per active track a `graph::Patch` built directly (no generated text; `doc::patchText` prints one for debugging) whose NodeIds come from the document: fixed ids for the role's own nodes (ports, mixer stage, the instrument call), `doc::nodeIdFor(role, entity id, k)` for nodes that exist per document entity (a tuning node per tuning id, a send input per sending track id; `NodeRole::Device` is reserved for M4's device chains: a device instance = a subcircuit call with `nodeIdFor(Device, device id, 0)`, cross-device wires = wires between those calls, so a chain edit keeps the state of every untouched device). State keys therefore follow document entities, never construction order. A lowering shares the previous patch object of a track whose IR is equal (`patchHash` with ids + `operator==`), which is how the link sees which tracks changed. MIDI = the default instrument (`stdlib/instruments/basic_synth.pqp`, Q15 A; pitch = `voice.hz`) → `voice_sum` → three `gain` stages (pq/dsp/atoms.h: a param gain at block rate, smoothed per sample only while it moves, settled = one multiply): `out` (constant-power pan · volume · gate · unmuted), export `post` (pan · volume · sendGate · unmuted) and export `pre` (pan · sendGate · unmuted); groups take `in` (balance pan) and returns the sum of their sends; the master `in` → balance · volume → `out`. **Sends live in the return** (the return set is not part of a track's patch): one `in s<k>` + `send<k>` param + `gain_add` per sender (a send at −∞ is a settled gain_add at 0: the accumulator passes through, in place), wired from the sender's `post` or `pre` export; so adding a return compiles only the return and the master. Return → return sends are lowered only while stored (Live disables them until used), and a send that would close a delay-free loop (a return loop; a return routed into a group that sends to it) only while stored, with an automatic `z1` on it (SPEC §15 2026-10-24; `LoweredProject::delayedSends`, `doc::delayedSends`; the link names it in the status line); `doc::conditionalSendsKey` tells the link when a send level edit changes the graph. Routing: a deactivated target group plays nothing (L1); a return may output to a group (L2). Mute/solo → `computeAudibility(p, {ignoreMute})` → the `gate`/`sendGate` params, and the mute is its own param `mute` (≥ 0.5 muted), so a mute lane plays (M3). Params carry stable keys (`"<id>:volume"`, `":pan"`, `":gate"`, `":sendGate"`, `":mute"`, `"<sender>:send:<ret>"` on the return's patch), mapped by the app to project param slots that never move. `lowerParams` recomputes values without lowering the graph. **Tuning signal (T2.6-fix1)**: a MIDI track whose track-level effective tuning is a main tuning with a linear form (`doc::trackTuningSignal`: EDO, MOS without MODMOS / generator sequence) gets `tun = tuning_hz(voice.degree, voice.equave, voice.hz, voice.cents, tgen, tper, tst, tref, table: k)` + `tunv = tune_voices(tun.hz, tun.cents)` before the instrument (EDO: `0, tper, 0, tref`, tper = the equave); the params' keys are per tuning (`"tuning:<id>:reference"`, `":equave"`, `":generator"`, `":period"`, `":stretch"`, `paramKey` of the tuning `ParamRef`s, so tuning automation lanes render into them; every following track links the same slot), their text defaults are fixed, and `k` comes from `LowerOptions::tuningTableOf` (unset: no nodes, the T3.17 behaviour; `lowerParams(p, true)` matches a lowering with it). **T2.6-fix3**: one node per tuning signal the track's clips follow (`doc::trackTuningSignals`: the track-level one, then clip overrides to other main tunings by id), chained (`tun<i> = tuning_hz(…, tun<i−1>.hz, tun<i−1>.cents, …, select: voice.tuning, node: i)`, params `tref<i>` …); Degree notes, Ratio notes anchored (through notes) to them, and reference-relative Ratio / Cents notes follow their clip's node (`doc::PitchFollow`); private, Scala and MODMOS tunings play static Hz (QUESTIONS Q105).
- **Engine** (`engine::Engine`): owns the Timeline and the ProjectProgram; a UI → audio SPSC of param writes and one of audition notes (mixed into the instrument slot's events); an idle gate (asleep after 250 ms of quiet: transport stopped, `ProjectProgram::quiet()`, output ≤ −120 dBFS; woken by any command, write, audition or publish) that answers blocks with silence without running anything. T1.x-silence (M3 review M15a): `quiet()` includes every unit's `tailsDone`, so the gate never sleeps (freezing state) while a sleepable tail rings — a delay whose echo is still in its line keeps the engine awake until it has played and decayed; what the gate still freezes is state outside every sleep segment (a free-running LFO, a held value), as before. T3.17-fix1: `quiet()` is a flag the program computes inside `process()` before its reclaimer hears the audio thread is quiescent, over the live slots only (M3 review C1: it read freed zombie units); the engine keeps its own monotonic time base (`time()`; a device block's frameTime is ignored, so a reopened device cannot move audition note-offs or the units' sample time back, M15b); `audioOffline()` ends sounding auditions; `audioStopped()` tells the compile path not to wait for an audio thread that is not there.
- **ProjectLink** (app, `TransportPort` + `MeterSource`): per ChangeSet — Param: `lowerParams` diffed against the last written values → `setParam`; Sequence: per-track `TrackSequenceDesc` cache (keyed by the track node, its tuning signals and, per clip, the definition its notes resolve in: the node table's for a clip following a signal, so a tuning param drag re-lowers no following track; T2.6-fix3) → `SequenceImage` published at once; pitches from `doc::resolveTrackPitches` (T2.7; T2.6-fix3: resolved in the node table's definition through `ResolveOptions::signalTuning`), a note following one of the track's tuning signals carrying its `PitchSource` (tuning id, node number, Degree with its (root's) degree, or Reference); a Param change that touches a tuning re-lowers the sequences of the static notes and publishes only if a description changed; Automation: `AutomationImage` (targets = slots; automated slots are not written from the UI); Timeline: `TempoImage`; Structural: debounced (50 ms) compile on a worker thread: lower → dirty tracks (patch not shared with the previous lowering) → `ProjectCompiler::update` (per-unit swaps) or, on a topology change, `rebuild` + `publishTopology` (TMM.17: a unit whose instrument member changes — a MIDI track gaining or losing its instrument, Instrument ↔ Audio role — is a topology change too: the unit becomes or stops being a voice unit, a new VoiceEngine). Every compile publishes interpreted images (audible at once) and then wants the JIT image of every unit schedule that has none (`LinkConfig::jit`, default on; pqdaw links the JIT through `pq_compile`): the background compiler builds them, `deliverJit` binds and publishes each into the unit that still runs that schedule with an **Instant** swap (bit-identical images, full state transfer: sample-exact, tested), under the compile mutex that serialises it with the compile path; a full unit queue is retried without the lock while the audio thread runs (≤ 5 s); a stale image (its unit recompiled) is dropped; a failed JIT leaves the unit interpreted (not retried). T4.x-fix-engine (M4 review M1): an image that cannot be published now — the output stopped, earlier compile results still queued, a pending topology, or the 5 s spent — is **parked** (`LinkStats::parkedJit`; a newer image of the same schedule replaces it) and published by `drainDeferred` after the deferred results (worker every 20 ms, `flush()`, `waitForJit()`), so every unit ends on its newest schedule's JIT image. A JIT publish does not wake a sleeping engine. T1b.x-perf: the default backend builds quick first images (Q111 option C; `JitStats::firstImages`, `refined`), and an edit of a unit that plays a JIT image keeps that image playing instead of publishing the interpreted one when the interpreted image would cost more than `LinkConfig::interpretBudget` of a core (`engine::estimateInterpretedNsPerFrame` at full polyphony; `LinkStats::heldForJit`): the edit's JIT image then replaces it with the default (crossfading) policy, and a failed JIT publishes the interpreted image after all (M4 review M2: through the deferred queue, its publish checked). M4 review M3: a topology rebuild holds the same way — a rebuilt unit whose member tracks are those of a unit playing a JIT image (Operator's `voices`, which makes a new unit; a continued unit) and whose interpreted image would cost more than the budget keeps the whole `Deferred` topology queued (`Deferred::waiting`, `LinkStats::heldTopologies`) until its JIT image is bound into `TopologyBuild::units`; a later compile, or a failed JIT, releases it interpreted. A cached unit's JIT image arrives within about a millisecond either way (the quick path asks the cache for the final image first). The images come from `LinkConfig::jitBackend` (default `compile::jitUnitBackend()`: the process compiler, which in pqdaw has the user cache and the AOT seed, T1b.4), so a unit already compiled once (any project, any earlier run of this binary) or a stock unit arrives in about a millisecond instead of ~100 ms. `jitStats()`, `waitForJit()`. **Tuning tables** (T2.6-fix1): `ProjectLink` owns `Resources::tunings` (a fixed array of `kTuningTables` = 256 view pointers, `ProjectCompileOptions::resources`); the UI thread builds a table (`engine::makeTuningTable`) the first time a snapshot shows a signal tuning with a new `ProjectLink::TableKey` (EDO n; MOS n, periods per equave, the root's chain position, the region hint — T2.6-fix2: a hint change on a Param edit is then a compile), immutable; the lowering's `tuningTableOf` is the index of (tuning, key). **T2.6-fix4** (M3 review M8): tables share one `dsp::TuningShape` per (kind, n) and are owned by who uses them — the current document (`held_`, rebuilt fresh at a Reset: New / Open never reuse an earlier project's table), the running compile, every unit image that reads one (`ProjectCompileOptions::keepAlive` → `UnitImage::keepAlive`, freed with the image by the program's epoch reclaimer) and the sequence cache; an unowned table is freed and its slot of the 256 reused; with all slots in use a tuning plays static pitches and the link says so (`LinkStats::tablesExhausted`, the status line). Each slot's `TuningLiveInputs` (owned by the link) are the taps of the automation lanes on its tuning's params (`liveTap`); every other lane renders one value per block. A continuous edit never rebuilds a table, so a Degree note that follows the node gets its static `NoteDesc::hz` resolved in the TABLE's definition: the node passes it through while its inputs equal that definition (bit-identical to static pitches) and computes from the params otherwise. Playhead: timeline reports (one per engine block) with a command counter, so `playhead()` shows the commanded state until the audio thread has taken it. Audition: the detail clip's track's slot. **T3.17-fix1**: compile results are handed to the program in order through a queue (`drainDeferred`): while the audio thread runs, a publish waits (bounded) for room; when the output is stopped (`Engine::audioStopped`, after one wake and 30 ms) the rest stays queued and is published by the worker (every 20 ms) once audio runs again, so `flush()` returns at once (L11) and nothing is dropped; JIT deliveries wait behind the queue. The worker collects the program's retired images for ~2 s after a compile, `playhead()`/`read()` collect at most every 200 ms (L13); `live()` stays true until every meter read has decayed below the meter floor, and an asleep engine reads as silence, so meters fall after a stop instead of freezing lit; `lastError()` is mutex-guarded (L12). **T3.17-fix3**: 512 note-event slots (128 events / 128 sounding notes per slot), beyond them the status line names the limit (`LinkStats::tracksWithoutSlot`, M5); automation lanes overridden by hand (`ViewContext::automationOverridden`, T3.15's Re-enable Automation) are left out of the `AutomationImage` until re-enabled (M4); a lane on a tuning without a signal is reported inert (and new ones are refused by the document, M3); `LinkConfig::chanceSeed` seeds chance (the window: a random one per session; `--render --seed N`, default 0).
- **Plugin-delay compensation** (T6.2, `src/app/src/project_link_latency.cpp`): every compile result carries `planLatency()` (the plan of `ProjectCompiler::latencies()` and, per project param slot, its unit's A_u); `drainDeferred` hands the plan to `ProjectProgram::setCompensation` right after that result's images / topology (not when equal to the last one: a project without latency never makes one). Changed shifts republish the automation image on the UI thread (`AutomationLaneDesc::shiftFrames`; posted, and picked up by `flush()` / `playhead()` / `renderOffline`). `ProjectLink::outputLatency()` = the master total.
- **Per-device cost** (T4.13, `src/app/src/project_link_cost.cpp`): `ProjectLink` implements `views::project_patch::DeviceCostSource` — `bindCost` binds every unit image (through `ProjectCompileOptions::onBind`: the initial build, unit updates, topology builds, JIT deliveries) to the `engine::CostMeter` of its schedule (keyed by the schedule, tagged with it; T4.x-fix-safety, M4 review H2: each image holds its meter through `UnitImage::keepAlive`, so a meter is freed by the program's epoch reclaimer with the last image that writes it, never by wall time; the link drops its own reference once the schedule no longer runs and no image holds the meter); `readDeviceCosts` (UI thread, `try_lock` on the compile mutex: a compile in progress returns the last values) attributes each reading to the member tracks' device calls by NodeId (only patches known alive — the current lowering's track patches and user devices' resolutions, the device library — are read).
- **AudioOutput** (app, `pq/app/audio_output.h`): one control thread owns the device: opens an `OutputConfig` (backend, share mode, device, rate = the engine's, buffer, channels) in the background, falls back to a NullDevice. AU.stream: the stream keeps running while the app is in use (the engine asleep costs a memset per buffer: FL Studio ASIO at 1024 frames, 0.62 % of a core for the whole process, 3.5 µs per 21.3 ms callback); it stops only after `kBackgroundSuspendSeconds` (300 s) of the engine asleep with the window in the background (`setForeground`, from the window's focus / minimise events; WASAPI's client reset done then), and restarts on the engine's wake hook or the foreground. (Until AU.stream it stopped after 1 s of engine sleep: every Play after a pause restarted the driver, and FL Studio ASIO dips its output ≈ 125 ms into every stream: the user's click.) Every driver start (open, reconfigure, resume, an ASIO reset's restart; not the NullDevice) is a cold start: `onDevice` plays silence without running the engine (left `audioOffline`, so its transport — a Play pressed meanwhile — waits) until ≥ max(output latency + 4 buffers, 0.25 s) of frames have gone out with the last 4 buffers xrun-free (`CallbackStats::xrunsNow`), at most 1 s; then the engine goes online on the audio thread, and the output ramps in over 5 ms. A stop this side initiates while something sounds (reconfigure, close, an ASIO reset via the hook) first ramps the output to 0 over 5 ms, then plays silence until the driver's latency + one buffer has drained (bounded), then stops. T3.18: `reconfigure(config)` (stop → close → open → start; the engine and its Program are untouched), `close()` + `rebind(engine)` (for a rate change), a lost device (WASAPI audio thread ended, ASIO reset failed) is reopened once, else silence on the NullDevice until `hardwareChanged()` (DeviceChangeNotifier) retries; ASIO's stream-changed callback re-prepares the BlockAdapter for a new buffer size and, at a rate ≠ the engine's, plays silence and reports `rateMismatch`; the device's outputs beyond 1/2 get silence; the test tone (uint32 phase, 2048-point table, ≤ −12 dBFS) is added after the engine on the audio thread.
- **Audio device manager** (app, `pq/app/audio_manager.h`, T3.18): implements `views::AudioPrefsPort` over the AudioOutput; owns the user's `AudioSettings` (`pq/app/user_settings.h`: `%APPDATA%\pqdaw\settings.json`, `$PQ_SETTINGS_DIR` overrides; atomic save, lenient load; the window's command-line device flags override it for one run). `apply(s)`: same rate → `reconfigure`; another rate → `close` → `ProjectLink::setSampleRate` (engine + compiler rebuilt at the rate, the document compiled again, slots / sequences / automation / params / transport kept: every image has the rate folded in, Q102) → `rebind` → `reconfigure`; `poll()` (UI thread, after the output's notification) follows a device rate change the same way, and marks device lists stale after a hardware change. Device lists: ASIO drivers from the registry (capabilities from the open driver: one driver per process), WASAPI endpoints from `enumerate()`. CPU meter = Δ callback time / Δ wall time between two calls, 0 while the stream is stopped.
- **Patch preview** (T4.4, `pq/app/patch_preview.h`; T4.5: probe taps from fixed pools — 8 rings, 256 levels — bound by probe id to the interpreter's and the JIT's image of each schedule, a tap freed by a probe that went away reused only once no image bound to it is alive — T4.x-fix-engine, M4 review L4: each image holds its slots' leases through `ProgramHost::publish`'s `keepAlive`, released by the first `collect()` after the audio thread ran the image for the last time; the ring pool grows to 32 while older images hold rings; one `CostMeter` tagged with the play() generation): `app::PatchPreview` implements the circuit editor's `views::circuit::PatchPlayer`: two ProgramHosts (global patches; patches with voice stages, played by the `--play` arpeggio), images built on the UI thread with the interpreter and published through the T1.9 path (state transfer, crossfade while audible; Instant when starting), the JIT image of the same schedule from a `BackgroundCompiler` (`PreviewConfig::jit`, pqdaw: `compile::jitUnitBackend()`) published with an Instant swap (bit-identical; a stale one is dropped). `process()` (RT-safe) adds it into a device block; `AudioOutput::setExtraSource(cb)` (a sequentially consistent handshake: it returns once no callback uses the previous source) mixes it in after the engine and the test tone, and keeps the stream from suspending while set — ShellApp sets it only while the preview plays (`onActiveChanged`) and rebuilds the preview's hosts after a device rate change (`setSampleRate`).
- **Offline**: `pqdaw --project <dir> --render out.wav [--seconds N]` = ProjectLink (synchronous compiles, no idle gate) → `Engine::renderOffline`.
- **Offline**: `pqdaw --project <dir> --render out.wav [--seconds N] [--seed N]` = ProjectLink (synchronous compiles, no idle gate) → `Engine::renderOffline`. A default length (the last clip's end + 2 s) above 4 h is refused: pass `--seconds` (L5).
- **Audio tracks** (T5.3; played since T5.4, see "Audio clips" below): the lowering gives an audio track `x = clipPlayer(clip: <slot>)` (global stereo) → its audio effects → balance pan and the MIDI track's three gain stages, role `graph::TrackRole::Audio` (lower.h "Audio"; golden `tests/app/fixtures/lowering/audio-track.pqp`). `LowerOptions::clipSlotOf` gives the slot: ProjectLink shares the event-slot allocator (`slotForTrack`: stable per track, freed with it); no slot = no clipPlayer (silence) and the track is in `LoweredProject::withoutSlot`. The clips are not in the graph: `doc::lowerAudioTrack(project, path, {available})` → `AudioTrackDesc` (per clip: sample id, ticks, the warp law with every number resolved — Off: τ(start), seconds, contentStartFrame, speed = rate · transpose × detune ratio; RePitch: content start, loop, frames per content tick —, linear gain, clamped fades, the crossfade window, reverse, `playable` = active and not missing; `sourceFrameAt` is the reference law). Audio clip edits are Sequence changes (no compile, tested: `tests/app/test_audio_tracks.cpp`).
- **Audio clips** (T5.4, `src/app/src/project_link_audio.cpp`): `ProjectLink` owns the `media::SamplePool` (its disk thread; its demotions retire through the link's own `EpochReclaimer`, of which the engine's timeline is a reader, so a pool outlives engines rebuilt by `setSampleRate`) and a loader thread (`SamplePool::load` off the UI thread; a finished load republishes through the UI poster `setUiPoster`, or at the next `flush()` / `waitForSamples()` / playhead poll). `publishAudio` lowers every audio track with a slot (`doc::lowerAudioTrack`, cached per track by its node, the tempo map, the tunings, the sample table and the resolution) into `engine::AudioTrackDesc`s, attaches the loaded samples (`engine::ClipSample`: the pool entry) and publishes an `engine::AudioSequenceImage` only when a description or a sample changed: on Sequence and Timeline changes, a relocation (`SamplesRelocated`), a re-resolution (`views::view_change::kSamples`) and a tuning param change (a Steps transpose follows its tuning; T5.3 resolves it). Missing samples (the context's resolution; without one `LinkConfig::projectDir`) and samples still loading play silence. Transport: the disk thread is engaged while playing; a locate while stopped (and a moved loop start) prefetches the stream of every streamed clip under the position or within the look-ahead (`ClipPlayer::streamStart`, exactly the claim the voice will make); `renderOffline` waits for loads and installs a blocking fill (`SamplePool::waitFilled`), so renders do not depend on disk speed. `diskUnderruns()` → `AudioPrefsPort::diskUnderruns()` → the control bar's CPU meter reads "DISK" for 2 s after late reads; the status line names a burst (at most every 5 s). `LinkConfig::edgeDeclick` (default on) is the plan's clip-edge declick setting (QUESTIONS Q151).
- **JIT cache and AOT seed** (T1b.4, `pq/app/jit_cache.h`): `main` calls `configureJitCache()` for the modes that compile (window, `--play`, `--render`): the user cache `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pqdaw\jit-cache` (`$PQ_JIT_CACHE_DIR` names another, `off` turns it off) and the read-only seed `<exe dir>/jit-aot`. `seedJitAot(dir)` (`pqdaw --jit-aot <dir>`, run by a POST_BUILD step after every pqdaw link, option `PQ_JIT_AOT_SEED`) plays the stock projects — a new project, 1/2/4/8 default-instrument MIDI tracks, with a return, in a group, a track per stock effect after the default instrument (T4.1) and per other stock instrument as its instrument (T4.12: Operator at its defaults; `StockDevice::kind`) — at 44.1/48/88.2/96 kHz through ProjectLink with a `Target::Baseline` compiler that stores into `dir`, so the seed's keys are exactly the DAW's (40 entries, ~0.1 MiB, < 1 s in release).

### Timeline: transport, sequencer, automation (engine, T3.16: `pq/engine/timeline.h`, `sequence.h`, `automation.h`)
- **Plain-data descriptions** (the engine never includes `document`; T3.17's lowering fills them): `TrackSequenceDesc { id, clips }`, `ClipDesc { id, start, length, contentStart, loopEnabled/loopStart/loopEnd, active, notes }`, `NoteDesc { start (content ticks), length, id, hz (resolved float32), velocity/releaseVelocity (0–127), chance, velocityRange, ratio, harmonic, PitchSource (T2.6: tuning id + degree/equave of a Degree note following a tuning signal, carried on its note-on as NoteEvent::degree / equave; tuning 0 = static Hz; doc::resolveTrackPitches fills it), keyDegree (T4.17: `doc::NotePitch::keyDegree`, the note's absolute degree in its clip's tuning — fractional between degrees for other pitches — carried as NoteEvent::noteDegree), enabled }`; `AutomationLaneDesc { target, emptyValue, points: AutomationPoint {tick, value, curve}, tap, perSample }` (T2.6-fix2: see Automation below); tempo/time signatures as `TempoImage::create(TempoPoint[], TimeSigChange[])`.
- **Images** (immutable, built off-thread): `SequenceImage::build` (per track a flat clip array sorted by start, overlaps trimmed (later wins, Q41); per clip a flat note array sorted by (start, id) with precomputed pass-0 / loop index ranges; unplayable notes dropped; 100k notes ≈ 2.6 ms), `AutomationImage::build`, `TempoImage`. `Timeline::publishSequence/Automation/Tempo` swap an atomic pointer and retire the old image to the timeline's own EpochReclaimer (`collect()`); the audio thread re-loads the pointers at every block start and is quiescent at the end of `process()`. T3.17-fix1: the audio thread keeps using its current images across blocks (and across a device stop), so it publishes which ones it holds (a hazard pair per kind: `cur`, and `cand` while it takes a newer one: store, re-read, bounded); a replaced image it still holds is kept back (`held_`) and retired once it lets go — before, an edit while the stream was suspended (the reader offline) freed the image the next block read.
- **Transport** (audio thread): commands `Play(t) / Stop / Continue / Locate(t) / SetLoop` through `post()` (SPSC, UI → audio) or `apply()`; reports `TransportReport { block, tick, songSample, bpm, loopWraps, playing }` through `poll()` (lossy SPSC, at most one per `reportEveryFrames` while playing, always after a command). Position = int64 song sample; each block plays ticks `[T0, firstTickAtOrAfterSample(P + frames))`, so a note at t sounds at exactly `ticksToSamples(t)` for any block size. Arrangement loop wraps at the exact sample `ticksToSamples(loop end)` (any number per block); a new tempo map keeps the musical position. `snapshot()` (TransportSnapshot for `ProcessContext::transport`) describes frame 0.
- **Sequencer**: per track a cursor (clip, pass, note index; loops unrolled lazily, pass by pass) and a min-heap of sounding notes; per block a single compare against `ticksToSamples(min over tracks of the next event)` decides whether any track has an event (idle block ≈ 21 ns for 1 track, 30 ns for 64; 0.47 ns/track). Events go to per-track lists in frame order, offs before ons at equal frames; event note ids are timeline-wide instance numbers. Chance / velocity range: `noteRandom(seed ^ playbackPass, note id, pass)`: the playback pass counts transport starts and arrangement loop wraps (T3.17-fix3, M3 review M16), so each playback and loop repeat decides anew, and a seed + command sequence (an offline render) decides the same way every time. A note's off is at least one sample after its on (L4). Wrap, locate and stop release every sounding note at that frame. Sequence swap: sounding notes follow their track id; ones still in the image (same id, start) keep sounding with the new length — a changed pitch (Hz, PitchSource, ratio, harmonic) is retuned by a Pitch step at frame 0 carrying the new identity (`kNoteEventRetune`, T2.6-fix3), never cut — removed ones get a note-off at frame 0 of the first new block; cursors re-seek at the current tick (no chasing).
- **Automation**: `AutomationRenderer` renders each lane into an audio-rate buffer per block (incremental cursor; fast paths for constant / straight / curved segments under a constant tempo; exact per-sample `samplesToTicks` when a breakpoint falls inside a run or the tempo ramps; an all-constant block costs O(1)); matches `evalAutomation(points, samplesToTicks(s))` (`pq/core/automation_curve.h`, Q39, header-only, shared with the views) within 6e-8 relative. While stopped it holds the value at the playhead. `writeAutomatedParams(params)` writes frame-0 values to `params[target]`; `automation().buffer(lane)` is the audio-rate buffer. **Audio-rate consumers (T2.6-fix2, M3 review M6)**: `AutomationLaneDesc::tap` (a `const float**` the consumer owns, e.g. a tuning table's `dsp::TuningLiveInputs`) gets the lane's buffer each block while it moves and null while the block is one constant (the consumer then reads the param, which holds it); the old image's taps are nulled at a swap and in the timeline's destructor. Lanes without a tap and with `perSample = false` render only their frame-0 value (one value per lane per block: the project link sets this for every lane but the tuning ones, whose consumers read params); the param values are bit-identical to the per-sample render's frame 0.
- **Shifted lanes** (T6.2): `AutomationLaneDesc::shiftFrames` (a lane whose target unit is compensated by A_u): while playing, `writeAutomatedParams` writes the lane's value at frame 0's song sample minus the shift (one `evalAutomation` per shifted lane per block); lanes with a tap are not shifted.
- **Audio clips** (T5.4, `pq/engine/audio_sequence.h`, `clip_player.h`): `AudioSequenceImage::build(slots, samples, tempo, {sampleRate, edgeDeclick})` resolves each clip into song samples once (body `[ticksToSamples(start), ticksToSamples(start + length))`, the crossfade windows that extend it, fades, edge declicks, the flat middle where the envelope is the gain) and prepares the resampler tables its rates need; Off clips read `f(s) = anchor + (s − bodyStart) · step` in 32.32 (no accumulation: a render does not depend on the block size; reverse = the forward frames mirrored in the sample domain), RePitch clips map song samples to content ticks exactly at every block end (linear between, split at loop wraps). `Timeline::publishAudio` swaps it like the other images; the timeline's `ClipPlayer` plays it on the transport's runs (`play` per run, `releaseAll` + `seek` on stop / locate / loop wrap with 2 ms fades, `idle` while stopped), one voice per clip under the playhead (a streamed clip's voice exists `clipLookaheadSeconds` early and claims its stream then), straight copies at rate 1 from integer positions, else `dsp::sincResample`, the envelope fused into that pass; with no voice and the next clip beyond the run, a run costs one compare. A swap keeps a voice whose clip has the same id and source law (gain changes ramp over 2 ms) and fades the others out while the new clips under the playhead fade in; a newer image waits until those fades are over (hazards `cur` + the fading image). Streamed samples are read through `media::SampleCursor` into a per-voice history buffer; late frames read as silence (counted). Its outputs (`channels()`, `active()`) are `ProjectConfig::clipIn` / `clipActive`. T5.x-perf-audio: above rate 2 a resident pooled sample that has mips is read on level L = `dsp::mipLevelFor(rate, levels)` at rate / 2^L ≤ 2 (32.32 position and step shifted by L; `ClipPlayerStats::mipFrames`), so a transpose above an octave costs at most a +12 st read; `AudioSequenceImage::build` prepares the stretch tables of both the direct and the mip reads (`Clip::peakRate`: the rate without the tables' margin); `ProjectLink::publishAudio` asks its loader thread for the levels each resident sample's fastest clip needs (`requestMips`; `waitForSamples` and so `renderOffline` wait for them: a render reads the same frames whatever the timing). A looped RePitch voice on a streamed sample claims a second cursor at the loop's other end `clipLookaheadSeconds` before a wrap and adopts it at the wrap (`ClipPlayerStats::loopClaims` / `loopAdopts`; Q151 item 7: before, every wrap past the head claimed a fresh stream and underran).
- **Integration**: `ImageRunner::setBlockHook`, `RenderRequest::hook`, `HostConfig::hook` take a `BlockHook` (`Timeline::blockHook()`: advance the timeline, write the snapshot and automated params) called before the event source each image block; `Timeline::eventSource(k)` is the `EventSource` of track k's VoiceEngine (T3.17 wires N instruments, one per slot). `process(sampleTime, frames)` is idempotent per engine block, so the hook and every track's source can all trigger it. Offline renders through it are deterministic and bit-identical to the same notes as a `NoteListSource` (tested).

### Leaf-atom kernel ABI (`pq/dsp/kernel.h`, `pq/dsp/atoms.h`; T1.6)
The interpreter's op record is `{KernelFn, KernelArgs}`; the JIT reproduces the same semantics inline.
- `using KernelFn = void (*)(const KernelArgs&) noexcept;` with `KernelArgs { const float* const* in; float* const* out; void* state; const void* resource; uint32_t frames; uint16_t numIn, numOut; uint32_t splMask; }`.
- **Lanes**: each kernel is compiled for L = 1 (global signal) or L = 8 (one voice group). A block buffer holds `frames · L` floats, frame-major with voices interleaved (`[s·8 + v]`, one frame = one `__m256`). Global stateless kernels are vectorised over time.
- **Rates**: an input is a block buffer (audio rate) or a Spl pattern of 8 floats constant over the block (param rate; per-voice values for L = 8, 8 copies of the value for L = 1). The Spl mask is a template specialisation chosen by `findKernel(atom, lanes, splMask)`, so unmodulated inputs cost one vector load per block and no per-sample branch.
- **Bools** are 32-bit lane masks (all ones / zero) in float slots, for both L. Every select on a bool (float `select`, integer `selecti`, the JIT's selects) reads the sign bit of the lane (blendvps), never single bytes, so a non-mask value at an engine input (1.0f: sign clear, false) means the same in every kernel and in both backends (T1b.1-fix1, M1 review L5).
- **State**: one POD block per stateful node (`atom_state.h`, 32-byte aligned, 8 lanes even for L = 1), sized by `stateBytes(atom, StateInit)` (`size_t`; a delay ring is at most `kMaxDelayRingBytes` = 1 GiB, i.e. `maxDelayFramesLimit(lanes)` frames, and `schedule()` rejects a larger `max` with a located error; each state arena is capped at 2 GiB) and written by `initState` (no allocation). Its type tag for hot swap is the `Atom` id (plus the lane count for delay lines, whose ring follows the header).
- **Resources** (wavetables, shaper curves, sample data, tuning tables) are immutable tables built off-thread (`tables.h`, `tuning_table.h`) and passed by pointer. TMM.2 adds two that are not immutable tables: `Resource::FrameTable` (a `dsp::FrameTableSlot`, whose current table a writer swaps while images run: the kernel reads it per call, never caches it across calls) and `Resource::VoiceTap` (a `pq::VoiceTap` written by the audio thread); `engine::Resources::frameTables` / `voiceTaps` index them by the slot ids of the document lowering (`LowerOptions::metaSlotOf`), `resourceFor` falls back to a one-frame sine slot built for the image's sample rate (no default tap), and `ImageHost` gives every tap its voice ops write the block's lane masks (`tapMasks`, only while someone reads) and every voice-arena state its group (`StateInit::voiceGroup`, set per group like the noise seeds; not part of the JIT cache key: the generated code never reads it). Frame tables (`frame_table.h`, TMM.1) follow the same rule: a kernel receives `const FrameTableView*` as its resource, keeps a `FrameReadState` (32-byte aligned, 64 bytes) in its state block, and calls `readFrameTableL1` (L = 1, vectorised over time) or `readFrameTableL8` with `positionPerSample = !splBit(mask, position input)`; both are `noexcept`, allocation-free and bit-exact against `readFrameTableReferenceBlock` within 1e-6 (TMM.12-fix1: a sine table's sine path is within 3.4e-7 of the exact sine and so within the rows' own 1.6e-6). TMM.12-fix1: inside a per-sample loop the JIT calls the `wavetable_pos` kernel at a block's first frame and computes the following frames inline as the readers' sine path while the kernel leaves `WavetablePosState::sineOn` set (a sine table, no crossfade, no tap reader, every lane on one frame; the lanes' coefficients beside it), else calls it per frame; such a stage may get a pair function.
- **Tuning node** (T2.6, SPEC §3.3; `pq/dsp/tuning_table.h`, `pq/engine/tuning_signal.h`): `tuning_hz(degree, equave, hz, cents, generator, period, stretch, reference, table) → (hz, cents)`. The resource (`TuningTableView`, built by `engine::makeTuningTable(tuning)` from `tuning::linearTuningForm`; `Resources::tunings`, both backends) holds the EDO / MOS form per constant-order interval: pure cents = K·g − M·P, stretched by (1200 + stretch)/1200, Hz = reference · 2^(cents/1200), all in double (k·g − m·P reaches 10^4 ¢; float32 would cost ~1e-3 ¢) and rounded once. Per lane (K, M) stay cached until the generator leaves its interval (a region crossing, found at that sample) or the degree changes; with every tuning input a param the pitch is computed once per lane per block and the per-sample work is a blend. A lane without a degree, or whose `hz` input is 0 (before its onset), passes `hz` / `cents` through. Unmodulated param inputs equal to float32 of the definition's double value read the double (within an ulp of the static resolved pitch); when every tuning param input of a block is at its definition value the lane passes its own `hz` (the note's static Hz) through and computes only `cents` (T2.6-fix1: an unmodulated tuning node renders bit-identically to static pitches, and skips the exp2). **T2.6-fix2**: the table is a shared `dsp::TuningShape` (the interval table of a chain length: bounds, unrotated (k, m), the inverse map, and per boundary buildMOSScale's degenerate window 1e-6/q ¢ and hint-less nudge side; `engine::makeTuningShape(key)`) plus the tuning's definition (`dsp::TuningDefinition`: periods per equave, the mode as the root's chain position `rootChain`, the region hint, the values). Exact boundary generators take the side buildMOSScale's `chainSafeGenerator` nudges to (the region hint, else the side with fewer step sizes) and the 1e-7 ¢ nudge itself (H3); the mode is carried across intervals like the explorer's `transferModeRotation` (rotation in interval t = `inv[t][rootChain]`, `tuning::MosLinearTable::carryRotation`, shared with the piano roll's morph and `SetTuningParam`'s generator edit) (M7); `TuningTableView::live` (`dsp::TuningLiveInputs`, written each block by the timeline through `AutomationLaneDesc::tap`) replaces a param input by its automation buffer while the lane moves, so automation reaches the node per sample (M6). The kernel reads `KernelArgs::splMask` at run time (one function per lane count; the JIT calls it). **T2.6-fix3** (M3 review H4, H5, M9): two more inputs, `select` (voice.tuning) and `node`: with node ≥ 1 a lane is this node's only if select == node, and a selected lane without a degree is reference-relative (`hz · ref / ref₀`); a Degree lane's output is `ref · 2^(tc/1200) · E` with `E = hz / hz₀` (hz₀ = the degree's pitch at the table's definition; E = 1 within 4e-7, the note's own static Hz) — a Ratio anchored (through notes) to a Degree note carries the root's degree and sounds at its ratio to it, and a glide composes; `.cents` = the input cents + (tc − tc₀) + 1200·log2(ref/ref₀) (the engine's cents moved by the tuning; the reference term per block). Unmodulated (every param input at the definition) the node copies its inputs. A track patch binds it as `t = tuning_hz(voice.degree, voice.equave, voice.hz, voice.cents, gen, per, st, ref, table: k)`, `tv = tune_voices(t.hz, t.cents)` (see infer above): the instrument's voice.hz derives from the tuning signal. voice.degree / voice.equave are param-rate voice signals (VoiceSignal::Degree / Equave).
- **Silence declarations** (T1.x-silence): `dsp::silenceInfo(atom)` / `dsp::silenceTail(atom, init)`, see "Silence and sleep" under the ProgramImage ABI.
- **Registry ↔ kernel**: `pq/graph/kernel_map.h` (`kernelMapping(AtomId)`) is the one mapping from language atoms to kernels: the kernel, and per registry input whether it is a kernel input (durations: `· sr` inserted by `optimise()`, after which `TypedPatch::durationsInSamples` is set), a resource, or a state-init const (`seed`, `max` delay, z1 `init`). Atoms without a kernel are marked Structural (`channel`, `pack`), Constant (`sr`) or Engine (`voice.*`, `transport.*`, I/O, `voice_*` reducers). Registry `stateBytes` = the kernel's state block. Tested against both tables (QUESTIONS Q14).
- **Feedback**: `z1` and the delay lines also have split kernels (`z1:read`/`z1:write`, `delay*:read`/`delay:write`) sharing the atom's state, so a feedback SCC scheduled per sample can read at the start of the sample and write at the end.
- **Filters** (EQ.1; `pq/dsp/svf_design.h`, kernels_svf.cpp): `svf(x, cutoff, q, gain; mode, stages, prewarp)` — `stages` (1 … 64; bell, notch: 1) cascaded TPT state-variable sections, each with an output mix over hp / bp / lp / x, so all eight modes (lowpass, highpass, bandpass, notch, bell, lowshelf, highshelf, allpass) are one recursion per section (svf_fast.pqp's shallow form). `gain` is a linear amplitude; mode / stages / prewarp are consts (an `atom:svf` block's settings). Prewarp `matched` (default): impulse-invariant poles, numerator magnitude-matched (Vicanek 2016; shelves: a least-squares |B|² fit), written back as SVF g / k / mix; `bilinear`: plain Simper. lowpass / highpass / shelves: Butterworth-spread section Qs (the user's Q scales the highest), interleaved low / high Q to keep partial gains small; shelves gain / N per section. One designer, `svfDesign` (double, RT-safe, no allocation), and its exact `svfResponse` (from the float coefficients the kernel runs, through the recursion's state space) — the response evaluator (`graph::LinearResponse`) uses it as the atom's response rule; `svfParamsFromInputs` is the kernel's input → design conversion. Per lane the coefficients are the exact design of its inputs whenever they rest (param rate: one design per change; a Blk call whose inputs are constant runs the param-rate cascade); EQ.1b: inputs moving on a line (the compiler's 20 ms param ramps, linear sweeps) run RAMP SEGMENTS — designs at the inputs half a segment and a segment ahead on the line, the six kernel coefficients of every section following the quadratic through the three by forward differences while each frame's inputs stay on the predicted line (2e−6), the length ≤ 32 frames and bounded by the relative step of tan(π·fc/sr), q and gain (≤ 0.1 per segment), before an input clamp and a frame before a gain reaches 0 dB (the identity form) — ≤ −90 dB from a design per frame on 20 ms sweeps (20 Hz ↔ 20 kHz included), the exact design again when they stop; curved audio-rate inputs design per frame (costly with matched, cheaper with bilinear); a voice group whose lanes share their inputs designs once (lane follows lane); gain is ignored by the modes without one. ≤ 16 sections run in float; 17 … 64 in double (a deep cascade's float rounding noise is amplified by the sections after it), each double state's 32-bit halves in the float state's field and the next, so every state word stays per lane.
