State: `SvfState` sized by the sections its mode / stages consts give (`StateInit::svfSections`, `StateTag::extra`; 896 B for a 1-section global filter, 1.5 KiB per voice group; 64 sections of 8 lanes: 44.8 KiB, more than EQ.1's fixed 24.8 KiB: the ramp differences), every word per lane (`LaneState::Whole`: shared values replicated). Silence: a Memory atom over x whose history decays (`dsp::hasDecayState`): the sleep plan checks it as `SleepStateKind::Decay` (every section's states ≤ ε, `dsp::decayStateSmall`, then flushed). The stdlib patch with every SVF response as an output is `svf_multi` (filters/svf.pqp).

### One hierarchy (GU.9 – GU.11; docs/plans/GU.md "One hierarchy")
The project is tracks of BLOCKS (`model.h` Device; checks.cpp refuses anything else, on load and in the store):
- **group** (`kGroupDevice`) — the one container. Its members name it (`Device::group`, nesting ≤ kMaxGroupDepth); its
  In / Out terminals are ports of the group; wires name blocks, never containers. Optional panel (`pq/document/panel.h`:
  elements bound to controls, settings and engine data inside it, any depth). The outermost group fed notes is an
  INSTRUMENT GROUP (everything inside runs per note; polyphony = its settings). A switched-off group switches off what
  is inside it (lowering, voice contexts).
- **atom block** (`atom:<name>`) — one atom of the registry that may stand alone (`devices.h atomBlockAllowed`), lowered
  inline; its inputs are wired, a const input is a setting.
- **value block** (`kValueDevice`) — one control: its knob position through its curve (GU.1) is its output.
- **native engine** (`meta:<type>`) — a meta type whose expansion is real DSP (Wavetable, Envelope, Oscilloscope,
  Spectrum, Spectrogram): a leaf with a face, state in `Device::meta` (path "" only), its main input its type's chain
  input. No inside.
Everything else is built from these: the modules (`modules.h`: Phase Accumulator, VCA, the engines' groups, Utility,
Saturator, LFO, Operator, the Basic Synth default instrument, the Wavetable Synth — group presets made in code, pasted by
`ops::addDevice("module:<key>")` onto the path or `pasteBlocks` off it), racks (parallel groups derived from wiring,
`racks.h`; the Device view draws them as racks), user presets (`.pqpreset` block sets). The patch language is the
stdlib's atom / function library and the lowering's output — not a container: there is no stock patch, no patch text of
a device's own, no per-device circuit editor (the circuit editor opens loose `.pqp` files only).
`DeviceLibrary` (`devices.h`) is the fixed block patches (group variants, value, one per atom) plus the meta prelude;
`patchOf` / `infoOf` describe a block. **Views**: one panel system — a group is drawn as its panel (`GroupPanelFace`) in
the circuit, the Device view and the panel window; GU.13: a leaf in the Device view is a panel of the same system — a
synthetic `doc::Panel` (`pq/views/panel/block_frame.h` `leafPanel`: its engine module's panel, else one control element
per param / setting) through `makeDocPanelTarget`, in a `BlockFrame` (jacks, title bar, activator, fold, preset slot,
rename); a rack's macro panel is a `BlockFrame` too (its own `PanelTarget`). Every panel's controls behave alike: the
elements take the device-panel gestures where the host allows them (`FacePointer::valueGestures`: `PanelHost` sets it,
the circuit canvas does not — its wheel pans), the Device view visits every frame's `PanelHost`s for its drop targets,
automation LEDs, live modulation rings (`PanelTarget::setModulation`), the envelope at the playhead
(`PanelTarget::showPosition`), map mode and the control menu (Show Automation, Knob Curve); a document change repaints
only the elements whose `Element::liveKey` changed. `DevicePanel` / `PanelLayout` are deleted. In the circuit an atom is
its symbol, a value block a knob, an engine its face. Opening a block opens a group (a leaf has no inside). Retired code: `retired/`.
**Older bullets below**: wherever one still names stock devices, user devices (`Device::source`), panel descriptions
(`.panel.json`), `Device::chainInput`, the device preview or the project-device circuit editor, that part is gone
(GU.10 / GU.11; retired/README.md lists the files); so is any `DevicePanel`, `PanelLayout`, `facePanel` / `autoPanel`,
`DeviceTarget` or `face_display.cpp` (GU.13: deleted; the leaf's frame is a `panel::BlockFrame`).

### Document model (`pq/document/*.h`, T3.4)
- `Snapshot = shared_ptr<const Project>`. `Project`: timeline (tempo + time-signature points and their built maps), main tunings (`TuningSlot`: name, colour, `TuningDef` = EDO | MOS params | `ScaleDef` (Scala .scl + optional .kbm) + reference Hz), locators, loop, track tree (`tracks`, Midi + nested Group), `returns`, `master`. `Track`: kind, name, colour, `TuningAssignment` (Inherit | Main | Private), `Mixer` (volume dB, pan, mute, solo, arm, non-default sends, output), active, children, clips (`ClipSlot {id, ClipPtr}` sorted by start), automation lanes (`ParamRef` + breakpoints). `NoteClip`: start, length, content start, loop, signature, tuning, active, `ClipFundamental` (T3.6-fix1), `NoteSeq`. **Virtual fundamental** (T3.6-fix1, Q40): stored = `ClipFundamental { steps: HarmonicStep {note, denominator, ratio} sorted by note id, lock: optional Rational }`; derived on read = `doc::virtualFundamental(clip[, ownDenominators])`: the lock, else 1/D with D = lcm over the reference-anchored Ratio notes of their effective denominators (a step's denominator while the note still has the ratio that step produced, else its own; D > 2^20 = none). `ops::transposeNotes` / `moveNotes` step those notes on F' = gcd(note, F) and write the step memory in the same Batch (`SetClipFundamental`, Meta); `ops::lockFundamental(clip, note, N)` = note / N, `unlockFundamental`. Ratios stay reduced and exact; undo, redo and the project file carry the memory (JSON `"fundamental"` on a clip, written only when set). `Note`: 64 bytes (start, length, `PitchId`, id, velocity, release, chance, velocity range, flags).
- Every shareable node is `shared_ptr<const T>`; an edit path-copies from the changed node to the root, everything else stays pointer-equal. `NoteSeq` stores notes sorted by `(start, id)` in immutable chunks of ≤ 128 notes, so a one-note edit copies one chunk.
- `Command` = variant of ~50 primitive structs + `Batch`; `apply(snapshot, command, ChangeSet&) → Result<Snapshot>` is pure (ids pre-allocated from the store's `IdSource`), returns the input pointer for a no-op. T3.6 builds higher-level edits as Batches.
- Edit operations (`pq/document/edit_ops.h`, T3.6, namespace `pq::doc::ops`): pure builders (snapshot + selection + parameters + the store's `IdSource`) → `Edit { Batch command; selection after }`, one history entry each; views call these instead of composing primitives. Track, clip, note, identity-aware Up/Down (`stepPitch`), time-selection (insert/delete/duplicate time) and automation edits. Helpers: `clip_time.h` (where clip content plays: loops, start marker; its tick math is `pq/core/clip_time.h` since T0/T1-lows, shared with the engine's SequenceImage, M3 review L16) and `automation_curve.h` (integer-tick queries over the breakpoint curve law, which lives in `pq/core/automation_curve.h` since T3.16: shared with the engine and the views; Q39).
- Identities and main tunings (T3.19, `edit_ops.h`): `convertNotes` also converts Degree / Cents → Ratio = `simplestRatioNear(cents, ratioSnapTolerance(tuning, cents))` (the smallest-denominator ratio within 0.45 × the smaller neighbouring step, clamped to [0.05, 20] ¢, so Degree → Ratio → Degree is the identity; `simplestRatioBetween` walks the continued fraction of the interval in exact integers); `setNotesPitch` (a typed identity); `addMainTuning` (one AddTuning, `Edit::tunings` = its id) and `removeMainTuning(tuning, replacement)` (SetTrackTuning / SetClipTuning of every Main user to the replacement, then RemoveTuning: one step). `Edit` gained `tunings`.
- Mute / solo (`pq/document/audibility.h`, T3.10): `computeAudibility(project)` → per track an output gate and a sends gate (Ableton's Exclusive Solo + Solo in Place; QUESTIONS Q89), `effectiveAudible(project, track)` = the output gate. Pure and O(tracks); in `document` so T3.17's lowering and the views share it. Mixer builders in `edit_ops.h`: `toggleTracksMute`, `soloTrack(additive)`, `armTrack(additive)`, `setReturnSendsPreFader`.
- `ChangeSet`: touched ids + class flags `Meta | Param | Sequence | Automation | Timeline | Structural` (`paramOnly()`, `sequenceOnly()`, …) and the changed `ParamRef`s; drives T3.17 (recompile only on Structural).
- `DocumentStore` (UI thread): current snapshot, `dispatch`, `History` (snapshot pairs; undo/redo swap pointers), gestures (`begin/commit/cancelGesture`: live notifications, one history entry), listeners (`StoreEvent`), and `ViewState` (fold/heights, not undoable; Q26; T4.13: `patchNudges`, the project patch view's manual nudges by track or device id, saved as the view's optional `"patch"` array, written only when not empty; TX.resize: `PatchNudge::scale`, a device block's uniform scale `kMinPatchScale` … `kMaxPatchScale`, the entry's optional `"scale"`, written only when not 1, outside the range a load error; no schema bump, like the nudges themselves).
- **Audio tracks and audio clips** (T5.3, `model.h` "Samples and audio clips", `audio_clip.h`, `audio_sequence.h`): `TrackKind::Audio` (main tree, groupable; its chain holds audio effects only; sends, routing, solo / mute and audibility as a MIDI track's; `Ctrl+T` = `ops::createTrack(Audio)`). `ClipSlot { id, clip, audio }` holds exactly one of a `NoteClip` (MIDI tracks) or an `AudioClip` (audio tracks), with `start() / length() / end() / active() / name() / colour()` for the edits that treat both alike: `ops::` delete, move / Alt-copy (only between tracks of one kind), resize, split, duplicate and the time edits run on both through one `ClipEditor` (working list of slots; `bounded` / `split` / `moved` per kind); consolidate refuses audio clips (it renders). `AudioClip { start, length, sample, warp ∈ {Off, RePitch}, contentStartFrame + seconds (Off), contentStart + loop + sourceBpm (RePitch: a note clip's content-tick rules, content tick 0 = source frame 0), gainDb, transpose (Cents | Steps of the clip's tuning, resolved when lowered | Ratio), detuneCents, reverse (non-destructive mirror), fadeIn / fadeOut {length, curve}, crossfadeIn, tuning, active }`. **Warp Off keeps its duration in seconds** (plan Q1): `length` is derived (`syncedClip`: the nearest tick to τ(start) + seconds) and every command keeps it in sync — `SetTempoMap` re-derives every Off clip (ChangeSet Sequence besides Timeline) — so all generic edits use `start + length`. Source frames keep their absolute time through every trim / split / move (fractional `contentStartFrame`; tested through `sourceFrameAt`). Commands: `AddClip { track, clip | audio }`, `SetAudioClip` (replace one audio clip whole: every audio-specific edit; Sequence: never a compile), `MoveClip` / `RemoveClip` / `RenameClip` / `SetClipColour` / `SetClipActive` for both kinds, `SetClipTuning` on an audio clip = Sequence; the crossfade of a clip whose left neighbour no longer ends at its start is dropped at the end of the command that broke it (`apply()`'s final pass over the touched audio tracks, so a Batch may separate and rejoin clips). Builders in `edit_ops.h` "Audio clips": `addAudioClips` (new samples + clips, overwriting what lies under them, one step), gain / transpose / detune / reverse / warp / fades / crossfade / Re-Pitch loop.
- **Project samples** (T5.3 table, T5.8 management; `samples.h`): `Project::samples` = `SampleRef { id, path (UTF-8 '/'; project-relative or absolute), contentHash, size, frames, rate, channels, displayName }` sorted by id; `AddSample` / `RemoveSample` (refused while played) / `SetSample` (Meta). Files are referenced where they are (user answer 3, 2026-10-30): `makeSampleRef` (the app's `SampleProbeFn` gives frames / rate / channels: media's probe once T5.1 lands) stores a path relative to the project when inside it. `fileIdentity` = size + FNV-1a 64 over the size and three 64 KiB windows. `resolveSamples` (stored path, then a recursive search of the project folder and `SampleSearch::folders` by name, verified by size and hash) → `SampleResolution { path, relocated }`; `missingSamples`; `relinkSamples(folder)`; `planCollect` / `runCollect` / `collectedRefs` / `collectAllAndSave` (every used file from outside the project into `samples/imported/`: a taken name with the same size + hash is reused, another gets " (2)" …; each copy to `.part`, flushed, renamed; `project.pqd` last). Path changes are not undo steps: `DocumentStore::relocateSamples` rewrites the entries in the current snapshot and every history snapshot (shared snapshots stay shared) and notifies `SamplesRelocated`. The `ViewContext` resolves on open / when the table changes, relocates what moved (a clean project stays clean), keeps the missing set (status line "N samples missing — Relink…"), and runs `file.collectAllAndSave` (the copy on a worker thread, progress and the final relocate + save posted back through the app's `setUiPoster`: an SDL user event) and `file.relinkSamples` (`FileDialogs::chooseFolder`).
- **Recording model** (T6.1, docs/plans/M6.md; `model.h` "Recording inputs", "Note input", "Take lanes", AudioWarp / WarpMarker / WarpParams, PunchRegion, RecordSettings; `commands.h` "Recording inputs", "Take lanes"; `edit_ops.h` (takes, comping, warp markers); `pq/document/input_map.h`; `lower.h` "Signal flow": Audio). *Mixer*: `arm` (MIDI and audio tracks; **Param** `ParamKind::TrackArm`), `monitor` Auto | In | Off (**Param** `TrackMonitor`), `audioIn` {firstChannel, channels 0 none | 1 | 2} (audio tracks; `SetTrackAudioInput` **Structural**), `midiIn` {All | Port name | None, channel 0 all | 1…16} (MIDI tracks; Meta), `trackDelayMs` ±1000 (Meta until T6.6). *Monitor gate*: an audio track with an input lowers `x = gain(clips, 1 − monitor) + gain(track.in, monitor)` with the runtime param "<id>:monitor" = `monitorGate(mixer)` (In 1, Off 0, Auto = arm; T6.7's engine refines Auto with the transport); `LoweredProject::audioInputs` lists the device channels the engine binds to `track.in`; without an input nothing is lowered. *Note input* (`Track::noteInput` {Keys | Hz, InputKeyMap Tuning (.kbm, else consecutive from key 60) | Consecutive | TwelveTetNearest | TwelveTetHz}, Meta): `buildInputPitchMap(project, track[, input])` → an immutable 128-entry `InputPitchMap` (one indexed load per key, `at()` RT-safe; `atNoteOn(key, bendCents)` freezes a bend in Hz mode; `pitchForDegree(d)` for the computer keyboard), every entry resolved by `resolvePitch` (pitch_resolve.h), the same per-identity path `resolveTrackPitches` uses, so a live note equals the drawn note (Hz, PitchFollow, signal, degree, noteDegree); Hz mode stores `freezeHz` Cents identities. *Take lanes*: `Track::takes` (`TakeLane {LaneId, name, clips}`, MIDI and audio tracks), `Track::auditionTake` (invalid: the main lane), `playedClips(track)` = what `resolveTrackPitches` / `lowerAudioTrack` (and the app's note lowering) read — auditioning is a Sequence change, an unheard take is never lowered; `ClipSlot::take` = comp provenance (display only). Commands `AddTakeLane` / `RemoveTakeLane` / `RenameTakeLane` (Meta; removing the auditioned take is Sequence), `SetAuditionTake`, `PromoteTake` (swap with the main lane; Sequence). `ops::compRegion(track, take, [t0, t1), crossfade)` = clearRange on the main lane + bounded copies of the take's clips (fresh ids; every note / source frame at its absolute time) + crossfades at the region's edges, one step; time edits move take lanes (a changed lane is replaced whole) and the punch region with the track. Sample-in-use, tempo re-sync of Off clips, removal ids and `maxEntityId` include take lanes. *Warp*: `AudioWarp` += Beats, Tones, Texture, Complex, ComplexPro (`isWarped`: content ticks like RePitch; `warpTransposes`: every mode but RePitch plays the transpose — `audioPitchRatio`, `AudioClipDesc::pitchRatio`); `AudioClip::markers` (`WarpMarker {sourceFrame, content}`, strictly increasing) and `warpParams`; `warpFrameAt` / `warpContentAt` (audio_clip.h; RT-safe) = the piecewise-linear law the lowering's `sourceFrameAt` and `withWarp` use (no markers: the implied segment at sourceBpm); `ops::add/move/removeWarpMarker` keep every other marker's song time, `ops::setWarpMode` keeps placement and the start's frame. The engines for the new modes are T6.3 / T6.5 (until then the app plays every warped mode as RePitch). *Project*: `punch` {in, out, enabled}, `record` {countInBars 0/1/2/4, metronome, metronomeDb, overdub, recordQuantize} (`SetPunch` / `SetRecordSettings`, Meta).
- Project files (`pq/document/serialize.h`, T3.5; schema 22 since T6.1: the recording model's keys (Recording model above), each written only when not its default, 21 → 22 changes nothing; schema 21 since GU.3: a group device may carry "panel" (Group panels below), 20 → 21 changes nothing; schema 15 since TX.groups-fix1: a wire may carry "chain": true (a grouped series' chain link), 14 → 15 changes nothing; schema 14 since TX.groups: a device whose "patch" is "Group" is a group, a device may carry "group": <group id> (a member), 13 → 14 changes nothing; schema 13 since TX.patching: a wire without "law" adds in its target's units and has no "amount", "law": "travel" + "amount" is the migrated knob-travel law (12 → 13 gives every wire "law": "travel": old projects lower to the same graph), a device may carry "free": true; schema 12 since TMM.10-fix2: a wire may carry "notes" (a MIDI track's note source) instead of "from" (11 → 12 changes nothing); 11 since TMM.10-fix1: a meta device's "chainInput", meta kinds by position (10 → 11 unplugs a meta audio effect that was first where the note source arrives); 10 since TMM.3: meta modules; 9 since T4.18: device lanes; schema 8 since T4.17: device names; 7 since T5.3: audio tracks, audio clips, the sample table; 6 since T4.8: racks; 5 since T4.4-fix1: user devices' texts): `serializeProject`/`parseProject` (text) and `saveProject`/`loadProject`/`openProject` (directory, UTF-8 paths, atomic temp + flush + `MoveFileExW`). Snapshot + `ViewState`, ids preserved; strict reader with JSON-path errors; `Migration` table (n → n+1) on an nlohmann DOM for old files.
- Main tunings, resolution (`pq/document/pitch_resolve.h`, T2.7): `effectiveTuningRef(project, path, clip)` = the effective `TuningDef` (Inherit: clip → track → parent groups → the first main tuning) plus which main tuning it is (invalid for a private one); `resolveTrackPitches(project, track)` = per clip and note the static Hz (double, `resolveHzDouble`; Ratio anchors to other notes of the clip followed, memoised, ≤ 8 links, cycles and missing anchors unresolved) and, for a Degree note in a main tuning with a linear form (`hasTuningSignal`), the tuning signal it follows (→ `engine::PitchSource`). Reassignment or a tuning edit is re-resolved by calling it again on the new snapshot.
- **Meta-module resources in the link** (TMM.2; `pq/app/project_link.h` "Meta-module resources"): `ProjectLink` answers `metaSlotOf` with a stable id per (device, name path, request index) from a fixed table of 4096 (0 = none: the engine default), and after each lowering creates the requested object for every listed id (a `FrameTableSlot` holding a one-frame sine at the link's rate, or a `VoiceTap` sized for the tracks' polyphony with one record per engine block), fills `Resources::frameTables` / `voiceTaps`, and frees the ids the lowering no longer lists (FIFO reuse; the objects stay until reuse, and every image holds what it reads through `keepAlive`). `metaFrameTable` / `metaVoiceTap` hand them to the UI (faces read taps through `VoiceTapReader`), `publishFrameTable` swaps a table in through the link's reclaimer (the timeline's audio thread is a reader) without a compile; `setSampleRate` republishes the defaults at the new rate. TMM.5: the link builds the instances' own tables — each frame-table slot remembers its instance (type, device, path, request) and the key of the job it last asked for; `refreshMetaTables` (after every compile, on MetaData changes and on device / meta param changes: a baked knob goes to the builder, its runtime param is never read) derives the job from the newest snapshot the link was given (`metaDoc_`; data hashed once per value) and submits it to the link's `meta::TableBuilder` when the key changed; a finished table is published if the slot still wants that key (else dropped). `renderOffline` waits for pending builds (`waitForMetaTables`), so renders are deterministic; `metaTableStats`. TMM.13-fix1: a Reset (New / Open) drops every meta slot at the next compile (H2: device ids restart per project); data is hashed outside the link's mutex (M11); `metaGeneration()` bumps whenever the slots change, so readers look a slot up again only then (M12). The faces' dots in the app: `pq/app/face_voices.h` `FaceVoiceHub` (TMM.13-fix1 M12: readers of instances without a slot are parked — no tap or table kept, not polled — and woken when it has one again) — one `views::TapVoices` per instance over `metaVoiceTap(device, path, 0)` (request 0's tap is what a type's face shows), bound by the shell into `ProjectPatchView::faceVoices`; the shell wakes the faces after a presented frame when a reader got records (`anyFresh`), and the view wakes them on each playhead refresh while playing.
- **Meta modules** (TMM.3; `model.h` "Meta modules", `pq/document/meta.h`, `lower.h` "Meta modules"; docs/plans/MM.md): `Device::meta` (`MetaMapPtr`, null when empty) maps a NAME PATH — the call binding names from the device's root patch ("acc1", "opA.acc"), or "" for a chain-level meta device (`Device::patch` = "meta:<type>") — to `MetaInstance{type, version, params, settings, data}`. A chain device keeps its params / settings as the device's own (automation, macros, wires as for any device); a nested instance keeps them in the instance, its params runtime params "dev<id>:<path>.<name>" (`ParamKind::MetaParam`, not automatable in MM). A nested call without an entry plays the type's default state; editing a module's state inside a stock device does not fork it. Commands `AddMetaInstance` / `RemoveMetaInstance` (classified by what they carry: settings → Structural, params → Param, data → MetaData), `RenameMetaInstance` (Structural; moves everything below the path), `SetMetaParam` (Param; path "" = the device's own param), `SetMetaSetting` (Structural), `SetMetaData` (the new change class **MetaData**, `ChangeSet::metaData` lists the instances: never a compile). Builders: `ops::addMetaDevice`, `setMetaParam` / `setMetaSetting` / `setMetaData` (a fresh call's default state added in the same step, so its first drag is a param write), the patch editor's `addMetaCall` / `renameMetaCall` / `removeMetaCall`, and `editDevicePatchKeepingMeta` (a hand edit that renames a meta binding keeps its state: `metaRenames` over the NodeIds `ParseOptions::previous` carried). TMM.5: `metaParamBakedOnly` — a chain meta device's bakeable param of a type without a live path cannot get an automation lane, a modulation wire or a macro mapping (the commands and the file reader refuse them with the reason); TMM.8: the Wavetable has a live path (`Type::liveBakeable`), so its fold / warp / sync take lanes, wires and macros except in Shape mode (`Type::bakedOnly` gives the reason); `LoweredProject::MetaResource::modulated` carries the modulated params to the link, whose table jobs then leave them unbaked; `ops::addMetaDevice` starts an effect Wavetable in Shape mode. Schema 10: a device's "meta" object (data as JSON values, float arrays as `{"$f32": base64}`), a type's older state version upgraded on load, a newer one refused. The lowering expands chain meta devices and specialises device roots whose circuits call meta modules (`<root>__m<id>_<hash>` calling `meta_<type>__d<id>_<path>_<key>`); `LoweredProject::metaResources` lists the resource slots (`LowerOptions::metaSlotOf`) with their data for the link. `DeviceLibrary` parses stock files and user texts with the prelude's signatures and caches each device's meta call sites.
- **Meta devices in chains** (TMM.10, TMM.10-fix1; `pq/document/meta.h` "a chain is wires", `lower.h` "Meta modules", `edit_ops.h` `addMetaDevice` / `setDeviceChainInput`; docs/plans/MM.md "Chain and devices"; SPEC §15 2026-11-01 "notes are a signal"; QUESTIONS Q?-TMM.10-a, Q?-TMM.10-fix1-a). **No type is an instrument or an effect**: every meta type has one CHAIN INPUT (`meta::Type::chainInput`, like a device's audio `in`: the Wavetable's `x`, the Envelope's `in`; a device may plug its chain into another input or unplug it, `doc::Device::chainInput` "" / input name / "-", command `SetDeviceChainInput`, the Device view's context menu "Chain Input"), and each device's chain input takes the previous active stage's output; the first stage of a MIDI track's own chain (and of an Instrument Rack chain) takes the NOTE SOURCE. `doc::chainStages(list, notes, voices)` is the one rule: what reaches each active device (`ChainSignal` Notes / Voices / Audio / None) and what it gives — a meta device fed by the notes or by a per-voice signal runs per voice (Notes → `meta::Form::ChainNotes`: its chain input reads the note's signal of its meaning, `meta::Reads` Frequency `voice.hz` / Phase `phasor(voice.hz)` / Gate / Signal: none; Voices → `ChainVoice`, a per-voice `in`), audio → `ChainAudio` (a global `in`), unplugged → `ChainAudio` with no `in` (it runs once, freely). Any other device gives audio: a per-voice signal reaching a stock device is summed at its global input, and a stock instrument's voices are summed at its output (Live's instrument; the one remaining rule, Q?-TMM.10-fix1-a item 2). Polyphony therefore follows the wires: a MIDI track plays voices when its first active device is a stock instrument or a meta device the note source reaches; the meta devices after it that the per-voice signal reaches form a VOICE RUN, which the lowering wires in a row, ends with a 5 ms linear release after note-off (`env = gate ? 1 : max(z1(env) − 1/(0.005·sr), 0)`; the track's `done` = !gate && env ≤ 0) and sums before the first other device; TMM.11: when the run's last stage is of a type with `meta::Type::voiceEnd` (the Envelope, a VCA on the run's signal), its `done` output is the track's `done` and there is no declick ramp. TX.scope-sound: "last stage" means the last one that is not a pass-through (a device whose first `out` is one of its inputs unchanged: the analysers, a switched-off device; `passThroughInput` in lower.cpp): the lowering walks back over pass-throughs — along the chain, along the one wire into a free one's input, through a group's Out and In — to the device whose output the run's signal is, whose type decides the voice end and whose callee the width, so an analyser never changes the sound (`lower.h` "Meta modules", Q?-TX.scope-sound-a). A meta device's KIND is only its position (`model.h` "Meta modules": Instrument exactly when it is first where instruments go; `normaliseMetaKinds` in AddDevice / RemoveDevice / MoveDevice, the 10 → 11 migration), never what it plays. `addMetaDevice` puts any type at any slot of any track: first where instruments go it replaces a stock instrument (which takes no chain signal, so it could not follow) and pushes a meta device on; elsewhere it receives the previous device's signal (an audio track's Wavetable: its phase from the audio). Inside a rack chain only the first stage takes the notes; later ones receive audio (Q?-TMM.10-fix1-a item 5). The prelude has every chain form per type (`Prelude::Entry::notes` / `audio` / `voice`, `chain()`).
- `document` depends on `tuning`, `core`, `graph`, `meta` (TMM.3) and nlohmann/json (migrations only). The audio thread never sees it.

### Widget toolkit (ui, T3.1: `pq/ui/widget.h`, `input.h`, `actions.h`, `test_driver.h`)
- **Tree**: `UiTree` owns one root `Widget`; widgets own their children (`add<W>(...)`). Ids are paths of names (`arrangement/track[3]/header/mute`; unnamed widgets are transparent). Override `paint`/`paintOver`, `onPointer`/`onKey`/`onText`, `onHoverChanged`/`onFocusChanged`/`onCaptureLost`, `onFrame`/`onTimer`, `contentExtent`, `arrange`.
- **Layout**: one top-down pass, no measure pass. `Length` = Fixed px | Flex weight, with min/max; containers are Row / Column / Stack / Custom (`Splitter`, `ScrollView`, views). Lengths are **logical px**; rects are **device px** = round(logical × `UiTree::uiScale()`), so text stays at integer pixel sizes. Only dirty subtrees are revisited.
- **HiDPI (`pq/ui/ui_scale.h`, T0.7-fix3)**: `UiScale` is the one logical → device conversion (`px`, `rect` snap each edge; `exact` for AA geometry; `fontPx(role) = round(role × scale)`). Source of truth: the app reads `SDL_GetWindowDisplayScale` at startup and on `SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_DISPLAY_SCALE_CHANGED` / `DISPLAY_CHANGED` (or `--scale`), then pushes it to `UiTree::setViewport` and `setSceneScale` (which re-resolves the `FontRole`s; new sizes are created there, never in paint); widgets read `PaintContext::scale` and `res.fonts`, scenes read `SceneTextures::scale`. Contract: layout and positions in logical px through `UiScale`; text through font roles (`TextSystem::font(FontRole, UiScale)`), never a hard-coded px size; the draw list, `lines.h` line widths, AA feathers and hairlines are device px and never read the scale; `TextSystem` measures and draws in device px. Pointer events are device px. `pqdaw --screenshot … --size WxH --scale S` renders W×H logical px at round(W·S)×round(H·S) device px.
- **Dense content** (clips, notes, grid) is drawn by one widget inside a `ScrollView`: it reports `contentExtent()` and paints only `PaintContext::clip`. Never one widget per note.
- **Redraw**: `markDirty()` adds the widget's clipped rect to the tree's `Damage`; layout adds the old and new rects of whatever moved. `requestFrame()` / `requestTimer()` are the only ways to animate; idle means no requests, and the loop blocks until the next event or `nextWakeTime()`. **Partial redraw (T0.7-fix1; per rect since T3.x-fix-paint)**: the frame loop reads the damage after layout (`UiTree::damageRects`), paints with `UiTree::paint(dl, res, now, span<const IRect> rects)` = one pass over the tree per damage rect under a draw-list clip of that rect (only widgets intersecting it paint; `PaintContext::clip` = the rect ∩ target, so virtualised views draw only that range; every pass of a frame shares `PaintContext::frame`, so per-frame stats and budgets count frames, not passes) and renders the list with those rects as the frame's damage. A playhead column, the control bar's readout and the header meters no longer repaint everything in their bounding box (M3 review H8). The single-region overload `paint(dl, res, now, region)` remains (benchmarks, tests). Contract for widgets and views: **paint only inside your rect** (push it as a clip if geometry could spill, e.g. glow, AA feathers past the edge; the text helpers `detail::drawText` / `views::paint::text` cut glyph ink themselves through `TextSystem::drawRunClipped`, horizontally to their box and vertically to `PaintContext::widget` = the painting widget's rect + declared overflow, with no extra draw command; `ui::Label` declares 4 px of bottom overflow for descenders) or declare the overflow with `setVisualOverflow(Insets)` (popup drop shadows do: damage and culling then use the grown rect), and report every visual change with `markDirty()` (or layout); anything drawn outside, or changed without damage, goes stale on screen.
- **Cancelling (T3.x-fix-edit)**: `UiTree::cancelInteraction()` = the captured widget's `onInteractionCancelled()` then `releaseCapture()` (`onCaptureLost`); `Shell::cancelInteraction()` (the app calls it on `SDL_EVENT_WINDOW_FOCUS_LOST`) adds cancelling any document gesture left open. Capture loss and Esc abandon drags in every editor (`Editor::abortDrag`, `ArrangementAutomation::abortDrag`, `RollState::abortDrag`); a range / rubber-band drag puts back the selection from before its press.
- **Input**: SDL-free `PointerEvent` / `KeyEvent` / `TextEvent` (`translateEvent` in `pq/ui/window.h` adapts SDL). A handled Down captures the pointer until every button is released; hover is frozen while captured. TX.node-menu-fix1: a widget that leaves the tree inside its own handler (a popup closing on the press that chose) is never captured — the capture would outlive it; widgets removed during dispatch must stay alive until it returns (`OverlayLayer::close` retires popups to a graveyard emptied at the next frame), so the tree may still read them there. Keys go: focused widget → its ancestors (a focused text-entry widget swallows printable keys) → modal scopes (newest first; `ScopeRules` passthrough modifiers / swallow unbound, which is how macro mode works) → `keyScope`s of the focus chain → global → Tab traversal. `ActionRegistry` holds string ids → handler + enabled predicate; a `Keymap` is data (scope, chord → action id) and is resolved to indices in `UiTree::setKeymap`. Dispatch does not allocate. TX.resize: `UiTree::keyHeld(key)` says whether a non-modifier key is held now (every key event updates it before routing, wherever the focus is; ≤ `kMaxHeldKeys`; `cancelInteraction` forgets them, since their releases may never arrive), for holds that act on what the pointer is over (S over a project patch block) rather than on the focus.
- **Tests**: `TestDriver` builds a tree at a size and scale, injects events, runs a fake clock through update → paint, and renders through `OffscreenTarget`.
- **Drag and drop (T5.7, `pq/ui/drag.h`)**: one session at a time in the tree. A source widget that holds the capture calls `beginDrag(payload)`; from then on pointer moves retarget instead of reaching the source, the release drops, Esc or a lost capture (window focus, the source removed) cancels; modifier keys retarget (Ctrl changes what a drop does). OS drags enter through `dragEnter` / `dragMove` / `dragExit` / `dragDrop` (the app's IDropTarget). The target is the deepest widget under the pointer whose `dragOver` returns an effect, asking ancestors while widgets return None; `dragLeave` when it changes or the session ends without a drop (never after `drop`). While the pointer rests near a scrolling edge, `update()` calls `autoScrollDrag(pos, dt)` up the ancestors (ScrollView: its viewport's edges, 24 logical px, up to 900 px/s) and keeps `needsFrame()` true. The tree paints a ghost (the payload's label, or its painter) after the root in every damage pass and damages its old and new rects; targets draw their own previews. T5.12: when an internal drag's pointer leaves the window and the app set a drag-out handler (`UiTree::setDragOutHandler`), the session ends there (dragLeave, the source's capture released) and the payload goes to the handler, which runs an OLE `DoDragDrop` (a modal loop on the UI thread until Explorer's drop or Esc; the audio thread is independent of it, the UI repaints once afterwards). Arrangement clips become a `Clips` payload once a clip move leaves the arrangement's rect.
- **Trees inside a widget (TX.panel-faces, `UiTree::setHost` / `ui::TreeHost`, `DrawList::appendTransformed`)**: a tree may be drawn inside a widget of another tree at its own scale (the project patch canvas hosts each closed stock device's `DevicePanel` this way: `pq/views/devices/panel_face.h`). The inner tree keeps its own layout, hover, capture and focus; its host forwards input to it, paints it into a list of its own and appends that list where it shows it (`appendTransformed`: every point and clip rect mapped `offset + p · scale`; scale 1 and a whole-pixel offset give the same pixels as painting in place). Through the link the inner tree's popups open in the outer tree's overlay layer (`OverlayLayer::of` follows `host()->hostWidget()`; a popup's anchor goes through `OverlayLayer::anchorRect` → `TreeHost::toHost`, so a chooser's menu opens under the control where the host draws it), and damage, frame requests, layout invalidations and new timers call `TreeHost::treeNeedsUpdate` (the host runs the tree's `update(now)` — `UiTree::now()` is the outer tree's clock — and repaints; `framesRequested()` tells a frame request from damage). Idle inner trees cost nothing.

### Theme, controls and overlays (ui, T3.2: `pq/ui/theme.h`, `controls.h`, `overlay.h`, `keymap_file.h`)
- **Theme as data**: `Theme` (surface/control/field/text/accent/selection/menu/tooltip/track/clip/grid/scrollbar colours, font roles 11/11/12 (small/body/title; bodyStrong SemiBold 11; T3.x-fix-look, Q107.4 B: two glyph-atlas pages for the 5-scale set; T3.2-fix1 had 12/12/13), `size.gutter` (the window-colour gap between panels), spacing, radii, sizes, 70-colour clip palette) is read from `assets/themes/ableton-dark.json` by a strict reader (a missing/unknown/duplicate token is an error naming it; `serializeTheme` writes the canonical file). `SceneTextures` owns it (`theme`, `uiFonts` = the theme's font roles resolved at the scale, re-resolved by `setSceneScale`/`setSceneTheme`); widgets read `PaintContext::res.theme` / `res.uiFonts` (plain member loads). Dev builds hot-reload the file (`ThemeFileWatcher` compares its write time; the window loop runs that check only when `app`'s `FileChangeNotifier` — a helper thread blocked on the directory's change-notification handle — posts an SDL event, so idle does no periodic work; T3.7 review). PQ Meter's palette stays in `signal_theme` and only signal displays use it (meters, modulation rings).
- **Controls** derive from `Control` (tooltip, info text, `VisualState` normal/hover/pressed/focused/disabled, `setStateOverride` for galleries): `Label`, `Button`, `Toggle` (Accent/Activator/Solo/Arm), `ValueControl` → `DragBox` (Live's number box), `Fader`, `Knob` (`ModulationRing` hook), `TextEdit` (on `TextBuffer`), `Dropdown`, `Scrollbar` (a `ScrollView::Dependent`), `Meter` (`MeterLevels` plain struct, repaints only when a drawn pixel changes). A value edit is a gesture: `onGestureBegin` (lazily, at the first change) → `onChange`* → `onGestureEnd`, one per drag / type-in / reset / key = one undo step (T3.4 `History` gestures). Esc during a drag, or the window losing focus (`Widget::onInteractionCancelled`), calls `cancelGesture()`: the value returns to the gesture's start and `onGestureCancel` runs instead of `onGestureEnd` (the mixer and control bar cancel their store gesture: no history entry; T3.x-fix-edit).
- **Mouse cursor** (T4.x-cursor-api): `Widget::setCursor(Cursor)` or an override of `virtual Cursor cursorAt(Vec2) const` (parts of one widget: a note's body vs its edges) says what the pointer should look like; `Cursor::Inherit` (the default) asks the parent. `UiTree::cursor()` resolves it — the captured widget's alone during a drag, else the hovered widget's up its ancestors, Arrow when nothing asks — and the app applies it with `applyCursor` (pq/ui/window.h: SDL system cursors, created once) after every input event and frame update. Shapes: Arrow, Pointer (clickable), Text, Crosshair (draw / wire), Move, ResizeEW / NS / NWSE / NESW, NotAllowed, Wait. `Splitter` shows the resize cursor of its axis over its handle. Conventions (T4.16a): a press that clicks (buttons, toggles, dropdowns, choosers, chips, fold triangles, menu-bar titles) → Pointer; a vertical value drag (knob, fader, number box) → ResizeNS (horizontal fader ResizeEW); text entry (text field, a number box's type-in, the code editor's text) → Text; a drag that moves a thing → Move; an edge / handle that resizes → the axis' Resize. `cursorAt` must be O(1)-ish and allocation-free: use state the widget keeps from its last Move (hovered part) or a cheap hit test, never a layout or text shaping. T4.16c (devices, racks, circuit, project patch; table in docs/REVIEW.md): every part's cursor comes from the same test its press runs — `FrameBase::cursorAt` (title band → Move; during a reorder drag Move, or NotAllowed when `DropTarget::refused`), the panel's `TitleBar` / `JackStrip` / displays, the rack's strip / chain list / `ZoneEditor::grabAt` / map overlay (`forEachTarget`, no allocation), `CableLayer::cursorAt` (the drag's cursor worked out on each move), `EditCanvas::cursorAt` (`hitTest`: the scene's grid), `ProjectCanvas::cursorAt` (`pressCursor` / `wireRefusal` worked out on each move, cached); `CircuitCanvas::hovered()` / `panning()` for subclasses. Device view keys (`device.selectPrev` … `device.chainDown`, `DeviceChainView::selectNeighbour` / `selectEnd` / `moveSelected` / `showNeighbourChain`, display order `shownOrder()` = `FrameBase::appendShown`) in docs/HOTKEYS.md. **Views (T4.16b)**: the arrangement's `LaneCanvas` / `BottomLanes`, its ruler and headers, the roll's note area, keys, ruler and velocity lane override `cursorAt` with the hit test their press uses (`Editor::hitTest`, `ArrangementAutomation::pressBand` / `pointAt` / `onLine` / `segmentAt`, `Ruler::hitAt`, `RollState::noteAt`, `RulerWidget::hitAt`, `VelocityLaneWidget::stemAt`), so the cursor never disagrees with the press; a drag keeps its own. The part a press would grab is highlighted (`RollState::HoverPart`: note + zone, drawn inside the note under its label; `Editor::HoverPart`: clip title strip or edge, drawn in `paintClip`; the automation's hovered breakpoint, enlarged): set on pointer moves and after a release, cleared on press and when the pointer leaves, a change damages only the old and new item's box (a breakpoint's columns). The automation's cursor depends on modifiers (Alt bends): `ArrangementView::onKey` forwards modifier presses to `LaneCanvas::modsChanged` (`drag::modsAfterKey`), which re-evaluates the hover. The beat ruler drags the loop brace (ends / whole) and locators (previewed; one `SetLoop` / `UpdateLocator` on release; Esc cancels). Keyboard nudges share `views/src/common/drag.h` (`stepByCells`, `kFineNudge` = 1/128 note).
- **Overlays**: an `OverlayLayer` as the root's last child hosts popups (`Menu` with nested submenus and keyboard navigation, `Popover`, tooltips); it is hittable only while a dismissable popup is open, so a press outside closes them and is consumed. `OverlayOptions::onOutsidePointer` (T3.19) sees the moves and presses outside every popup while that popup is open (newest first; true consumes a press instead of dismissing): the menu bar switches menus with it. Popups take and restore focus; closed popups are destroyed at the next frame.
- **Tree services** (`UiTree`): `setResources` (what widgets measure text with outside paint), `clipboard()` (`SdlClipboard` in the app, in-process otherwise), `TreeObserver`s (hover changes, presses: tooltips, the T3.7 info view via `infoFor(hovered)`), `focusVisible()` (focus came from the keyboard), `actions()`. Widgets expose `info()` / `tooltip()`. Custom layouts are arranged even without children.
- **Keymaps**: `assets/keymaps/ableton.json` (scope, keys, action id, status `implemented` | `deferred:M<n>`); `applyKeymapFile` binds implemented entries and fails on an unregistered one. `docs/HOTKEYS.md` lists every binding.

### Views and the app shell (views, T3.7: `pq/views/view_context.h`, `transport_port.h`, `shell/shell.h`, `view_scenes.h`)
- **Directory layout** (one subtree per view family so parallel tasks do not collide): `include/pq/views/` shared API; `shell/` (T3.7), `arrangement/` (T3.8–T3.10, T3.15), `mixer/` (T3.10), `piano_roll/` (T3.11–T3.12), `mos_wheel/` (T3.13–T3.14) under both `include/pq/views/` and `src/`; `src/common/paint.h` private drawing helpers. A subtree lists its sources in `src/<subtree>/sources.cmake` (`set(PQ_VIEWS_<SUBTREE>_SOURCES …)`), included by `src/views/CMakeLists.txt` when present.
- **`ViewContext`** (UI thread): owns the `DocumentStore`; views edit only through commands (`pq::doc::ops` builders, `dispatch`, `begin/commitGesture` for drags = one undo step) and subscribe with `ctx.subscribe(listener)` (document `StoreEvent`s; the context's own listener runs first) and `ctx.observe(fn)` (`view_change` bits: transport, grid, layout, project, status); both return RAII `Subscription`s, and the context must outlive every widget holding one. It also holds the view state outside the document: start marker, the shared `GridSpec` (+ the focused editor's resolved division for the grid display), detail view visible/mode, browser visibility, status line. Project I/O: `newProject` / `openProject(dir)` / `saveProject` / `saveProjectAs(dir)`; `dirty()` compares the current snapshot pointer with the last saved/loaded one (undo back to it is clean), or is set by `viewStateChanged()`, which views call after editing the saved, non-undoable `ViewState` (folds, track / lane heights, lane visibility; T3.x-fix-edit). The `file.*` actions go through `FileDialogs` (the app's SDL dialogs; tests script it), with save/discard/cancel on unsaved changes.
- **Actions and keymap**: the context registers the global actions (`transport.playStop`, `transport.continue`, `edit.undo`, `edit.redo`, `file.new/open/save/saveAs`, `view.toggleClipDevice`, `view.toggleDetail`); views register theirs in `ctx.actions()` when installed and set `ctx.scope("arrangement")`-style key scopes (ids stable for the context's lifetime). `applyKeymap` rebinds from the keymap file and can be repeated. **View actions (T3.11)**: a keymap entry marked implemented must resolve even when its view is not installed (a bare context or shell in tests), so the context pre-registers every id in `ViewContext::kPianoRollActions` with a forwarding handler; the view installs the real handler with `setViewAction(id, handler, enabled)` and clears it on destruction; without a handler the action is disabled and its chord falls through (Q27). T3.11 also moved `edit.toggleDrawMode` (B, `drawMode()`, shared with the arrangement like Live's) and `grid.narrow/widen/toggleTriplet/toggleSnap/toggleFixed` (Ctrl+1–5, editing the one shared `GridSpec`) into the context's global actions, and added `detailClip()` / `setDetailClip(ClipRef)` (the clip the clip view edits; the arrangement sets it on selection) with the `view_change::kTool` / `kDetailClip` bits.
- **`TransportPort`**: `play(from)`, `stop()`, `continuePlayback()`, `locate(t)`, `setLoop({start, end, enabled})`, `playhead()` → `PlayheadSnapshot {playing, tick, sequence}`, `auditionHz(hz, velocity, durationMs)` (T3.11: preview a pitch; the app's simulated transport ignores it), `releaseAuditions()` (AU.voices: the press ended; every sounding audition is released now, ≥ 50 ms after its onset, so a piano-roll press sounds while held, at most its length). Plain data; views read the playhead only while playing (the control bar's position readout wakes on a timer at the next sixteenth, 4–100 ms, and damages only its own rect). The context keeps the port's loop equal to the document's `LoopRegion`. `FakeTransport` (views, header-only) records calls for tests.
- **Menu bar and Tunings panel (T3.19, M3 review H7)**: `main/menu-bar` (`shell::MenuBar`, src/shell/menu_bar.cpp; 20 px above the control bar) = Live's File / Edit / Create / View / Options / Help built at open from `ui::Menu`; every item runs an action id of `ctx.actions()` (greyed when unregistered or disabled; the chord from the keymap as its shortcut; Edit's clipboard items pick `pianoroll.*` when the focus is in the clip view, else `arrangement.*`); while a menu is open, moving over another title switches (OverlayOptions::onOutsidePointer). New ids: `view.toggleTunings` (global; `ViewContext::tuningsVisible`, kLayout) and `tuning.newMain` (`kTuningsActions`, the panel's view action). `ShellSlot::Tunings` (`main/body/top/tunings`, 236 px, between the browser and the track area, hidden by default) holds `TuningsPanel` (`pq/views/tunings/tunings_panel.h`): a card per main tuning (colour, name, remove; summary + Wheel…; EDO Steps / Equave, MOS Generator / Period / Stretch, Reference; who uses it; a red dot on automated parameters, right-click → Show Automation Lane through `ArrangementAutomation`). Continuous parameters are `SetTuningParam` store gestures (Param only), the EDO size and a typed ratio equave `SetTuningDef`. The panel re-reads only on `TuningChip::tuningMayChange` events (values only for Param events) and only while shown (else a stale flag). The info view also re-reads `infoFor(hovered)` on `view_change::kInfo` (`ViewContext::infoChanged()`), for widgets whose info changes in place (the roll's pitch readout).
- **Shell** (`Shell::create(tree, ctx)`; T3.x-fix-look: the panels sit on the window colour with `size.gutter` gaps around and between them, the splitter handles being the gutters; the control bar is the window colour with panel-coloured fields, the selected track's `TuningChip` and a `CpuMeter` that polls `AudioPrefsPort::cpuLoad` at 2 Hz only while playing; the detail view starts collapsed and the first detail clip opens it, `ViewContext::setDetailClip`): root = backdrop + `main` (Column: `control-bar`, `body` splitter [`top`: `browser` slot (hidden until M5), `track-area` slot (+ hidden `mixer` slot); `bottom` splitter: `info` view | `detail` (`clip` / `device` slots)], `status-bar`) + `overlay` (last child). `ShellSlot` names the hosts; `installDefaultContent(shell)` (src/views/src/shell/default_content.cpp) is where each view installs itself (one line per view). T4.13 added `ShellSlot::Patch` (`main/body/top/track-area/patch`, hidden: the project patch view). The shell binds the keymap to the tree and feeds the info view from a `TreeObserver` (`infoFor(hovered)`). Every shell widget paints inside its rect and reports changes through `markDirty`/layout, so the window redraws only damage (T0.7-fix1).
- **View scenes** (`view_scenes.h`): a table of named scenes (`shell`, `arrangement-basic`, `pianoroll-12edo`, `pianoroll-31edo`, `pianoroll-velocity`, …) = the shell over a project plus optional setup; `renderViewScene(scene, projectDir, w, h, scale)` renders one headless (`pqdaw --screenshot <scene> out.png --project <dir>`, the goldens in `tests/views/golden/`).
- **Arrangement view** (T3.8, `pq/views/arrangement/arrangement.h`, `src/arrangement/`): `ArrangementView` in `ShellSlot::TrackArea` (`installArrangement`; focused at start so the `arrangement` key scope is live). Children: `overview`, `ruler` (bar numbers, loop brace, locator/tempo/signature markers, start marker; click = `locateAt` → start marker + `TransportPort::locate`, drag = zoom/scroll), `tracks` (a vertical `ScrollView` over `lanes` = one virtualised `LaneCanvas` + one `TrackHeader` widget per visible row, headers right of the lanes as in Live), `vscroll`, `time-ruler`. Horizontal axis = the shared time window (TL.1: `ViewContext::timeWindow` start + span, the project patch view's lanes show it too) on the lanes' width: `originTick()` + `pxPerTick` (logical px/tick = width / span). Rows = the track tree flattened (folded groups hide members; heights from `ViewState`, logical px). Per track a struct-of-arrays clip index (start/end/running-max end) → binary search of the visible clips; per clip a cached note preview keyed by clip pointer (+ tuning def pointer), so only clips whose snapshot pointer changed rebuild. Previews are progressive: a per-paint build budget (`kPreviewBuildNotesPerPaint`; the rest built in later frames without painting, then one repaint), dense clips skip their notes while the zoom changes (`zooming()`, settles after `kZoomSettleSeconds`), and the cache is bounded in notes (`kPreviewCacheNotes`, LRU by paint). **Preview tiles (T3.8-fix1)**: a preview is generated in clip-local device px (column 0 = the clip's rounded left edge, so it does not depend on the scroll's pixel fraction) by one run generator shared by the direct path (one rect per run) and the tile path (rasterised into the renderer's `TileAtlas`, drawn as one quad), which therefore draw identical pixels. With a GPU, a clip of ≥ `kTileMinNotes` notes whose body fits a tile is drawn from its tile, keyed by (clip snapshot + tuning = the preview's key, device px per tick, body height); colour is the vertex colour (muted grey, selection: no rebuild). Tiles are rasterised only in `LaneCanvas::onFrame` (build-ahead: pass 1 touches the visible clips' tiles so allocations never evict them; pass 2 builds within `kTileBuildTexelsPerFrame`), only once the zoom has settled; until then a clip draws its previous tile stretched, or directly within `kTileFallbackRectsPerPaint`, or just its body, and the lanes repaint once after the build only if a clip was drawn approximately. Tile quads and their clips' borders are emitted after the rows, one draw command per tile page (overlapping clips flush first). A tile that finds no room is marked Failed for its key and drawn directly. Stats: `tilesDrawn`, `tileNotes`, `tilesStale`, `tilesMissing`, `tilesBuilt`, `tileFailures`. `renderViewScene` runs pending frames/timers before its screenshot. Playhead: read from the port only while playing, on a timer due at the next device pixel (1/144 s … 100 ms); a move damages its old/new columns only. Extension points: `TrackHeader::mixerSection()` + `setHeaderMixerWidth` / `setHeaderDecorator` (T3.10), `setTimeSelection` / `onPaintOverlay` / `rowAt` / `tickAtX` (T3.9). **Editing (T3.9)**: `arrangement::Editor` (`src/arrangement/editing.cpp`, owned by the view) holds the `ArrangementView::Selection` (clips, tracks, time range or insert marker, `range` flag), the drag state and the in-process clipboard; `LaneCanvas` forwards pointer input to it and paints from it (selected tracks' tint, clip outline, time band, the dragged clips at their target with the originals skipped). Drags are previews (no document change) dispatched once on release; a drag frame damages only the dragged clips' old and new rects, a selection change only the selected rows over the changed time span. Each edit is one `pq::doc::ops` builder, or several chained on a scratch snapshot (`doc::apply`) into one `Batch` (carve a range = `insertEmptyClip` + `RemoveClip`; range consolidate = split + consolidate; paste = carve + `AddClip` with fresh ids): one undo step. The actions are registered with `ActionRegistry::set` like T3.8's; the right-click menu (edit commands + grid) uses `ui::openContextMenu`. A click on a clip calls `ctx.setDetailClip`; `onOpenClip` (set by `installArrangement`) shows and focuses the clip view on a double-click. Colours: theme group `arrangement.*` (sampled from Live 12, Q47). `ui::ActionRegistry::set` (add-or-replace) lets a rebuilt view take its actions over in the same `ViewContext`; a destroyed view parks them disabled.
- **Track headers and mixer** (T3.10, `pq/views/mixer/mixer.h`, `src/mixer/`, `src/arrangement/tracks.cpp`): `MixerStrip` is one track's mixer controls in two layouts: `Header` (installMixer puts one into every `TrackHeader::mixerSection()` through `setHeaderDecorator`: volume / pan / send number boxes, activator = mute, solo, arm or Pre/Post, output chooser, meter; lines hidden when the row is low) and `Vertical` (the mixer view, `MixerView` in `ShellSlot::Mixer`, Ctrl+Alt+M, replacing the arrangement while shown). The tuning chip is T3.14's `TuningChip` in `TrackHeader::chipSection()` (under the name). Strips share a `MixerController` (shared_ptr: edits as `pq::doc::ops` mixer builders, value drags as store gestures = one undo step each, track labels per snapshot, menus, meter polling). Returns and the master are pinned under the scrolling rows (`ArrangementView::bottomRows()`, `bottomRect()`). A Param-only document change (a mixer drag) rebinds rows and headers without repainting the lanes; mute repaints them (muted tracks draw grey). Meters: the plain-data `MeterSource` (`live()`, batched `read(ids, levels)`) is polled at 30 Hz only while live. Track commands (Ctrl+Shift+T, Ctrl+Alt+T, Ctrl+G, Ctrl+Shift+G, Ctrl+R in-place rename, Delete after a header click, colour palette popover, height drag on a header's bottom edge = ViewState) live in `arrangement::Editor`. Colours: theme group `mixer.*`.
- **Automation lanes** (T3.15, `pq/views/arrangement/automation.h`, `src/arrangement/automation*.cpp`): `ArrangementAutomation` (owned by the view, `ArrangementView::automation()`) keeps the view state (not undoable): automation mode (A, `arrangement.toggleAutomationMode`), per track the lanes shown (TL.5, Q239 option 1: its PINNED lanes, then one FOLLOW lane showing the parameter last asked for — `showParam` from a knob's Show Automation, a control pressed in a band, the tunings panel — which re-points it instead of adding a lane; a pinned parameter is only revealed; the follow lane's parameter merges into its pinned lane while pinned, so no parameter shows twice; first shown = its automated lanes with `LaneView::visible` pinned (the saved pins), the follow lane on the chosen parameter; `pin` / `unpin` / `setPinned`, `pinned`, `followParam`, `isFollowLane`) and the parameter drawn over its clips in automation mode (the header's `ParamChooser`); lane heights are `LaneView::height` once the lane exists. TL.2: a return's / the master's shown lanes are kept too (the project patch view's band sub-lanes show them; the pinned rows here stay A mode only). `ArrangementRow` gained `lanesH` / `firstLane` / `laneCount`: lanes sit right under their track (`ArrangementView::laneRows()`, `laneRect`), `rowAt()` and the T3.9 editor still see only the track's own lane. One `LaneHeader` widget per lane (chooser, TL.5 pin — filled red: pinned, a click unpins; outlined: the follow lane, a click pins — + (a new pinned lane) / −, resize; the chooser menu and the envelope menu carry Pin / Unpin Lane), placed by `TrackStack` from `headerWidgets()` / `laneHeaderWidgets()`. Parameters: `paramChoices` (track mixer params + every main tuning's continuous params; one lane per `ParamRef` project-wide, so a tuning automated on another track is shown and edited where it lives, `findAutomationLane`), `paramDisplay` (fader position for volume/sends, log for Hz, the MOS region ±25 % for the generator, inverted for the activator). Editing (`AutomationBand` = a lane or an automation-mode row; the pinned returns / master only in automation mode): add on the line (snapped), move (relative snap, display-scale value, clamped between unmoved neighbours, readout), double-click / Delete, Shift/Ctrl and time-selection multi-select, Alt-drag curvature, shapes over the time selection; drags are previews dispatched on release as one `pq::doc::ops` edit (Automation-only ChangeSet; the first edit of an unautomated parameter adds `AddAutomationLane` to the same Batch: Structural). An Automation-only document change repaints only the bands showing the touched lanes. Envelopes: `EnvelopeCursor` / `envelopeVertices` evaluate the shared curve law (`pq/core/automation_curve.h`) incrementally; the polyline has a vertex per breakpoint plus ≤ 2 px chords on curved segments, collapses to first/min/max/last per pixel column and is drawn as one `smoothPolyline` ribbon per band, clipped to it; it starts/ends at breakpoints three columns outside the painted part so partial repaints equal full ones. **Envelope tiles (T3.x-fix-paint, `automation_tiles.cpp`)**: with the renderer's TileAtlas an automated band's line (ribbon coverage: core + `kSmoothFeather` sampled at pixel centres, or the dense path's column spans) is rasterised into tiles of 512 timeline px (column g = ticks [g/k, (g+1)/k), drawn at g + round(lanes.x − origin·k): scrolling keeps them), keyed by (lane snapshot, held by the cache so its address cannot be reused; exact zoom; band height and value range; display scale; line width; dense flag), and drawn as one quad per chunk with the line colour as vertex colour (overrides: no rebuild). Built only in `LaneCanvas::onFrame` (`buildEnvelopeTiles`, ≤ 512 Ki texels per frame, never while zooming); a chunk without its tile is drawn directly and the build damages it whole, so a band never shows tile and direct pixels side by side after a frame. Direct path: no GPU, a band being dragged (its preview points), pinned returns / master, while zooming. Stats `envTilesDrawn` / `envTilesMissing` / `envTilesBuilt` / `envTileFailures`. Overrides: `ViewContext::automationOverridden` / `reenableAutomation` (`view_change::kAutomation`): a Param dispatch of an automated parameter overrides it; envelopes and `MixerStrip` markers turn grey, the "Re-enable Automation" button shows above the headers. Colours: theme group `automation.*`. Scenes `automation-lanes`, `automation-mode-over-clips` (fixture `tests/views/fixtures/automation`).
- **Clip view / piano roll** (T3.11, `pq/views/piano_roll/clip_view.h`, `row_model.h`; `src/piano_roll/`): `ClipView` (in `ShellSlot::ClipDetail`, hidden without a clip) = clip box | corner (Fold, Scale) + ruler (loop brace, start/end markers, bar numbers) | keys + note area | lane header (Chance) + velocity lane; one widget per part, each painting only its rect. Shared state (`RollState`, private): the clip and its caches (tuning, `RowModel`, per-chunk summaries keyed by chunk pointer: time extent, Degree span, ratio-denominator lcm, non-Degree count), the view transform (device px per tick, scroll), the selection (sorted ids + a bitmap for O(1) tests), the drag preview. **Rows**: `RowModel` = explicit rows (absolute degree, cents, top/centre/bottom content px, flags: equave start, natural, in scale, named) highest first, built per tuning/zoom/fold; Degree notes sit on their row (O(1) unfolded), Ratio/Cents notes at true pitch interpolated in cents between row centres, marked off-grid beyond 0.001 ¢. **Layouts (T3.12)**: `RowLayout` Uniform (EDOs), Proportional (MOS / Scala default) and Equidistant, filled in `RowModel::build`; everything downstream reads only top/centre/bottom/cents (see the next item). **Edits**: every gesture is previewed (`DragState`, no document change) and dispatched once on release through `pq::doc::ops` (one history entry); Up/Down via `transposeNotes`, which steps Ratio notes on the clip's **virtual fundamental** (the document's, T3.6-fix1: `doc::virtualFundamental`; `RollState::virtualFundamental` only caches it per clip snapshot, feeding the per-chunk lcm of the notes' own denominators), drawn per SPEC §4.3 as a line labelled with F (`1/D`, or a lock such as `7/20`) and its harmonics (T3.x-fix-look: the lines under the notes, their numbers in a strip at the note area's right edge drawn in `paintOver`, an "F = 1/D · Hz" chip in the corner when F is out of view, the clip box's JI switch = `ClipView::setShowHarmonics`). Labels that share an axis are placed through `views/src/common/labels.h` `LabelSpace` (a label touching one already placed is skipped; placement over the whole widget, so partial repaints agree). Arrow keys (T3.x-fix-look): the roll's Left / Right move the selected notes by a grid cell (`pianoroll.nudgeLeft/Right`); the arrangement's move the insert marker, Up / Down select the neighbouring track (`arrangement.markerLeft/Right`, `trackUp/Down`). **Damage**: drags of ≤ 256 notes damage exactly the old/new preview rects (+ their velocity-lane columns), larger ones the note area + lane; painting culls rows by binary search and notes by chunk time extents.
- **Identities in the roll (T3.19)**: right-click on the note area = the note menu (convert the selection to Degree / Ratio / Cents via `ops::convertNotes`, Set Pitch… = a type-in popover parsed by `RollState::parsePitch`: "7/4", "386.3c", "4\22+1"; Lock Fundamental ▸ harmonic 1–16 and Unlock; Row Labels ▸); draw with Shift (draw mode or Shift+double-click) adds reference-anchored Ratio notes on the virtual fundamental's nearest harmonic (`RollState::ratioFor`; no fundamental: the row's pitch snapped like Convert to Ratio) — `DrawnNote::mode` Row / Overlay / Ratio. Ratio notes are labelled "n/d · k" (k = harmonic over F) at their top-left when ≥ 24 logical px wide and as tall as the small font (collected during the note pass into `RollState::noteLabels`, drawn after the notes). `RowOptions::labels` / `RowLabels` Auto / Name / Degree / Cents / Ratio (`formatRowLabel`; Auto = Ratio for an all-ratio Scala scale, else Name; Cents and Ratio labels follow `retune` in place, ≤ 15 chars = no allocation); the clip box's Row Labels chooser and the keys' right-click menu set it. Hover readout: `RollState::hoverAt` re-formats only when the row / note under the pointer or the snapshot changed (title "Row E3" / "Ratio Note 7/4", body cents · Hz · nearest simple ratio and deviation · overlay EDO step · harmonic of F) and calls `ctx.infoChanged()`: a row change damages only the info view.
- **Scale / MOS layouts and live morph** (T3.12, `row_model.h`, `src/piano_roll/`): MOS and Scala clips get one row per degree in `RowLayout::Proportional` (default; mos-roll.js `layoutRows`: rows at true cents, px per cent = lane · size / equave, asymmetric blocks from the distance to each side's nearest other pitch, nominal min(22, max(4, 0.62 · lane)) logical px, 1-px gap; rounded to whole device px; content y linear in cents from a fixed origin, so a morph keeps unchanged pitches in place; a scale out of pitch order falls back to Equidistant) or `Equidistant` (one equal lane per degree); `RollState::setScaleLayout` keeps the centred row. Rows carry a step class (the step above the degree, 0.01-¢ buckets, largest first) and z (= its rank); the note area paints Proportional notes in z order (counting sort into reused buffers: small steps under large ones, selected on top) and `noteAt` picks the top one. The keys of a scale layout are the row blocks coloured by class: tonic `roll.keyTonic`, L `roll.keyLarge`, s blended toward `roll.keySmall` by hardness (mos-explorer softness), extra MODMOS / Scala sizes interpolated and pulled toward `roll.keyExtra` (`stepClassColours`). **Background EDO** (`setOverlayEdo(n)`, view state): lines (`roll.edoLine` / `edoEquave`) placed through the rows like any pitch; it is the `displayGrid` of `transposeNotes` / `moveNotes` / `stepPitch` (Cents notes step on it), and Alt in draw mode / Alt+double-click draw Cents notes on its nearest line. **Live morph**: `RollState::refresh` follows a generator-only change of a MOS (same size, rotation, period, no MODMOS / generator sequence, not folded) in place when old and new generator lie inside one `MosLinearTable` interval: `tuning::Mos::retuneLinear` (n FMAs from `rotateLinearForm`, allocation-free; `Tuning::mos()` has a non-const overload for it) + `RowModel::retune` (cents, classes, geometry; no allocation), and `ClipView` then repaints only keys, note area and clip box (`pitchOnlyChange`); anything else rebuilds (`doc::buildTuning` → buildMOSScale). Sources: the MOS wheel's preview, and the clip box's Generator box (`beginGeneratorMorph` / `morphGenerator` / `endGeneratorMorph`): each step is `ViewContext::setTuningPreview` (no document change), past a region's end it moves into `findAdjacentRegion`'s region with `transferModeRotation` (as the chart), and release dispatches one `tuningCommand` for the clip (one undo step). The clip box shows Layout / EDO Grid / Generator rows only for scale tunings (EDO goldens unchanged). Scenes `pianoroll-5L2s-prop`, `-equi`, `-near-collapse`, `-over-31edo`, `pianoroll-scala`.
- **MOS wheel region chart** (T3.13, `pq/views/mos_wheel/mos_chart.h`, `src/mos_wheel/`): `MosChart`, one widget porting mos-explorer's canvas (axis, Stern–Brocot bands from `tuning::buildMosRegions`, EDO markers ≤ `MosChartConfig::maxEdo`, temperament ranges, generator marker, size scrollbar, hover card). No document dependency: it emits a plain `MosSelection` {key, perPeriodKey, periodsPerEquave, period, generatorCents, region bounds, mirror, modeRotation, exact `edo`} through `setOnChange(fn(optional<MosSelection>, MosGesture))` — `Live` while the generator is dragged, `Commit` on a click / drag release (= one undo step for the receiver), `Cancel` on Esc — and `select(perPeriodKey, gen, rotation)` / `clearSelection()` set it from outside without emitting (T3.14 hosts it in the popover and turns a Commit into a tuning command). Generator drags snap to the visible EDO markers (exact m·period/n; Alt bypasses) and cross into `findAdjacentRegion`'s region carrying the mode (`transferModeRotation`). Layout (bands, markers, temperament rows, label strings) is cached and rebuilt only on view / rect / config / selected-region changes; a generator change damages the marker column (split around the selected row), the value pill and the selected band only. Colours: theme group `mosChart.*` + the explorer's region palette and temperament colours. Scenes `mos-chart-default` / `mos-chart-zoomed` open it full-window in the shell's overlay layer.
- **MOS wheel circle and popover** (T3.14, `pq/views/mos_wheel/mos_circle.h`, `mos_wheel.h`): `MosCircle` ports `drawMOSCircle` (step slices, degree spokes with cents, tonic, JI markers, hardness slider, ◀ gen ▶ / ◀ step ▶ via `tuning::applyModeAction`, parent / child arrows, MODMOS dot drag, all-intervals overlay). Like the chart it has no document dependency: input `setSelection(MosSelection)` + `setModmos`, output `setOnChange(fn(selection, modmos, MosGesture))`. Its scale model (pitches, colours, labels, title parts) is cached and rebuilt only when the value / config / toggle changes, the JI table only when the displayed period or stretch changes; paint reuses scratch vectors. `generatorForHardnessIn(sel, r)` = `mos_calc::generatorForHardness` in closed form (L − r·s is linear in the generator inside a region; exact on the unrounded steps, ~µs instead of the CLI's 0.4-ms bisection; Q83). `MosWheelPopover` (a `ui::Popover`) links chart and circle and adds an EDO field, a Scala file pick (`FileDialogs::chooseScalaFile`, default: cancelled) and temperament presets; it edits a draft for one `TuningTarget` (clip / track / main tuning) and commits it on close as ONE command (`tuningCommand`: SetClipTuning / SetTrackTuning with a private tuning, or SetTuningDef); Esc / Cancel leave no history. While open, the draft is the context's **tuning preview** (`ViewContext::setTuningPreview` / `previewedTuning`, `view_change::kTuningPreview`; not in the document, so no Structural change per drag frame): the piano roll resolves its clip's tuning through `previewedTuning`, so it morphs live. The popover moves by its title band (`OverlayLayer::move`; the position is remembered for the session, `MosWheelPopover::rememberedPosition`). `TuningChip` (clip box; T3.10's headers; the control bar) opens it (narrow chips show `compactTuningLabel`) (it re-resolves only on document events that can change a tuning, `TuningChip::tuningMayChange`: Reset, Structural, tunings or tuning params touched, and repaints only when its text or swatch changed, so a mixer drag costs no chip work, T3.x-fix-paint); `installMosWheel(shell)` binds `tuning.openMosWheel` (Ctrl+Shift+W: the clip in the clip view, else the first main tuning). Colours: theme group `mosCircle.*`. Scenes `mos-circle-5L2s`, `mos-circle-modmos`, `mos-wheel-popover`.
- **Circuit canvas** (T4.2/T4.3, `pq/views/circuit/symbols.h`, `circuit_scene.h`, `circuit_canvas.h`, `circuit_viewer.h`; `src/circuit/`): the base of the circuit editor (T4.4), probing (T4.5) and the project patch view (T4.13). **Symbols** (`describe(patch, node)` → `SymbolSpec`: symbol, glyph, size and pins in world units, multiples of 4; `paintBody(dl, spec, origin, scale, Layer::Fill|Stroke, colour)`): ANSI gates, op-amp triangles (comparators, summing amps), × / ÷ circles, oscillators with waveforms, z⁻¹ / delay boxes, filter boxes with response glyphs (stdlib filter callees), mux trapezoids, IC blocks with labelled pins, const / param / source terminals, in / out / export flags, subcircuit blocks; inputs left, outputs right, unconnected optional inputs hidden, pin stubs drawn by the canvas (crisp, snapped like wires). **Scene** (`buildScene(patch)` → `CircuitScene`, plain data, shared read-only): the stored layout completed by `graph::completeLayout` with the symbol geometry, wires as orthogonal polylines (corners added where a stored route misses a pin), wire styles from a local forward inference (kind, const / param / audio rate, per-voice → doubled, audio into a subcircuit's param pin → dashed modulation), junctions (net points where ≥ 3 directions meet), labels (titles, binding captions, pin labels, comments), a uniform 256-unit spatial grid (CSR) for culling and hit tests. `tidy(patch)` = autoLayout with the symbol geometry. `indexScene(scene, include)` (T4.13) = the derived parts (junctions per net, bounds, spatial grid) of a scene built by hand: buildScene ends with it, the project patch view builds its own scene and calls it. **Canvas** (`CircuitCanvas`, a widget): view = world corner + zoom (device px = zoom × UI scale); Ctrl+wheel zooms at the pointer, wheel pans, middle / Alt drag pans, F fits; click / Shift / Ctrl selection, rubber band, Esc, double-click → `onOpen`; hooks `onSelectionChanged`, `onHoverChanged`, `setWireLit` (T4.5), `paintOverlay` and (T4.13) `paintUnderlay` (virtuals: over / under the scene, every level of detail, not cached in the Far chunks). LOD: Far (< 0.3: node outlines + 1-px wires in one colour, sub-pixel jogs skipped; the whole picture cached in world-anchored 256-px chunks of two coverage tiles — wires, nodes — built in `onFrame` within 300 µs per frame, visible chunks first then the ring around the view; while zooming the last complete scale's chunks are stretched; selection / hover drawn over them), Mid (symbols, styled wires, junctions), Near (≥ 0.75: + titles / captions in the UI font; pin labels ≥ 1.0; hidden when they do not fit). **Symbol tiles**: per distinct body (`SymbolSpec::bodyKey`) two coverage tiles (fill, stroke) rasterised by `ui::rasterizeCoverage` at the exact device scale in `onFrame` (≤ 1 Mi texels per frame) once a zoom has settled (`kZoomSettleSeconds` timer); a settled frame draws each body as two quads (grouped per page and layer: few draw commands), a zooming frame stretches the previous level's tiles, no atlas (CPU paints) = direct drawing; tiles equal direct drawing to 1/255. Pan / zoom / selection / hover repaint only the canvas; idle = no frames, no timers. **Viewer** (`CircuitViewer`, an overlay `Popover`; `loadPatchDocument` parses a .pqp with its imports): title bar (file › patch path, Back, Tidy, Fit, Close), subcircuit navigation into this file's or an imported file's patch, `laidOutText()` = `spliceLayout` of the shown layout. `installCircuitViewer(shell)` binds `view.openPatch` (View menu; `FileDialogs::choosePatchFile`, the app's SDL dialog). Scenes `circuit-symbols` (`symbolSheet()`), and the patch scenes `circuit`, `circuit-svf`, `circuit-svf-core` (`isPatchScene`: `renderViewScene`'s `projectDir` is the .pqp). Colours: theme group `circuit.*`. The shared drag rules (click slop, press → drag tracker, rubber band) are `src/common/drag.h` (M3 review M17, first step: the arrangement, automation and piano-roll editors use its slop too). **Hanging cables** (TX.cables, SPEC §15 2026-11-02 "cables and the node menu"; `pq/views/circuit/cables.h`, `src/circuit/cables.cpp`): a scene wire with `SceneWire::cable` is a cable, not a trace — its points are its rest shape (`cableRest`: the static solve, a catenary of length chord × `kCableSlack` from a table solved once per angle, 1° apart, so a rest shape is a lookup), its segments and the scene grid hold that curve (the Far chunks draw it thin, one colour, antialiased; no junction dots on cables), `cableKey` names the connection across rebuilds. The canvas keeps a `CableSim` (Verlet at a fixed 1/120 s step, damping, ends pinned, one-sided length constraints, gravity's restoring pull toward the hanging shape at ω² = g / sag: the rest shape is the exact equilibrium; a moved end carries the rope `kCableCarry` of the way and it swings; calm cables sleep on their rest shapes, and every cable sleeps ≤ 1.75 s after its last move) synced by key in `setScene` (kept connections keep their motion, new ones start asleep at rest). A cable's ends: its scene ends, a control's live `Face::controlAnchor` (at its rim, toward the source; an anchor that moves lands at once, never swings) and a lifted node's drag offset (below). Frames run only while a cable is awake (`onFrame` → `stepCables`: damage = each moving cable's old ∪ new bounds), never at Far (settled at once). Drawn after the faces and labels (`paintCables`, Near and Mid): Catmull-Rom through the rope (≈ 12 px chords), a soft shadow, an additive glow under lit tubes, the tube (7 lanes across: rim, specular highlight, body, darker underside), a dark groove on per-voice cables, a plug at each end (socket ring, sleeve, collar; shorter on short cables), then the additive light flowing source → target on lit cables; width 6 world units (1.5 … 16 px); cables crossing the face whose control the pointer is on are drawn at `kCableFade`. `hitTest`: pins, then cables (the rope as it hangs now, within 4 px or the tube's half width, through a grid over the cables' current bounds; over a face's control only a plug), then nodes and traces; `facePointer` leaves a press on a plug to the canvas. Glow: `cableLevels(wires, levels)` / `cableSignalLive()` (the host's), `wakeCables()`; polled ≤ 30 Hz only while live (and once after) and only for visible cables at Near / Mid; a lit cable repaints per poll, silence repaints nothing. `cablePoints`, `cableMidpoint`, `cableGlow`, `cablesMoving`, `setCablesAnimated(false)` (tests: every move lands at once). **The loose cable** (TX.node-menu-fix1): a new connection being drawn out is a real cable too — `setLooseCable(from, to, style, snapped)` on every move of the host's drag (its own one-cable `CableSim`, `kLooseCableCarry`: it trails the pointer and swings; `snapped` adds the selection halo), drawn last over the other cables at every level of detail; `dropLooseCable()` on release: at the next scene a NEW cable (none of those there when it came out of its port) from the same port whose other end is nearest its free end takes its motion over (`CableSim::adopt`: no pop to the rest shape), else it goes at the next frame; `clearLooseCable()` on cancel. Frames only while it moves. **Lifted nodes** (TX.block-drag): `setNodeDrag(nodes, offset)` lifts scene nodes for a host's drag while the scene still has them where they were — `paint` runs two passes: the scene without them (their non-cable wires left out; a chunk of the Far picture they or their wires were in drawn directly without them, its tile kept), then the lifted pass with the view shifted by the offset (`paintUnderlay`, their wires, bodies, faces / panels, labels, `paintNodeOverlay` with `lifting()` true), a trace with one end lifted as a Z between its ends, and their cables last (plugged into their ports at the offset; they swing). TX.drag-grow: `setNodeShift(nodes, layers, offsets)` (only while lifted) moves further scene nodes without lifting them — what a drag makes room for — each shift layer drawn in a pass of its own (view shifted by its offset; `paintLayer()` / `nodeLayer(v)` tell the host's `paintUnderlay` / `paintNodeOverlay` which layer's parts to draw) before the lifted pass, hit and plugged at its offset; a trace with ends in different layers is a Z; `SceneWire::fromNode / toNode` name the node a pinless end moves with (a chain into an opened capsule's frame edge: its In / Out terminal). `hitTest` / `faceUnder` find them where they are drawn (a cable under one still hits: a splice). Each move changes only the offset (no allocation, no scan); a new scene drops the lift (the host lifts the new scene's nodes again); `clearNodeDrag` puts them back.
