- **Racks + macros** (T4.8, `pq/document/model.h` "Racks", `commands.h`, `edit_ops.h` "Racks", `lower.h` "Racks"; `src/views/src/device_chain/rack_frame.cpp`; QUESTIONS Q140). **Document**: a rack is a `Device` with a `rack` payload (patch `AudioEffectRack` / `InstrumentRack`, kind AudioEffect / Instrument): `Rack { chains (Chain: ChainId, name, devices, volumeDb, pan, active, solo, key / velocity / select Zone), mappings (MacroMapping: MappingId, macro, device, param, min, max, curve), macro names, visible macros, variations, Rand exclusions, key zone unit, per-voice chains, show chains / macros }`; macros and the selector are the rack's params (`macro1` … `macro16`, `select`, 0 … 127, `SetDeviceParam`). Racks nest; `findDevice` finds a device at any depth (`DeviceLoc::chain` / `rack`), `findChain`, `findMapping`, `mappingOf`, `forEachDevice` / `forEachDeviceIn`. Commands AddChain / RemoveChain / MoveChain (Structural), RenameChain (Meta), SetChainMixer (Param, `ParamRef::chain`), SetChainActive / SetChainSolo (Structural), SetChainZone (Param, Structural when the zone switches on / off), AddMacroMapping / RemoveMacroMapping / SetMacroMapping (Structural), SetRackOptions (Meta, Structural for the key unit / per-voice chains); AddDevice / MoveDevice take a `ChainId`; removing / moving a device out of a rack drops the mappings onto it (and its wires) in the same step. Builders: `groupDevices` (Ctrl+G), `addChain`, `addChainWithDevice`, `moveDeviceToNewChain`, `deleteChains`, `duplicateChain`, `setChainMixer` / `Active` / `Solo` / `Zone`, `mapMacro` / `unmapMacro` / `unmapParam` / `setMacroMapping`, `setMacroValue`, variations (`storeVariation`, `recallVariation` (one step), `deleteVariation`, `renameVariation`, `randomizeMacros` (seeded), `setMacroExcluded`), `cloneDevice` (fresh ids, mappings follow), `ungroupDevices` (T4.17, Ctrl+Shift+G: a one-chain rack becomes its devices in place; several chains refused). T4.17: every device may carry its own `name` (Meta, `RenameDevice` / `renameDevice`, Ctrl+R in place over the title: `DevicePanel::setTitle` / `beginRename`; schema 8). Schema 5 (a device's `"rack"`; migration 4 → 5 changes nothing). **Lowering**: inline in the track patch (`lower.h` "Racks"): chains summed through the chain mixer (`gain` stages with document-derived gains `c<chain>_gl` / `_gr`), zones as gains with ramp coefficients the document derives (`c<chain>_s0` … / `v0` … / `k0` …; select zones gate an effect chain's input; key / velocity zones per voice), muted chains left out; rack params `d<rack>__<name>` only when read or wired; a mapped param's pin gets the mapping math (`NodeRole::Mapping`), not a param; wires into rack params and mapped params follow Q135. `NodeRole::Chain` / `Mapping` / `Rack` (ids spanning role spaces, like `Wire`). `rackSubcircuit()` builds a rack's nodes as a patch of their own (the circuit view; with a `DeviceLibrary`, user devices inside it call their renamed roots as lower() does); user devices (T4.4-fix1) may sit at any depth (`makeCallees` walks every device; a rack itself has no `source`); `chainParamValues()` (the link's fast path: a chain mixer / zone drag writes only the existing slots, `ProjectLink::writeLinked`); a macro turn is one param write. **Views**: `DeviceChainView` holds `FrameBase` frames (a `DeviceFrame` per device, a `RackFrame` per rack: strip, macro panel = a T4.6 `DevicePanel` over the rack's params, chain list with the chain mixer, zone editor, the shown chain's frames nested, an add slot); `Impl::all` indexes every frame shown (panels, cables, rings work on nested devices and macros); drops into chains / new chains; map mode (`setMapMode`, `pickMacro`, `mapPicked`, an overlay); mapped knobs show the macro-driven value and ring, and turn their macro; `showChain`, `showZones`, `openRackCircuit` (the viewer follows structural / meta edits of the rack: `CircuitViewer::replaceDocument`; closes when the rack is removed); nested device frames get T4.4-fix1's Open in Circuit View / Revert to Stock and are rebuilt when their user text plays another graph (`FrameBase::keeps`); the project patch view (T4.13) draws a rack as one plain block (`project_patch::Node::rack`, `inner` = its devices at any depth, whose calls sit inline in the track patch; a wire to / from one of them ends on the block at port `<port>@<device id>`; `nodeOf` of an inner device is the rack's block; `checkAgainstGraph` counts inner calls with their rack); actions `device.group`, `device.ungroup`, `device.rename`, `device.addInstrumentRack` / `device.addAudioEffectRack` (Create menu). T4.x-fix-ui (M4 review): the macro panel shows the macros in use plus a free column (`shownMacros`: mapped or named, capped by `visibleMacros`), the chain selector is the zone editor's (Chain tab marker, value beside the tabs), the zone editor has an axis and shows the hovered bar's range in the info view; key zones read `voice.noteHz` (Hz unit) / `voice.noteDegree` (Degree unit), decided at note-on (M7). Scenes `audio-rack`, `instrument-rack`. **Nesting of any depth** (T4.x-fix-engine, M4 review M9; the user: no depth cap): every walk of a rack tree is iterative — `forEachDeviceIn`, `findDevice`, deep `operator==`, `checkList` / `checkRackOwn`, the project file's writer and reader (explicit level stacks; path segments in a deque) and its duplicate-id report, the lowering (`lowerList` with `instrumentRacksPlay` answering each Instrument Rack once, `planWires`' flow, `chainOf` / `instrumentRack`, the mapped-macro chain in `lowerSites`), apply's path-copying edits (`findInTree` + `copyAlong`, `pruneMappings` post-order), `cloneDevice`, and the compile's zero propagation (`idleAfterBlocks`) and sleep-plan walks; `Rack::~Rack` hands nested chains to the outermost destructor. The file's indentation stops growing at 32 levels (a deep nest is written in O(size)). Limits that remain: one track patch holds at most 65,535 inputs and params (`graph::kMaxPorts`, 16-bit port counts: about 32,000 rack chains per track; refused with an error by `composeUnit`), and the Device view frames nested racks inline down to `DeviceChainView::kMaxNestedRackFrames` (24) levels (deeper racks show their strip and chain list, not their chain's devices).
- **Groups** (TX.groups, SPEC §15 2026-11-01 "resize, groups, and the free-device rack"; `pq/document/model.h` "Groups", `commands.h` SetDeviceGroup, `edit_ops.h` "Groups" (`src/document/src/edit_groups.cpp`), `lower.h` "Groups", `devices.h` `groupPatch` / `groupDeviceInfo`; `pq/views/project_patch/*` (TX.groups paragraphs), `src/views/src/device_chain/group_frame.cpp`; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.groups-a). **One mechanism, nothing new underneath**: a group = a group device + free devices tagged as its members + ordinary wires. **Document**: the group is a `Device` with patch `kGroupDevice` ("Group", kind AudioEffect, no params / settings / text / meta / rack), in its track's own list at its place in the series (or free); a member is a device of the same list with `Device::group` = the group's id, always `free` (no chain order inside); its terminals are the group device's own ports — In = its output `kGroupInPort` ("input"), Out = its input `kGroupOutPort` ("output") — so In → member and member → Out are plain `Wire`s, and a wire across the capsule's boundary is any other wire (wires name devices, not containers). Positions inside are the circuit's view state (`ViewState::patchNudges` by device id, offsets from the capsule's automatic row), as for every free device. `checkList`: members free, their group a group device of the same list, nothing of either kind in a rack chain (GU.2: groups nest, up to `kMaxGroupDepth`, no cycles). `SetDeviceGroup` is Structural since GU.2 (was Meta: since IG.1-2 membership decides what runs per note). `RemoveDevice` of a group removes its members and their wires; `MoveDevice` of a group to another track moves its members with it (appended); a member cannot leave its track alone. `pasteDevices` / `duplicateDevices`: a copied group brings copies of its members and of the wires among them and its terminals; a copied member alone is a free device. Schema 14 (`"group"`). **Builders**: `ops::groupInCircuit(snapshot, ids, DeviceLibrary, devices)` (Ctrl+G in the circuit): one track's own list; the selected series devices must be next to each other in the series; the group goes in at the first one's place; each becomes a free member and the series becomes wires — In → the first's chain input, each main output → the next's chain input, the last's → Out (the lowering's names: a device's first non-const `in` / a meta module's chain input, its first `out`); selected free devices move in as they are (only free ones: a free group, no wires). Refused when it could change the sound: several tracks, a rack chain, a rack, a stock instrument, a group or a member, a gap, the first device taking the notes or voices (a group is an audio effect), a switched-off or unplugged device inside the series, a wire already joining two series ports, and — checked by applying the edit to a scratch snapshot and comparing `wireStates` — a feedback loop whose 1-sample delay would move onto a new wire. `ops::ungroupInCircuit(snapshot, DeviceLibrary, group)` (Ctrl+Shift+G): reads the series back from the one wire into Out along each device's chain input (exactly one wire from In or a member) to In, removes those wires, clears the members' tags, removes the group and puts the series back at its place in that order (MoveDevice no-ops when it is already there: ungrouping what grouping made gives back the same document); refused when the wiring is not one series (several wires into Out, In feeding another input, wires on the group's own ports; a free group with terminal wires). **Lowering**: the group device calls `groupPatch()` (`patch Group { in x; in output; out y : … stereo; out input; y = output; input = x }`, added to the callee library when a group plays) like any effect; members are free devices; the chain after a group is declared stereo (an upper bound: a mono sum stays mono, no pack of a stereo signal). A wide signal (or one whose width only inference knows, the In terminal) into a meta module's input arrives as its mid, as the chain gives it (lowerSites; on mono the signal itself). So a grouped series is the series' graph with its links as wires: rendered bit for bit equal, interpreter and JIT (tests/app/test_groups.cpp). **Project patch view**: a group's block is a CAPSULE (`Node::group`). Opened (default; `LayoutOptions::closedGroups` lists the closed ones, `ProjectPatchView::setGroupOpen`, view state of the session): the block is the capsule's frame — not a scene node; the canvas draws it in `paintUnderlay` (rounded frame, title strip "Group · N devices", outlined while `selectedGroup()`) — around its contents: the In / Out terminals (`NodeKind::Terminal`, `device` = the group, the circuit's input / output symbols) and the members (ordinary device blocks: faces, panels, S-resize, wires, Delete), laid out In → members (list order) → Out on one inner spine, then the members' nudges; the capsule grows to hold them, its chain pins (`Node::pinTop`) at the inner spine so the band's chain runs straight through it; wires inside take the capsule's title strip as their gutter; a wire into an INPUT (`Edge::input`: an audio input, Out) is drawn solid (a signal path), into a param dashed. Closed: one block like a rack (`inner` = its members, `<port>@<id>` ports for wires crossing its edge, internal wires not drawn) with a miniature of its contents (`Node::mini`, `miniWires`, drawn in `paintOverlay`). Gestures: Ctrl+G / Ctrl+Shift+G (`groupSelection` / `ungroupSelection`; the block menu's Group / Ungroup / Close Group / Take Out of Group), a press on a capsule's frame selects the group (`selectGroup`) and drags it in the chain (Ctrl: nudges), its title strip's double-click closes it, a closed block's opens it; a block dragged inside its capsule moves (a nudge), out of it leaves the group, a free block dropped into a capsule joins (`setDeviceGroup`: one step); wires start on In and end on Out like any pins. `checkAgainstGraph` counts members as blocks of their band and checks that every capsule holds its terminals and members. Scene `project-patch-group`. **The embeddable canvas** (also for TX.free-rack): `ProjectPatchView(ctx, scope)` is the same view, model, canvas and gestures scoped by `LayoutOptions::scope` — the model is built as usual and then pruned (`Builder::prune`) to the scope group's capsule, its terminals and members and the wires among them, on one band without source, mixer or strip (`ProjectPatch::scoped`: no bands or containers painted); it is shown while its parent is, binds no view actions, never morphs, builds itself on its first arrange with a size and fits its contents (`CircuitCanvas::fitAll`). A free-device rack is the same with another prune (the track's free devices). **Device view**: members are not frames (`device_chain::shownDevices`); a group's frame (`GroupFrame`, `makeGroupFrame`) is a title bar ("Group · N devices": selects, reorders like any device) over the embedded scoped `ProjectPatchView` (zoom, pan, wire, faces and panels edited in place, S-resize, Ctrl+G / Delete inside), its width dragged at its right edge (`Impl::groupWidths`, the session); cables to a member end on its group's title bar (`DeviceChainView::groupPoint`), wires inside a group are drawn by its panel only. Scene `device-group-panel`. The Device view's own Ctrl+G still makes a rack (T4.8). **TX.groups-fix1** (QUESTIONS Q?-TX.groups-fix1-a): the In terminal carries whatever reaches the group's place — `chainStages` gives a group Notes / Voices in → Voices out — and the lowering picks what the group plays by that stage (`Callees::groupCallee`): `groupPatch()` for audio, `groupVoicePatch()` (no `global` bound) where voices reach it (a stage of the meta voice run: x takes the run's signal, y gives it on), `groupNotesPatch()` at a MIDI track's note source (the group is the track's instrument; a wire from In gives the input it reaches the note's signal of that input's meaning — Frequency `input` = voice.hz, Phase `phase` = phasor(voice.hz), anything else nothing (Inactive) —; a run ending in a group ends by the `done` of the device whose output alone reaches Out when its type ends voices, else the 5 ms release). Switched-off FREE devices pass their chain input to their main output while a wire reads it (`pass__<in>__<out>`, `LDev::bypass`), as the chain passes them by. Ctrl+G's series wires are **chain links** (`Wire::chain`, schema 15): planWires plans them before every other wire and lowerSites sums them first into an input, so a loop through a grouped series keeps its z⁻¹ on the wire that had it; Ctrl+Shift+G takes the series as the members its chain links touch (list order) and checks the links are exactly that series' (a schema-14 group without links is read back from its wires as before), and moves nothing when the series is already at the group's place. Ctrl+G groups any stretch of a voice run (In = notes or per-voice signal, polyphony following the wires), switched-off devices and unplugged ones inside a series; refusals left: a stock instrument, a stock or unplugged device inside a voice run (the run sums there), a switched-off device where the notes arrive, a stock effect at the note source, a switched-off unplugged device, an unplugged device whose output the next unplugged one drops, nesting.
- **Free-device rack and the Device view's cables** (TX.free-rack, SPEC §15 2026-11-01 "resize, groups, and the free-device rack" (3), "answers to Q186"; `pq/views/device_chain/device_chain.h` (TX.free-rack sections), `src/views/src/device_chain/free_rack.cpp`, `wiring.h` / `wiring.cpp`, `pq/views/project_patch/project_patch_view.h` (`FreeRack`), `project_patch.h` `LayoutOptions::freeTrack`; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.free-rack-a). **The rack**: the shown track's free devices (`Device::free`, not members of a group in the chain; a free group comes with its contents: `DeviceChainView::inFreeRack`) are no frames of the chain (`device_chain::shownDevices` drops every free device) but blocks of a `FreeRackPanel` over the chain: a tab strip ("Free devices · N", collapse / expand, "+ Add Free Device") and, expanded, `ProjectPatchView(ctx, FreeRack)` — the project patch view embedded like a group panel, its model pruned by `Builder::pruneFree` to those blocks and the wires among them (one band without source, mixer or strip; `ProjectPatch::scoped`; the same set as the circuit's free row), with the circuit's gestures (zoom, pan, wire, faces and panels in place, S-resize, nudges — shared with the circuit —, Delete, Ctrl+G) — and a resize handle. `setRackTrack` follows the Device view's track and refits (`fitEmbedded`; a size change refits a view the user has not moved). An embedded view's `shown()` checks every ancestor: a collapsed rack (or a group panel in a hidden detail view) builds nothing and follows no document change until it shows (`arrange` then syncs). Expanding grows the shell's body splitter by the body (`DeviceChainView::onRackHeight`, set by `installDeviceChain`): the arrangement moves up and the chain keeps its height; collapsing gives it back; the handle moves both. Open and height are `ViewPrefs::freeRackOpen` / `freeRackHeight` (the app's user settings "views"); an open rack grows the splitter once at startup. **Moves**: a chain frame's title bar dragged over the rack is `DropTarget::Kind::Free` (`takeIntoRack`: `ops::setDevicesFree`); a rack block dragged out of its canvas calls `ProjectPatchView::onBlockOutside` (moving: the chain's drop marker, `Impl::trackSlot`; released over the chain: `putIntoChainAt` → `ProjectPatchView::putIntoChain`, one step); inside the rack a block only moves (a nudge) or joins / leaves an opened capsule. A wire dragged out of the rack calls `onWireOutside`: the Device view hands it to the cable layer as from a jack (`onJack` Down + Move: the layer takes the pointer), which drops it on a knob, an input socket or a rack block (`ProjectPatchView::wireTargetAt`); a cable dropped over a badge is still the layer's. **Cables** (`CableLayer`): besides document wires (`Kind::Wire`), the chain's own path (`Kind::Chain`, `DeviceChainView::chainLinks` from `doc::chainStages`: active devices in order, free ones take no part, an unplugged meta device takes nothing in, a switched-off device is passed by; notes / voices amber, audio green; from the chain's left end for the note source or an audio track's clips). Ends (`CableEnd`: point, docked, the way the cable leaves it — Up a jack or a knob, Right an output socket or a rack pin, Left an input socket, Down the collapsed rack's tab): a frame's inputs and outputs (`Impl::Ports`, from the patch it plays: non-const inputs that are not params or `<param>_add`; outs, then exports) are sockets on its left / right edge stacked up from its foot (`inputSockets()`: the drop targets), exports keep their jacks, params their controls, a free device's ends are its pins in the rack (`ProjectPatchView::outputPoint` / `inputPoint`, clamped to the canvas) or a point on the collapsed tab (`tabPoint`, spread in list order), a note-source wire starts at the chain's left end (`notesEnd`). Wires between two rack devices are drawn by the rack's canvas only; a wire into an input (`Cable::input`) is drawn heavier and at least audio-coloured. Every cable has a badge (a chain link's is a 9-px joint whose tooltip reads "A → B · audio (the chain)"; not selectable, Delete does nothing). Cost: the layer recomputes the ends after each layout pass (≈ 0.02–0.03 ms for 18 cables) and damages what moved; nothing runs while nothing moves. Scenes `device-free-rack` (expanded) and `device-free-rack-collapsed`.
- **Node menu** (TX.node-menu, SPEC §15 2026-11-02 "cables and the node menu"; `pq/views/circuit/node_catalog.h`, `node_menu.h`, `src/circuit/node_catalog.cpp`, `node_menu.cpp`; `pq/views/project_patch/project_patch_view.h` ("node menu"), `src/project_patch/node_menu_edits.cpp`; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.node-menu-a). **Catalog** (`nodeCatalog(scope, …)`: built once per device library and User Library index snapshot, shared): every atom (`graph::AtomRegistry`; at the project level only those that make a free device alone), every meta type with a face (the analysers under Visualisers), the stock devices and the two racks (`doc::DeviceLibrary::catalog`), the User Library's presets and user devices (the browser's `library::Index`); each `NodeEntry` carries its signal ports, params with defaults and two facts — a MAIN input (the first signal input that drives something: not a setting, a param or its `_add`) and a main output (the first): `mainPortsOf(signature, callee, instrument)`, the one rule every gesture connects by (no per-type cases). `searchNodes`: every term fuzzy (`library::fuzzyScore`) in the name or an alias, or a substring of the description; compatible entries (`NodeFilter`: with an input / output / both) first, the category holding the best match first. **Typed specs**: `nodeSpecValid` / `splitSpec` (`* 2`: the operator and a literal operand); `specDevice(spec)` builds a free device's patch text of that one node through `graph::edit` + `graph::print` (its required inputs as `in`s, its outputs as `out`s; a node without a real first input gets an audio input that drives nothing). Such a user device names no stock device: `DeviceLibrary::resolve` parses it from its text alone (`devices.h` "User devices"). **Widget** (`NodeMenu`, a `ui::Control` in the overlay layer, focused, accepts text): search box, category tabs, grouped list (headers; incompatible rows dimmed after the rest), preview pane; it only chooses (`onPick` / `onSpec`). **Project patch view**: `spawnNode` / `insertOnEdge` / `insertVca` / `spliceIntoEdge` / `dissolve` compose the document's builders on a scratch snapshot (`doc::apply` after each) and dispatch ONE `Batch` (one undo step); the placement is a nudge (view state), and the push-apart is the view's one overlap rule (TX.block-drag, "Project patch view"): the separation solved at once, then each block glides there as a critically damped spring at fixed 240 Hz sub-steps; one re-layout per frame, no frames once there, `setPushAnimated(false)` places them at once. The layout grows a band down to hold its blocks wherever their nudges put them (the bands below move with it). Cable hit tests go through `CircuitCanvas::hitTest` (`ProjectCanvas::edgeAt`), so TX.cables' curves are what Tab / Z / Shift-splice find. The circuit editor's Tab: `CircuitEditor::openAddMenu` → `addAt` (PatchEditor::addNode takes `* 2` and `meta_<type>` too). TX.node-menu-fix1: the menu is drawn in the meta faces' look (theme `face.*` tokens: near-black body, hairlines, the signal cyan and its glow; a line glyph per category; a mini port diagram with jacks) and opens with a `kOpenSeconds` fade (`DrawList::fadeSince`; frames only then), `kPointerGap` right of and below the pointer (left of it without room; never larger than the window); while it is open the view marks where its choice goes (`spawnMark`) and an insert menu shows its cable selected. In the project patch view a new connection (an output's drag, W's backwards cable, the cable held after a Ctrl-release) is the canvas's loose cable, snapped onto the pin / control a release would connect (`ProjectCanvas::updateLoose`); a node spawned from a dropped cable is placed with its main input (backwards: its main output) where the cable was dropped. A document change during a gesture re-resolves the gesture's model indices by identity (`ProjectCanvas::beforeRebuild` / `afterRebuild`; a gesture whose block, band or source went away ends without an edit).
- **Main path** (TX.all-free, SPEC §15 2026-11-03 "everything is free; the chain is just cables"; `pq/document/main_path.h`, `edit_ops.h` "TX.all-free", `src/document/src/edit_paths.cpp`, `lower.h` "THE MAIN PATH"; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.all-free-a). **Document** (schema 17): `Track::devices` is a SET of blocks (its order only where an unplaced block is drawn); the series is ordinary wires. `kMainPort` ("") names a block's main output (`Wire::port`) / main input (`Wire::param`), the main ports by `doc::mainPortsOf` (moved here from `views/circuit/node_catalog`: a signature's chain in / first out, a rack "" / out, a group x / y, a meta type's chain input); a wire from a track's source with port "" is the track's own signal (any kind); `Wire::mixer` (a TrackId, `to` invalid, `param` "") ends a wire at its own track's mixer (`Wire::toMixer`; serialized `"mixer"`). `Wire::mainLink()` = both ends main. `doc::mainPath(p, t)` follows main links from the source (a branch: the oldest link that reaches the mixer, else the oldest; never a block twice; group members never) → `MainPath {blocks, links, complete}`; `flowOrder` (the path then the other blocks), `canonicalWire` (writes "" for main ports by name: every view / op that makes a wire goes through it, so a hand-drawn cable between main ports joins the path), `meta.h` `trackStages` (`chainStages` along the path; `ChainStage::offPath`). **Ops** (one undo step each, the path stays connected): `wireSeries`, `insertOnPath(device, track, slot)` (splice into the path's link at `slot`), `moveOnPath` (dissolve at the old place + splice at the new; rack-chain sources move into the track), `dissolveDevices` (delete, neighbours joined), `takeOffPath` (keep the block, join its neighbours), `spliceIntoWire` (into a path link or any wire: between its ends), `setDeviceGroup`, `connect(Wire)`; `addDevice` / `addMetaDevice` / paste / duplicate splice at their slot, or nowhere with `ops::kOffPath`; `createTrack` / `groupTracks` / `makeDefaultProject` (the master) draw source → … → mixer; group / ungroup (Ctrl+G / circuit) take the series' place on the path and give every wire id back on ungroup (the same document). The instrument-first rule and the chain order rule are gone (instruments stay MIDI-track-only; a rack's chains are still lists with their rules). **Migration 16 → 17** (`serialize.cpp` `ChainWirer`): each track's non-free devices wired in series (ids above the file's largest: the tracks in order, the returns, the master last), `"free"` dropped, tracks that already have main links left alone; `migrateWireLaws` / `migrateUnitsWires` skip main-port wires. **Lowering**: the path's blocks compile exactly as the chain did (the same IR and NodeIds: every fixture project renders bit for bit as before, `tests/app/test_all_free.cpp`), its links are consumed (Lowered / Inactive past a break); every other wire is a wire; the mixer = the path's end (when complete; else 0) + Σ wires into it. **Views**: the Device view lists the main path (`DeviceChainView` `pathDevices`; a drop splices, a reorder is `moveOnPath`, Delete `dissolveDevices`, the free rack holds exactly the blocks off it); the project patch view draws every document wire as a cable (main links included: `ProjectPatchModel` edges for source / mixer ends), lays a band's spine out in path order with the off-path blocks in a row under it, drops a cable on a mixer, and a block's menu has Take Off Path / Dissolve; the node menu spawns blocks off the path (kOffPath) and Shift-drag / Z splices them. **TX.all-free-fix1** (`lower.h` "NOTES CABLES"; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.all-free-fix1-a; GU.f7: its stock-instrument part — notes cables into stock instruments, `Cables::notesTo`, `kSilent` — went with the stock devices); a notes cable into an off-path meta module's input reads the note's signal of its meaning (Frequency: Hz, Phase: phasor, else its default) in the voice context; off-path racks play by their wires (rack `out` is a wire source, an Audio Effect Rack's `in` a wire target); a cable into another track's mixer is a send-like route (`export m<wire>` → `in m<wire>`, `graph::WireKind::Audio`; TX.cross-track: one closing a cycle between tracks is a loop like any other, below; `AddWire` and the patch view accept it); the document no longer orders instruments in rack chains (`checks.cpp`: instruments only on MIDI tracks); the patch view's free-device menu offers instruments on MIDI bands.
- **Oversampled groups** (TX.group-oversample; `pq/document/model.h` "Oversampled groups", `lower.h` "Oversampled groups", `panel.h` groupOversampleSetting): any group's setting `oversample` (0 … 3: 1× / 2× / 4× / 8×; absent = 1×; SetDeviceSetting: one undo step, Structural) runs its contents at N× the rate. The lowering (`oversampledGroups`, `assignOversampleRegions` after the track patch is built) puts the nodes of every device inside the group (any depth: their call and input adapters, NodeRole::Device k ≤ 5), of the wires between two of them, and of the modulated params of devices inside (k ≥ 16) into ONE `OversampleRegion` per outermost oversampled group, factor = the largest of its nest (regions do not nest; an inner group asking for less is covered, more raises the whole region); the outermost group's own call stays outside (its terminals are the region's edge: what the response displays probe; its In terminal's note phase, groupNotesPatch's `phase`, enters by phase interpolation: the region's `phase` list). infer() puts half-bands on every audio-rate wire across the edge (a cable from outside into a control inside: upsampled when audio rate; params and literals pass as they are); a member whose callee has a region of its own (the Wavetable's live path / Shape mode) stays outside at its own factor. An instrument group's region runs per voice (8 voice lanes per converter). The response through it: Evaluator above. Latency: up + down at DC 3.62 / 4.74 / 5.17 base samples at 2× / 4× / 8× (`groupOversampleLatency`, `dsp::os::roundTripLatency`), shown in the menus; T6.2 reports it (the converters' latency rules) and the project compensates it to the nearest sample (Per-track compile units "Plugin-delay compensation"). **UI**: every group's Device-view frame has an inspector column (`inspectorPanel(group, width, polyphony)`: OVERSAMPLE, and POLYPHONY on an instrument group; 96 px, 150 with polyphony); the EQ and EQ Band panels have an Oversample menu bound to their group's setting; the panel editor's binding menu offers it on any group. Costs: bench/RESULTS.md (TX.group-oversample).
- **Voice contexts / instrument groups** (IG.1-2, SPEC §15 2026-11-04 "instrument groups", 2026-11-06 "single-signal cables"; docs/plans/IG.md; `pq/document/instrument_groups.h` voiceContexts / migrateToInstrumentGroups / ops::wrapInstrumentGroups, `model.h` "Instrument groups" (GroupPolyphony, Wire::single), `commands.h` SetWireSignal, `lower.h` "Voice contexts"): what runs per note is decided by structure alone — the members of a group whose main input is fed notes (an instrument group; GU.f7: the lone stock instruments that were contexts of their own are gone); everything else is single-signal (a meta module on a path reads the newest note). A context is lowered in its notes source's track patch (hosted there when that is another track; a remote placeholder `in v<device>` continues its home's path). Inside, cables are per note unless marked single-signal (reduced by sum / max / newest, as every cable leaving the group); a member whose inside inputs are all single-signal runs once; single-signal and outside signals are broadcast (`shared`). The group's Out sums its per-note terms and adds its single-signal terms once; its voices end by the Envelope reaching Out, else the 5 ms release. Polyphony: the group's settings (`voices`, `mono`, `legato`, `glide`, `steal`; voices and steal reach the engine via ProjectTrack::maxVoices / steal; legato and glide are stored only, IG.4). A new MIDI track: source → instrument group [In → Basic Synth → Out] → mixer (no Envelope: Basic Synth ends its own voices; GU.10: the group is the default instrument's preset "Basic Synth" — In → Phase Accumulator → Wavetable → Envelope → Out — and the Envelope ends the voices). Schema 19: an older file's MIDI instrument / meta voice run is wrapped on load. Loops across a voice sum or through two instrument tracks: Refused, VoiceLoop (IG.3).
- **Nested groups, atom and value blocks, block sets** (GU.2, docs/plans/GU.md "Phases" 2, "Nesting and instrument groups"; `pq/document/model.h` "Groups" (groupContains / groupMembersDeep / outermostGroup, kMaxGroupDepth; TX.insert-group: wireContainer — where a wire is drawn, the innermost group holding both ends, a group terminal counting as inside its group; Z / Tab insert on a cable put the block there), "Atom blocks and value blocks", "Block sets"; `devices.h` atomBlockAllowed / atomBlockPatch / valuePatch / builtinBlockPatch; `edit_ops.h` "block sets" (copyBlocks / remapBlocks / pasteBlocks); `library_files.h` group presets; `lower.h` "NESTED GROUPS", "ATOM AND VALUE BLOCKS"; `pq/views/project_patch/project_patch_view.h` (GU.2 paragraphs); QUESTIONS Q?-GU.2-a). **Nesting**: a group may be a member of a group (Device::group names the innermost); RemoveDevice / MoveDevice of a group take everything inside at any depth; `groupInCircuit` inside a capsule makes a group there (membership only: wires name devices, so nothing is rewired and the sound cannot change), `ungroupInCircuit` gives the members back to the group around it; an instrument group is never nested into a new group. **Instrument rule**: `voiceContexts` decides groups outer first — only the OUTERMOST group fed notes is an instrument group; everything inside it at any depth is its member (`byMember`), a nested group included: it runs per note when its parent's per-note fixpoint says so and then plays `groupVoicePatch()` (its terminals per voice; no voice sum, no end of its own — an Envelope behind a nested Out still ends the voices, the nested group being a pass-through). **Atom block**: Device::patch "atom:<name>" plays `atomBlockPatch(name)` — built from the atom's signature with the graph API (no text), cached per process, every allowed one in the DeviceLibrary's library; its `in`s are the atom's inputs (optional ones keep their default, const ones are settings), its `out`s the atom's outputs; the call flattens to the atom. **Value block**: patch "Value" plays `valuePatch()` (`param value` — GU.4: unranged, so the engine never clamps a curve's units to [0, 1] —, `y = value`): a control whose curve (GU.1) is the range and law. TX.reset-default: `Device::defaultPosition` (value blocks only, saved as "default"; Meta) is where a reset puts its knob — `ops::setDeviceParam(…, nullopt)` (Delete, double-click, a rack macro's reset) sets it; the builders give the module's default, a macro the position it was made at; absent (a block the user places): the declaration's 0. The node menu's atoms spawn atom blocks (typed specs stay one-node user devices) and it offers "Value". **Block sets** (`doc::BlockSet`: devices — top-level first — and the wires among them, ids as they were): `copyBlocks` (a picked group brings everything inside it; note-source and mixer wires never), `remapBlocks` (fresh ids for devices, rack internals, chains, mappings and wires; group tags and wire ends follow — the one place a set's ids change: GU.3's panel bindings join it there), `pasteBlocks` (one step, off the path, top-level blocks into a capsule). A **group preset** is a `.pqpreset` whose "devices" hold the group first and everything inside it and whose "wires" hold their wires (`serializeGroupPreset`, `PresetFile::groupPreset`); the browser's load and the node menu paste it. **Views**: `groupNode` is recursive (a capsule node, then In, its members — a nested group's capsule and contents, or its closed block —, Out; `capsules` in that order so placement runs outer → inner and growth inner → outer; `moveKids` / the prunes / `checkAgainstGraph` recurse); drops go into the innermost opened capsule under the pointer (never one inside the dragged block); an instrument group's capsule has the instruments' accent, a heavier frame and "Instrument · N voices" (Node::instrument / voices, from voiceContexts); nested capsule bodies step lighter per level. Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V (`ProjectPatchView::copySelection` / `pasteAt`, `ViewContext::blockClipboard`), the block menu's Copy / Save as Group Preset (`Library::saveGroupPreset`: Groups/<name>.pqpreset). Scene `project-patch-nested`.
- **Group panels** (GU.3, docs/plans/GU.md "Concepts", "Panels and the panel window", "Phases" 3; `pq/document/panel.h`, `model.h` Device::panel, `commands.h` SetGroupPanel, `edit_ops.h` "group panels"; `pq/views/panel/element.h`, `group_panel_face.h`; `src/views/src/panel/`; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.3-a). **Document**: a group's `Device::panel` (`PanelPtr`, null = none: the group is its mini circuit) = size + `PanelElement`s in paint order: `id` (the panel's own, 1 …), `kind` (the views' registry key; the document never interprets it), rect, `style` (sorted name → number | text; colours are theme tokens), `bindings` (slot 0 the main one; an XY pad's slot 1 its y) of `Binding{kind Control | Setting | Data | Signal | Panel, DeviceId, meta path, name}`, optional `when` (shown while element `element` has one of `values`). Rules: a binding names the group itself (its settings) or a block inside it at any depth (`bindingAllowed`; a Panel binding a group inside); SetGroupPanel (Meta: never lowered) and the reader refuse others; `apply()`'s final pass after every structural command (`finishPanels`) takes off elements whose bindings no longer hold — one undo step restores both; `ops::remapBlocks` remaps bindings on paste (element ids kept). Schema 21 ("panel": {"size", "elements": [{"id", "kind", "rect", "bind", "style", "when"}]}; 20 → 21 changes nothing). An instrument group's polyphony is described by `doc::groupSettings()` (`SettingSpec`: range, step, default, choices, unit) for panels and the inspector; an edit is SetDeviceSetting (Structural: one recompile). **Views**: `panel::Element` (static layers ≤ 2 keyed by kind + style + size = `staticKey`, live layer, element-local pointer / key / text events, `selection()` for conditions) made by `makeElement` from the registry (`registerElement(schema, factory)`; schema = style fields with defaults + binding slots, for GU.5's inspector; generic kinds in elements.cpp / curve_element.cpp; unknown kinds a placeholder); `panel::PanelTarget` (what elements read and edit: `info` → `BoundInfo` {label, curve, choices, switch, steps, setting, generation}, `position`, `format` / `parse`, gestures begin / setPosition / setCurve / end / cancel, `setOnce`, `reset`) with `makeDocPanelTarget(ctx, group, menuHost)` (the group's track indexed once per snapshot; resolutions kept while their block is the same object; a control drag = one store gesture of param writes; a setting previewed, committed once; curves through `ops::setParamCurve`); `panel::GroupPanelFace` (a `Face`: source = `docPanelSource` or a fixed panel; elements rebuilt only when the panel pointer changes; visibility per paint; static layers from the process-wide element tile cache, built in `prepare()`, drawn as one quad each; input routed to the element under the pointer, keys / text to the last pressed); `panel::PanelHost` (a widget hosting any Face fitted to its rect: the Device view's group frame and inspector, GU.5's window); `panel::inspectorPanel(group, width)` (the generated polyphony panel). **Hosts**: a closed group with a panel is `Node::groupPanel` (sized to the panel; no miniature), its SceneFace path `"group-panel:<w>x<h>"` made by `ProjectPatchView::makePanel`; the Device view's `GroupFrame` shows the panel (else the mini circuit) and, for an instrument group, the inspector column at its left. Scenes `project-patch-group-panel`, `device-group-panel-elements` (`project_patch::buildPanelProject`). **GU.5 panel window** (`pq/views/panel/panel_window.h`, `src/views/src/panel/panel_window.cpp`, `src/views/src/project_patch/panel_windows.cpp`): `panel::PanelWindow` is a child widget of the main `ProjectPatchView` over its canvas (not an overlay popup): title bar (Edit toggle, + send, minimise, close), a `PanelHost` with the group's `GroupPanelFace` (play), the `PanelEditor` over it (edit: select / move / resize / snap 4 px / align / delete; drags preview a local panel and commit ONE `SetGroupPanel` at the release) and the generated `PanelInspector` (kind, binding menu, visibility, geometry, the kind's `StyleSpec`s, a curve element on the same binding). Window place / size / mode / minimised: `doc::ViewState::panelWindows` (`PanelWindowView`, saved as the view's optional "panels"; no schema bump). Send: `ProjectPatchView::selectedControl` (set by every left press: `controlAt`), `sendToPanel` builds elements with `panel::defaultElement` at `panel::freePlace` (or the drop point); P, the window's +, block / output-cable drops on a window. `PanelTarget` gained `driven` (wired controls, indexed per wire list: the modulation ring) and `connect` (a mod source dropped on a control: `ops::connect`). Link lines: `ProjectPatchView::panelLinks` / `controlWorld`, painted in the canvas overlay. `GroupPanelFace` answers `paramAt` / `controlAnchor` for "<name>@<id>" (the closed group's port keys), so the model anchors a closed panelled group's wired inner params on their elements. **GU.5b modules look as before; an opened group is a circuit** (docs/plans/GU.md 5a; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.5b-a): rich element kinds registered beside the generic ones (`src/views/src/panel/rich_elements.cpp`): `dial` (the VCA's round 360° knob, its readout inside, every interior point of its control's curve a draggable MARKER — `dsp::CurvePreset::Vca` splits its outers at ±10 / ±100 with `dsp::splitCurveAt`, the same function —, a chevron opening a floating popup: range, curve box, presets), `arc` (a control on an arc round (cx, cy), hit in its band and sector only, ticks at listed values through the curve, a corner readout; a Cents curve reads in semitones), `orbit` (the Phase Accumulator's centre: orbit, hub lit by slot 1, voices of slot 0's signal through `PanelTarget::voices`). `Element` gained `outline()` (the bottom element's outline makes the panel round: `GroupPanelFace::shape`, `panel::panelShape`, `Node::panelRound`; no rectangular plate), `popupRect` / `paintPopup` / `setPopupOverFace` (forwarded by `GroupPanelFace` as `Face::popupRect`, hit before everything), `pollVoices` / `animating` / `voiceReader` / `liveRects`. The PA and VCA panels are built from these (`doc::modules.h`); the PA's Pitch curve holds ±12 semitones over the middle half of the travel (straight segments). `ProjectPatchView` closes a group the first time it sees it when it has a panel (`seenGroups_`; modules spawn closed). **Opened group = circuit** (`project_patch` model `circuitLayout`): the capsule's contents laid out by `graph::autoLayout` on a proxy patch (In terminal layer 0, Out last), the wires among them hanging cables like every other (TX.cable-look; the layout's routes are not used), blocks inside keep their pins on their edges (no orbiting: their cables hang from the pins); a closed group's miniature draws its wires hanging too (`cableRest`, 8 points). TX.cable-look anchoring: a closed panelled group's main input whose signal inside goes only into one `add` atom whose other feed is a single value block (the PA's In → `hz + freq` ← Frequency) is anchored on that value's control (`addedControl`; `Port::anchorKey` = "value@<id>", the key the scene gives `SceneWire::control`): visual only; an atom block anywhere is drawn as its circuit symbol (`Node::symbol`: `circuit::describe` of its atom; `pinOffset(n, i, input)`), a value block as a small knob (`Node::valueKnob` + `groupPanel`: a `GroupPanelFace` of `panel::valueKnobPanel`, SceneFace path `"value-knob:<w>x<h>"`; a wire into `value` ends on the knob). `Knob::hit` is its dial (label and readout are the panel's: a value block is moved by them). **GU.6 embed and break apart** (QUESTIONS Q?-GU.6-a): a child group left whole on its parent's panel is an element of kind `doc::kEmbedKind` ("panel") with slot 0 `doc::panelBinding(child)`; `doc::embedFit` fits the child's panel into its rect (aspect kept, centred). Views: `EmbedElement` (`src/views/src/panel/embed_element.cpp`) holds a nested `GroupPanelFace` of the child's panel (`PanelTarget::childPanel`, a forwarding target onto the parent's: every binding inside the child is inside the parent) and forwards paint, pointer, keys, text, popups, voices, outline and static tiles at the fit; nests to any depth. `Element` gained `bind(target, host)` (called by the face on build / attach), `needsPrepare` / `prepare`, `controlAt(local)` and `anchorFor(binding)` (so `GroupPanelFace::paramAt` / `controlAnchor` / `bindingAnchor` find controls inside embeds); `panel::panelShape(panel, list)` takes a bottom embed's round outline from its child. Send: `ProjectPatchView::controlsOf(group)` = its Panel binding when it has a panel (P / + / drops embed it at the child panel's size). `ops::breakApartPanelElements(group, ids)` replaces embeds by copies of the child's elements at the same places (fresh ids, conditions renumbered; nested embeds stay embeds): one step; the panel window's Break Apart (menu, Ctrl+Shift+G). A child moved out of the parent loses its embed by the GU.3 prune rule.
- **Group panels** (GU.3, docs/plans/GU.md "Concepts", "Panels and the panel window", "Phases" 3; `pq/document/panel.h`, `model.h` Device::panel, `commands.h` SetGroupPanel, `edit_ops.h` "group panels"; `pq/views/panel/element.h`, `group_panel_face.h`; `src/views/src/panel/`; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.3-a). **Document**: a group's `Device::panel` (`PanelPtr`, null = none: the group is its mini circuit) = size + `PanelElement`s in paint order: `id` (the panel's own, 1 …), `kind` (the views' registry key; the document never interprets it), rect, `style` (sorted name → number | text; colours are theme tokens), `bindings` (slot 0 the main one; an XY pad's slot 1 its y) of `Binding{kind Control | Setting | Data | Signal | Panel, DeviceId, meta path, name}`, optional `when` (shown while element `element` has one of `values`). Rules: a binding names the group itself (its settings) or a block inside it at any depth (`bindingAllowed`; a Panel binding a group inside); SetGroupPanel (Meta: never lowered) and the reader refuse others; `apply()`'s final pass after every structural command (`finishPanels`) takes off elements whose bindings no longer hold — one undo step restores both; `ops::remapBlocks` remaps bindings on paste (element ids kept). Schema 21 ("panel": {"size", "elements": [{"id", "kind", "rect", "bind", "style", "when"}]}; 20 → 21 changes nothing). An instrument group's polyphony is described by `doc::groupSettings()` (`SettingSpec`: range, step, default, choices, unit) for panels and the inspector; an edit is SetDeviceSetting (Structural: one recompile). **Views**: `panel::Element` (static layers ≤ 2 keyed by kind + style + size = `staticKey`, live layer, element-local pointer / key / text events, `selection()` for conditions) made by `makeElement` from the registry (`registerElement(schema, factory)`; schema = style fields with defaults + binding slots, for GU.5's inspector; generic kinds in elements.cpp / curve_element.cpp; unknown kinds a placeholder); `panel::PanelTarget` (what elements read and edit: `info` → `BoundInfo` {label, curve, choices, switch, steps, setting, generation}, `position`, `format` / `parse`, gestures begin / setPosition / setCurve / end / cancel, `setOnce`, `reset`) with `makeDocPanelTarget(ctx, group, menuHost)` (the group's track indexed once per snapshot; resolutions kept while their block is the same object; a control drag = one store gesture of param writes; a setting previewed, committed once; curves through `ops::setParamCurve`); `panel::GroupPanelFace` (a `Face`: source = `docPanelSource` or a fixed panel; elements rebuilt only when the panel pointer changes; visibility per paint; static layers from the process-wide element tile cache, built in `prepare()`, drawn as one quad each; input routed to the element under the pointer, keys / text to the last pressed); `panel::PanelHost` (a widget hosting any Face fitted to its rect: the Device view's group frame and inspector, GU.5's window); `panel::inspectorPanel(group, width)` (the generated polyphony panel). **Hosts**: a closed group with a panel is `Node::groupPanel` (sized to the panel; no miniature), its SceneFace path `"group-panel:<w>x<h>"` made by `ProjectPatchView::makePanel`; the Device view's `GroupFrame` shows the panel (else the mini circuit) and, for an instrument group, the inspector column at its left. Scenes `project-patch-group-panel`, `device-group-panel-elements` (`project_patch::buildPanelProject`). **GU.5 panel window** (`pq/views/panel/panel_window.h`, `src/views/src/panel/panel_window.cpp`, `src/views/src/project_patch/panel_windows.cpp`): `panel::PanelWindow` is a child widget of the main `ProjectPatchView` over its canvas (not an overlay popup): title bar (Edit toggle, + send, minimise, close), a `PanelHost` with the group's `GroupPanelFace` (play), the `PanelEditor` over it (edit: select / move / resize / snap 4 px / align / delete; drags preview a local panel and commit ONE `SetGroupPanel` at the release) and the generated `PanelInspector` (kind, binding menu, visibility, geometry, the kind's `StyleSpec`s, a curve element on the same binding). Window place / size / mode / minimised: `doc::ViewState::panelWindows` (`PanelWindowView`, saved as the view's optional "panels"; no schema bump). Send: `ProjectPatchView::selectedControl` (set by every left press: `controlAt`), `sendToPanel` builds elements with `panel::defaultElement` at `panel::freePlace` (or the drop point); P, the window's +, block / output-cable drops on a window. `PanelTarget` gained `driven` (wired controls, indexed per wire list: the modulation ring) and `connect` (a mod source dropped on a control: `ops::connect`). Link lines: `ProjectPatchView::panelLinks` / `controlWorld`, painted in the canvas overlay. `GroupPanelFace` answers `paramAt` / `controlAnchor` for "<name>@<id>" (the closed group's port keys), so the model anchors a closed panelled group's wired inner params on their elements.
- **Racks are parallel groups** (GU.9 v2, docs/plans/GU.md "One hierarchy" 9; `pq/document/racks.h`, `edit_ops.h` "Racks"; `src/views/src/device_chain/rack_frame.cpp`; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.9b-a). T4.8's `Rack` / `Chain` / `MacroMapping`, `ParamKind::ChainMixer`, the rack lowering and the rack file format are gone: a rack is **derived from the wiring**, never stored. `doc::findRacks(project, track)` -> `RackInfo`s: sibling groups (same `Device::group`) with exactly one wire into their main input and one main wire out, sharing both ends, are a rack's chains (two or more; one when it holds a chain mixer: what Add Rack / Ctrl+G make); chains ordered by `Track::devices`; the rack's id is its lowest chain id; an Instrument Rack's chains are fed notes (each an instrument group of the track's voice engine: voicing does not double). Inside a chain, found by ROLE (GU.f1: the setting `rackRole` = `doc::RackRole`, written when the block is made; a rename never changes it, a user group named "Mixer" has none): the **Mixer** group (value blocks Volume dB -> gain / Pan / Gate + atoms `mul sub add min pack gain` = T4.8's `gain(x, pack(gl, gr))`), the **zone groups** (Key / Velocity / Chain Zone: Lo / Fade Lo / Fade Hi / Hi value blocks, a voice atom block or the selector, atoms -> `gain`; made only while the zone is on), the series In -> [instrument -> key / velocity zones -> single-signal ->] effects -> [chain zone] -> Mixer -> Out. Rack level: **macros** = value blocks with setting `macro` = slot wired into the chains (a mapping = a Travel wire, amount max - min, the target's knob = min), the **selector** value block (role Selector), **variations** = `var<k>` settings of the macro blocks; a chain's on / solo = group settings, the Mixer's Gate written by the edits. Every rack edit (`ops::addRack`, `addChain`, `addToChain`, `moveIntoChain`, `deleteChains`, `setChainMixer`, `setChainZone`, `mapMacro`, `storeVariation`, `groupDevices` ...) emits ordinary block / wire / param / setting commands (one step each); `relay` re-lays a chain's series; `splice` / `detach` keep a rack's feed and sum shared (a parallel branch is never bypassed). Lowering: nothing of its own; generic fixes it needed: a nested group's Out-terminal wires are not its inputs (the per-note fixpoint), voice terminals are atom blocks (outside a voice their `__voiceless` stand-in gives 0), `pack` is an atom block. Views: the Device view frames a rack chain on the path as the rack (`RackFrame`, `FrameBase::derived()`: refreshed on every edit); the other chains, macros and selector are neither free-rack blocks nor cables (`heldByRack`); rack options (shown macros / chains, count) are view state; the circuit stacks a rack's other chains under its chain on the path (`Node::rack`).
- **Built-in modules** (GU.4, docs/plans/GU.md "Phases" 4; `pq/document/modules.h`, `src/document/src/modules.cpp`; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.4-a). The Phase Accumulator and the VCA are GROUP PRESETS of atom and value blocks, built in code (`ModuleKind`, `moduleInfos` / `findModule` by key or name, catalogue items `module:phase_acc` / `module:vca`, `moduleBlocks`): **Phase Accumulator** = In → `add` "hz + freq" ← Frequency → `mul` "× ratio" ← `cents_to_hz` "2^(pitch/1200)" ← Pitch, 1 → `phasor` → `add` "+ phase" ← Phase → `frac` → `mul` "× amp" ← Amplitude → Out (value blocks Frequency 0 – 20 kHz Exp0 k 14 **signed**, Phase 0 – 1 **wrap**, Amplitude 0 – 4, Pitch ±4800 c; `phaseAccumulatorCurve`), panel 120 × 136 of four knobs; **VCA** = In → `mul` "×" ← Value (its knob position on `vcaCurve(min, max, zone)`, the `Vca` curve preset) → Out, panel 120 × 172: a 360° knob with its readout inside and a curve editor on the Value block's curve. `phaseAccumulatorBlocks` / `vcaBlocks` take options (ids, the group they sit in, positions, curves) and return roles. Spawned by the node menu (`circuit::NodeItem::Module`, project scope; Z inserts a VCA), Add Free Device, the browser (Meta Modules) and `library_load` (pasted; a module cannot hot-swap a device). GU.f7: the load conversion of the retired `meta:phase_acc` / `meta:vca` devices is deleted (such a file is refused); Operator's FM is a per-voice cable into the next row's Phase. Lowering: a GroupNotes In feeds an atom or group block its `Frequency` meaning (the note's Hz) when the block reads no meta param of its own (`lower.cpp`). The view shows a group open by default; the scenes close the modules (their panels).
- **Engine modules and rich elements** (GU.7, docs/plans/GU.md "Phases" 7; `pq/document/modules.h` "modules around a native engine", `pq/document/panel.h` "Displays tapped by the lowering", `src/views/src/panel/engine_elements.cpp`; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.7-a). The **Wavetable**, **Envelope**, **Oscilloscope**, **Spectrum** and **Spectrogram** modules are groups around ONE native engine (`meta:<type>`, a leaf: `engineModuleBlocks`, `enginePanel`, `moduleOfEngine`; In → the engine's chain input, `out` → Out; catalogue items `module:wavetable` …, the bare engines hidden from the project's spawn lists, still in rack chains). Their panels are the old faces rebuilt from elements: the RICH kinds `drawtools`, `harmonics`, `wavetable` (table display + position slider + keyframe markers), `warpmode`, `envelope`, `scope`, `spectrum`, `spectrogram` — each a SECTION of the engine's face class (`pq/views/meta`), scaled into its rect, events routed in face units where the face has a control in the section — plus generic knobs / sliders for the plain controls (phase, fold, warp, sync, amount) and a bottom `shape` with `plate` 1 (the plate's corner); `meter` (new: L / R RMS + peak). Bindings: Data (an engine: its params, settings, data through `DocTarget`; its voices `ViewContext::faceVoices(device, path)`, its analysis `analysisFeed(device)`) or, for the displays, Signal. `PanelTarget::engineFace(binding, type, host)` makes the face and SHARES it among a panel's elements bound alike (one state); `PanelTarget::signalFeed` (the meter). **Lowering taps**: `panelTaps(panel)` (kind → `ElementTap`: `orbit` Voice, displays Signal; Signal binding at slot 0) — the track patch gets `voice_tap(x, slot)` / `signal_tap(beat, note, L, R, slot)` on that output (`panelTapsOf` in lower.cpp; slot `metaSlotOf(device, tapPath(kind, port), 0)`, listed in metaResources with type ""), so the PA's orbit dots (frac's output) and a scope on any signal work in the app (`ViewContext::setSignalTapSource` → the link's `metaSignalTap`; `AnalysisHub::feed(device, path)`); SetGroupPanel is Structural when the taps change. `GroupPanelFace` routes the wheel to elements and polls while any element's reader is live.
- **The Wavetable Synth; style schemas** (GU.8; `pq/document/modules.h` "the example instrument", `pq/views/panel/style_schemas.h`; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.8-a). `doc::wavetableSynthBlocks` builds an instrument group (`makeInstrumentGroup`) of modules only — Osc PA → Wavetable → Envelope, two LFO pairs (PA with In unplugged → Wavetable `os` 1 → `mul` ← Depth value block → a Units wire into the Wavetable engine's `position` / the Osc's Pitch value block) — and a panel of an unbound `tabs` element, the Pitch knob and per tab the child modules' embeds (`when` on the tabs). `ModuleInfo::instrument` marks it: listed under Instruments (browser, node menu) and spawned by `ops::pasteBlocksAtNotes` (pasteBlocks + `splice` at slot 0 when a MIDI track's path is empty; one step). Style schemas are views-side, outside the document: `panel::StyleSchemas::shared()` (built-ins per kind + the user's, one JSON file set by the app: `<User Library>\Styles\schemas.json`), `schemaFields` (a kind's Number / Choice / Token fields; Text fields are content), `applyStyleSchema`; the panel window's inspector rows "Schema" (apply to the selection: one `editSelected` step) and "Save as".

- **Stock devices as group presets; Auto Filter and EQ Eight retired** (GU.10, docs/plans/GU.md "One hierarchy"; `pq/document/modules.h` "the stock devices as group presets"; QUESTIONS Q?-GU.10-a). **Utility**, **Saturator**, the **LFO** and **Operator** are modules (`ModuleKind::Utility` …, catalogue items `module:utility` …; `utilityBlocks`, `saturatorBlocks`, `lfoBlocks`, `operatorBlocks`) built in code from atom blocks, value blocks and the PA / Wavetable / Envelope modules, each with a panel laid out like its old face; the default instrument of a new MIDI track is `defaultInstrumentBlocks` (the instrument group "Basic Synth": In → Phase Accumulator → Wavetable → Envelope → Out; `ops::createTrack`). Switches are value blocks 0 / 1 and choices muxes of real indices (an atom block's bool input sums its wires as reals); `channel` is an atom block (Utility's L / R; its index a setting). **No stock device ships**: `stockDevices()` is empty in the app; a test process names the STAND-IN devices in `PQ_STAND_IN_DEVICES` (`kStandInDevicesEnv`; the shared test main sets it to `tests/stand_in_devices`, a `devices.txt` and copies of the retired patches and panel descriptions; a stdlib directory overrides a stand-in by relative path), so the chain / wire / group / lowering / view tests keep their devices; `tests/support/stand_in_synth.h` `useBasicSynth` turns a new track's default into the IG.1-2 layout (Basic Synth in the group) those tests were written for, `makeLoneInstrument` likewise for the scenes. **Retired** (`retired/`, not built, `retired/README.md`): the seven stock `.pqp` / `panel.json`, EQ Eight's and Auto Filter's displays and math (views), their tests, analysis-tap tests, device-panel scenes and goldens, `pq_bench_devices`. `ops::deleteDevices` skips a block inside another listed group (it goes with the group). The panel embed (`ForwardTarget`) forwards `engineFace` / `signalFeed` (an embedded engine module draws its face: Operator's Envelope tabs).
- **Band and EQ modules; structural value blocks into const inputs** (EQ.3, docs/plans/EQ.md "Structure", "Ops"; `pq/document/modules.h` "the band and the EQ", `edit_ops.h` "EQ.3", `src/document/src/edit_eq.cpp`; `lower.h` "Structural value blocks"). **EQ Band** (`module:eq_band`, `eqBandBlocks`): In → `svf` → Out with value blocks found by their `bandRole` setting (Frequency 0 – 20 kHz on one Exp segment of bend ln 2001, so the knob travel is the response axis x = log(1 + f/10 Hz) exactly; Q 0.1 – 40 octave; Gain ±24 dB → the svf's linear gain; Stages 1 – 64 stepped; Type 0 – 7 stepped in `SvfMode` order, menu `kBandTypeItems`); Type and Stages wire into the svf's `mode` / `stages` const inputs. **EQ** (`module:eq`, `eqBlocks`): value blocks Mode (Stereo | L/R | M/S), Output, Edit Chain (`eqRole`), chain groups A / B (`bandChain` 1 / 2) of bands in series (default A: low shelf 100 Hz, bells 400 Hz / 2.5 kHz, high shelf 8 kHz; B empty), the matrix as muxes on the literal Mode (Stereo: In → A, nothing of B or the matrix scheduled; L/R: channels → A, B → pack; M/S: (L ± R)/2 → A, B → pack(A + B, A − B)), then a smoothed Output gain. `eqInfo` / `bandInfo` / `eqOfBand` read them by role (the series from the chain's In). Ops (each one step, the strip laid out again in it: `layEqStrip` replaces the elements tagged `eq: strip`): `addBand` (in frequency order; > 32 per chain refused), `removeBand`, `setBandType`, `setBandStages`, `setEqMode` (the first split copies A into an empty B), `setBandBypass` (the band group off), `setBandSolo` (`bandSolo` / `soloRestore` settings: saved, undoable). Panel (EQ.4: below): the `response` display (its magnitude, phase and delay areas: EQ.4d), the Mode menu (through `ops::setEqMode`), three tabs on the Edit Chain block (`A|B` the strip follows, shown when Mode ≠ Stereo, under `L|R` / `M|S` shown per mode), the Output knob, per band a column (Type menu, Hz, Q ("Q 0.71"), a vertical gain slider for the gain types, Stages ("×16") for the others; EQ.4b: the `number` element's style fields `decimals` (≥ 0: the value in its units with that many decimals; −1: the control's own readout), `prefix`, `suffix`). EQ.4b: the band's own panel (a lone band, or a band drawn closed in the circuit) has the `response` display (its output and input, the analyser; its three areas) over its column laid flat. `Visibility` value `kWhenHidden` (−1): shown while the source element is hidden (chain A's columns in Stereo). **Lowering**: a stepped / constant value block wired straight into an atom block's `real const` input sets it to its knob value (a literal at the call, as a setting; `WirePlan::constFeeds`); such a block is structural even when driven (`lowersAsLiteral` true; a wire or lane into it is ignored by the const input); a wire into a const input from anything else stays Invalid. A **literal mux** (index one wire from a literal block, its picked input one wire) lowers as that wire (`WirePlan::folded`: the wires out of it start at the picked source), before type inference, so its branches keep their own widths (the EQ runs A on the stereo In in Stereo, mono in L/R and M/S). Role / solo settings are allowed on value blocks / groups (`rackValueSetting` / `rackGroupSetting`). **`PanelTarget::run(EditBuilder)`** (element.h): an element's structural edit, built from the document and the store's ids and dispatched by the doc target through the store (one undo step; a refusal is a notice).
- **Tracks are not walls** (TX.cross-track, SPEC §15 2026-11-03; `lower.h` "LOOPS BETWEEN TRACKS", "CROSS-TRACK NOTES CABLES"; QUESTIONS Q?-TX.cross-track-a): (1) a wire that closes a delay-free loop through other tracks (a cable into another track's mixer, a modulation / input wire, a send) gets the circuit editor's rule: the closing wire (id order; sends first) has the z1, the partition merges the loop's tracks into one unit and the scheduler runs the loop's SCC as one per-sample Loop region (the rest stays block-wise; `WireRefusal::Cycle` is gone; refused only through two instrument tracks or through the voices — TX.voice-loop). (2) IG.1-2 replaced the guests: a notes cable from MIDI track A into a block on another track makes it (an instrument group) a voice context of A's, lowered in A's patch and wired to its own track by ordinary cross-track wires (see "Voice contexts").Tracks without such cables or loops lower to the same IR.
- **Preferences window** (T3.18, `pq/views/prefs/audio_prefs.h`, `src/prefs/`): `PreferencesWindow` (a `ui::Popover` centred in the overlay) = Live's Preferences → Audio tab: driver type, audio device (ASIO; + Hardware Setup = the driver's control panel) or input / output devices (WASAPI), input / output channels, sample rate, buffer size, latency readout, test tone (on, volume, frequency), CPU usage, status line. Every choice is applied at once through the plain-data `AudioPrefsPort` (the app's `AudioDeviceManager`; `FakeAudioPrefsPort` for tests and scenes); a driver / device change keeps rate and buffer when the device can do them, else the nearest it can. A chosen device that is not connected stays listed ("(not connected)"). `installPreferences(shell)` binds `app.preferences` (Ctrl+, : a ViewContext view action, enabled while `ctx.setAudioPrefs(port)` is set; pressed again: closes). Closed, the window does not exist; open, a 2 Hz timer polls status and CPU and damages only changed readouts. Scenes `audio-prefs`, `audio-prefs-wasapi`.
- **App** (`src/app/src/shell_app.*`): the window loop treats the shell as a widget tree and calls `ShellApp::handleEvent` (SDL file-dialog results marshalled through a user event, quit confirmation, `SDL_StartTextInput`/`StopTextInput` following text focus; Esc does not quit), `onFramePresented` (first-frame log against `main()` entry and process creation; `--idle-check`) and `exitCode`. `pqdaw --project <dir>` opens it; `--scene shell` starts with a new project.
- **App** (`src/app/src/shell_app.*`): the window loop treats the shell as a widget tree and calls `ShellApp::handleEvent` (SDL file-dialog results marshalled through a user event, quit confirmation, `SDL_StartTextInput`/`StopTextInput` following text focus; Esc does not quit), `onFramePresented` (first-frame log against `main()` entry and process creation; `--idle-check`) and `exitCode`. **Startup (T0.7-fix6)**: `openWindowApp` creates the Vulkan instance + device on a worker (with `Window::platformInstanceExtensions()`, checked against the window's list afterwards) while SDL starts video and creates the window, then loads fonts + theme (`createSceneText` on an atlas handed to the renderer through `RendererOptions::atlas`) on a second worker while the swapchain and renderer are made; `createSceneTextures(renderer, text)` finishes. The renderer's `VkPipelineCache` is seeded from `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pqdaw\vk_pipeline_cache.bin` (`pq/app/pipeline_cache.h`: our header with size + FNV-1a 64 checksum around the driver's blob; the renderer also checks the blob's own header against vendor/device/pipelineCacheUUID) and saved after the first frame when it changed. `markStartupPhase` / `addStartupNote` (`pq/app/startup.h`) record ms since process creation; `PQ_STARTUP_LOG=1` prints the table at the first frame, `PQ_STARTUP_EXIT=1` quits after it, `PQ_STARTUP_SERIAL=1` restores the serial order (A/B); `tools/bench/startup.ps1` runs N starts and prints the median. `pqdaw --project <dir>` opens it; `--scene shell` starts with a new project.

### Analysis displays (TX.spectrum: `pq/dsp/transfer.h`, `signal_tap` in `pq/dsp/atoms.h`, `pq/views/devices/analysis.h`, `pq/views/devices/spectrum_plot.h`, `pq/meta/spectrum.h`; TX.scope: `pq/dsp/scope.h`, `pq/meta/oscilloscope.h`; TX.spectrogram: `pq/dsp/spectrogram.h`, `pq/meta/spectrogram.h`, `pq/ui/texture_pool.h`)
- **Taps (audio thread)**: `signal_tap(inL, inR, outL, outR, slot)`, a global sink (8 lanes: summed) that copies its four inputs into the `SignalTap` resource `slot` (a `pq::TapRing` of 4 × `ProjectLink::kAnalysisTapFrames`) while a reader is registered; unwatched one relaxed load per call. TMM.13-fix1 L3: during a hot swap's crossfade the engine runs the outgoing image under `pq::TapWriteMute` (a thread-local flag `TapRing::write` checks), so only the incoming image writes each block once. Who has one: an active **analysed stock device** in a chain (`doc::StockDevice::analysed`: EQ Eight, Auto Filter; `doc::analysedDevice(patch)`, edited forks too) gets it from the lowering next to its call (`lower.h` "Analysis taps": NodeIds `nodeIdFor(Device, id, 8 … 13)`, slot `LowerOptions::metaSlotOf(device, doc::kAnalysisTapPath, 0)`, listed in `LoweredProject::metaResources` with type "" and kind `meta::ResourceKind::SignalTap`); the **Spectrum meta module** (`meta::spectrumType()`, id `spectrum`: out = in, no op between the ports, its request 0 a SignalTap) taps its input. Without `metaSlotOf` (tools, tests) there is no tap. The link creates the rings in `syncMetaSlots` (`MetaSlot::ring`, `Resources::signalTaps`, kept alive by every image through `keepAlive`), answers `ProjectLink::deviceAnalysisTap(device)` (its own `#analysis` request, else a chain meta device's request 0) and posts `onAnalysisTaps` to the UI thread after a compile that made new rings (the app: `ViewContext::analysisTapsChanged()` → `view_change::kAnalysis`: device chains wake their panels' displays and faces, the project patch view its faces).
- **Analysis (one worker per watched tap, `devices::SpectrumFeed`)**: computes only what the visible displays acquired (`kWantOutput` | `kWantInput` | `kWantResponse`): the output spectrum (`dsp::SpectrumAnalyzer` on (L + R) / 2, peak hold), the input's, and the measured response (`dsp::TransferAnalyzer`, input mid → output mid: |H| and a confidence per analyser point: nothing on silence, unexcited bands or unrelated signals). A 2-channel tap is output only (the stand-alone device preview). Hidden: the worker blocks and the tap has no reader. Settled (levels below the floor, nothing confident, silent data): the UI stops its frames; a **watched** feed (the project's) keeps its reader, skips silent chunks and looks every 100 ms, and posts a wake when sound arrives (`setWakePost` → UI thread → `runWakers`). `devices::AnalysisHub` (owned by the `ViewContext`, `setAnalysisSource(tapOf, rate)` / `analysisFeed(device)` / `analysisHub()`) makes one watched feed per device tap and supersedes a feed whose tap changed (kept while a display holds it); `adopt` (scenes, tests).
- **Display (UI thread, `devices::SpectrumPlot`)**: the one spectrum + response component every signal display draws with, PQ Meter's look (its header lists each layer: tinted low/mid/high backdrop, 12 dB and frequency rules, Ice-by-level fill + level³ bloom + body + core, peak hold through the knot spline, the measured response in violet weighted by its confidence, priority-placed labels on backing pads, the hover readout with nearest note in C3 = 261.626 Hz naming, 45 ms ease). It paints into a draw list at a device rect (panels and vector faces alike), holds its feed while painted (`wake()` at paint start shows the feed's snapshot at once), and lets go when hidden (`frame(now, shown)`). Users: the panel displays `spectrum`, `eq` (EQ Eight: exact curve from the band coefficients, the measured overlay behind the "Measured" chip), `filter` (Auto Filter: the output spectrum behind its exact curve, the same chip; `DevicePanel::showMeasuredResponse`), offered where the target's feed carries the device's input (`DeviceTarget::outputSpectrum`: the Device view's and the project patch view's document targets ask the hub); the Spectrum face (`Face::setAnalysis(feedOf, wakeHost)` / `wantsAnalysis`: FaceDisplay and `CircuitCanvas::FaceBinding::analysis` bind it; its "voices" are the feed's frames, so the hosts poll it ≤ 60 Hz only while it moves; PEAK / FREEZE chips).
- **Oscilloscope (TX.scope: `pq/meta/oscilloscope.h`, `pq/dsp/scope.h`, `src/views/src/meta/scope_face.cpp`; Q?-TX.scope-a)**: the meta type `oscilloscope` (out = in, like `spectrum`) taps `signal_tap(transport.beat, note, L, R)` into its request 0 — the transport's beat position (the Beat timebase), `voice_lowest(voice.hz)` when its expansion is voiced, else 0 (the note-locked period), the signal. The sink has no live output, so the beat keeps nothing awake (the region sleeps as a Spectrum's does). Its display options are the instance's data (a record of numbers; never recompiles). The feed keeps the tap's history when a consumer wants `kWantScope` (`SpectrumFeed::scopeEnd` / `scopeRead`: 2^18 frames per channel, allocated by the first scope consumer; "none" = NaN in the clock / note channels, 0 in the signal; a scope-only consumer computes no spectrum; it settles after 2.2 s of silence on L and R; with no data it appends paced silence, as the spectrum does); the transfer analyser is now made when first wanted. The face reads the window its timebase needs at each poll (≤ 60 Hz while the feed moves), runs `dsp::ScopeTracker` (PQ Meter's timebases, locks, McLeod detector, shape alignment, edge trigger) or `dsp::ScopeBeat` (phase slots against the tapped beat), and paints with `ui::scopeTrace` / `scopeEnvelope`. `FacePainter::textAligned` (TX.scope) places a run by its measured width.
- **Spectrogram (TX.spectrogram: `pq/meta/spectrogram.h`, `pq/dsp/spectrogram.h`, `src/views/src/meta/spectrogram_face.cpp`; Q?-TX.spectrogram-a)**: the meta type `spectrogram` (out = in) taps `signal_tap(transport.beat, 0, L, R)` into its request 0; options are the instance's data (a record of numbers), as the Oscilloscope's. The feed computes COLUMNS when a consumer wants `kWantSpectrogram` (independent of the other wants: a spectrogram-only consumer computes no spectrum; `noOutput_` counts the consumers that want no spectrum; TMM.13-fix1 M4: the worker owns the analyser and analyses without `gramMutex_`, staging finished columns and taking the lock only to copy them into the ring and publish the ring's state): `dsp::Spectrogram` (PQ Meter's reassigned spectrogram: three FFTs per hop, the 85 ms low chain below 200 Hz, the leakage mask, consensus, the tent splat combined by max onto 1024 rows of the display axis) on the worker, finished columns in a ring of `kGramRing` = 512 (`gramState` / `gramRead`: each column's 1024 rows, its centre, the tapped beat there and its peak). Its configuration is the consumers' `SpectrogramRequest` (`setSpectrogram(key, …)` / `clearSpectrogram`: the request with the most columns decides, ties keep the one in force; a hop / window change continues the column sequence, a new axis bumps `GramState::mapping`). Settles after as many silent columns as the deciding display shows; with no data it appends paced silence (the picture scrolls on in time). The face reads only the columns it has not seen, reduces each by max into its texel rows, caches level + source row, colours (tilt, the visual gate with a per-row floor estimate applied row by row, floor / ceiling, gamma, fade, ten colormaps by level / frequency / brightness) and uploads only the changed columns (or gate-moved rows) into its ring texture; scroll mode draws one or two textured quads behind a sub-column servo, Loop places columns by the tapped beat. The track tuning's degrees come from `MetaTarget::pitchGuides` (`DocTarget`: the device's track's effective tuning).
- **Widget-owned textures (TX.spectrogram, `pq/ui/texture_pool.h`)**: `Renderer::images()` is a `TexturePool` (`SceneTextures::images`, `PaintResources::images`, `FacePainter::setImages` / `images()`; null without a GPU) through which a widget or face creates an RGBA8 texture, updates rects of it (`Renderer::updateTexture`: staged now, on the GPU before the next frame's draws; a one-texel-wide column is a rect of w = 1 with rowPitch 4) and destroys it, while painting. The renderer detaches the pool when it goes (calls become no-ops), so a face may outlive it (it holds the pool through `share()`). The canvas and the FaceDisplay pass it to the faces' painters.

### Analytic responses (EQ.2: `pq/graph/response_rule.h`, `pq/graph/response.h`, `pq/document/group_response.h`; docs/plans/EQ.md "Analytic responses")
- **Response rules (graph)**: `AtomSignature::response` (atoms.h) is an optional `ResponseRule`: given ω, z = e^−jω, the atom's known non-signal input values (durations in samples) and which inputs carry the signal, it writes the complex gain from each signal input to each output and returns Exact / Approximate / Unavailable (src/graph/src/response_rules.cpp). Rules: add, sub, neg, mul / div / muladd by a non-signal value, lerp (t known), select / mux / demux (choice known), channel / pack (the evaluator maps their channels), z1, delay / delay_cubic / delay_allpass (the kernels' own interpolators exactly: z^n((1−f) + f z), the Catmull-Rom taps, z^n(c + z)/(1 + c z), with the kernels' clamps), gain / gain_add (settled); tanh, fast_tanh, sin, fast_sin (slope 1) and clip (1 when lo < 0 < hi) are Approximate. No rule: nonlinear (Unavailable through it). EQ.1's `svf` adds its rule from `dsp/svf_design.h` the same way. EQ.1b: a rule may keep `AtomSignature::responseCacheBytes` of scratch per node and channel across one evaluation (`ResponseArgs::cache`, filled on the `fresh` first call, preallocated by `prepare`): the svf rule designs once per evaluation and keeps its sections as ω-independent quadratics in u = e^jω − 1 (`dsp::svfSectionPoly`; u per point in `ResponseArgs::ejw1`), so a 32-band EQ at 1024 points costs one design per band (12.0 → 1.2 ms per evaluate).
- **Evaluator (graph, `LinearResponse`)**: works on an inferred (flattened, typed, pre-optimise) TypedPatch between two ports. prepare() (allocates; once per structural revision): signal = forward from the source (its inputs cut), relevant = signal ∩ backward from the sink; one complex unknown per (relevant port, channel); the non-signal inputs of relevant nodes become a CONTROL program (consts, params, `sr`, pure kernels through graph's constant folding — the runtime's kernels —, channel / pack, a `curve` atom through a caller-given curve; anything else is unknown); the unknowns' dependency graph is cut into SCCs (Tarjan). evaluate() (no allocation): control program, each rule once (frequency-dependent ones again per point; z1 terms read one shared z slot), then per batch of 8 points (the points are SIMD lanes, struct of arrays) substitution in dependency order and complex Gaussian elimination with partial pivoting over each loop only. H per (source channel, sink channel). Status: Exact / Approximate / Unavailable (a relevant atom without a usable rule, a gain that is an unknown signal). TX.group-oversample: OVERSAMPLE REGIONS — a node of a ×n region is evaluated at ω/n (`LinearResponse::Point`: ω, z, e^jω − 1 per rate r = log2 n; the z1 terms read a z slot per rate), its `sr` and durations at n·sr; the converters are evaluated at the base ω by their rules (the half-band cascades as the kernels compute them, float coefficients), so through a region H = up(ω) · H_inner(ω/n, n·sr) · down(ω); the images the up filter leaks (folded back by the down) are ignored (≤ −116 dB to 20 kHz); a phase / bool converter has no rule. Verified against interpreted impulse responses (tests/engine/test_linear_response.cpp, ×2 / ×4 / ×8 and two regions in series) and against a rendered EQ at 4× (tests/app/test_group_oversample.cpp). TX.mod-bands: SET PORTS — prepare() takes candidate flat output ports whose value the caller gives (`setPorts()` / `setValue`, like a param): the control program reads them without evaluating their drivers and the signal pass stops at their node, so a control an LFO / envelope / the input itself modulates gives H with the control frozen at the value given (verified against the circuit with that value as a param) instead of Unavailable.
