- **Document entry (`doc::GroupResponse`)**: a group's In → Out response. prepare(project, lowered, devices, group): finds the group's call in the lowered track patches, infers that patch with probes on the call's `input` and `y`, prepares the evaluator between them, and maps every param it reads named `d<id>__<name>` to the device control (its callee declaration; driven controls' knob positions, their `curve` atoms to the control's curve via LoweredProject::curves). update(project) re-reads the controls (paramValue / paramPosition / controlCurve): a knob drag or a curve edit never relowers; a structural edit (a literal value block, a band added) needs prepare() again. Measured (check preset): a 32-section feed-forward chain of atoms (288 unknowns, 96 params) at 1024 points 0.6 ms per evaluate. EQ.4: `prepareMany` (several groups, one inference per track) and `stamp()` (changes when update() changed a value). TX.mod-bands: every DRIVEN control's final value (`CurveSite::value` / `valuePort`: the node after its last stage, the one reaching the pin; matched in the flat patch by NodeId through `TypedPatch::origin`) is a set port, so its knob position param and `curve` atom are behind the cut; it is held at `evalCurve(controlCurve, position)` with position = the live modulated one (`setLive(i, pos)`, `driven()` lists the controls as Control bindings) or else the knob's (`paramPosition`).

### Response displays (EQ.4: `src/views/src/panel/response_elements.cpp`, `pq/views/panel/group_responses.h`; docs/plans/EQ.md "Displays (EQ.4)")
- **`response`** (panel element; slot 0 a Signal binding on a group's output `y` — it names the group and is the analyser's tap —, slot 1 optionally its `input`: both tapped by the lowering, panel.h "Displays tapped by the lowering"): the group's analytic magnitude response. x = log(1 + f / 10 Hz) / log(2001) over 0 … 20 kHz (the band Frequency knob's travel); y dB ±6 / 12 / 24 / 48 (a chip; view state). Layers: the analyser (`devices::SpectrumPlot` on the same axis: `SpectrumAxis::offsetHz`, `SpectrumPlotStyle::alpha`; output, input dimmed behind the IN chip; SPEC chip), the teal grid (theme `device.responseGrid`), each band's own curve (its colour, `signal_theme::kBands` by its place in the chain; filled toward 0 dB, its line fading out near 0 dB), the total (`device.response`, mint: fill, two glow passes, core; dashed when Approximate; none and "no curve" when Unavailable), the nodes (gain types at their gain, others on their own curve; numbered; selected: a ring; driven controls: GU.13's live modulation range along the control's axis, or a ring). The bound group decides: an EQ (`eqInfo`) shows the ACTIVE CHAIN (Stereo: A; L/R, M/S: the Edit Chain block's) and, split, the other chain's total and nodes at 35 % alpha; a band (`bandInfo`) its curve and one node; any other group its In → Out curve. Gestures (PanelTarget; one undo step each): double-click empty → `addBand` (bell at the pointer's f / dB; the new band selected), drag a node → frequency + gain (Q for the gainless types; Shift: a tenth), wheel → Q (a run = one step), Alt + wheel → `setBandStages` ±1, double-click a node → gain 0 dB, right-click → Type / Stages / Bypass / Solo / Delete, click → select, Delete → `removeBand` of the selected band.
- **Areas** (EQ.4d): the `response` element is a stack of three areas, top to bottom MAGNITUDE (the above), PHASE and DELAY, each opened / closed by its title chip (MAG / PHASE / DELAY; a closed area's whole bar), independently (view state `magnitude` / `phase` / `delay`, defaults open / open / closed; per element in the PanelTarget, not saved). A closed area is a 15-unit bar; the open ones share the rest 3 : 1 : 1, so the element keeps its size (the panel's layout does not move). Only open areas derive anything. **Phase**: the active chain's total in degrees — WRAPPED to ±180° (EQ.4b, the default: a fixed scale with rules at ±90°, the line cut where it wraps, EQ.4c's dotted markers at each wrap) or UNWRAPPED (auto-scaled to ≤ 3 rule spans; the WRAP / UNWRAP chip, view state `unwrap`; each step's turn count from the group delay, trapezoid over the step, so a step of many turns keeps them) — the selected band faint. **Delay**: group delay τ = −dφ/dω in ms, 0 ms at the floor, auto-scaled up to the largest drawn value + 8 % in ≤ 4 steps of 1-2-5 ms (labels in ms, the unit beside the top); true values: a negative delay (a minimum-phase cut near its centre) lowers the floor into a shaded band, at the same scale while it fits in a quarter of the strip, else squeezed into that quarter. τ at grid point k = −arg(H(f_k + ε_k) · conj H(f_k)) / (2π·ε_k), ε_k = 10⁻³ of the step to the next point: a second evaluation on the shifted grid (its own GroupResponses key, so both stay cached); exact while 2π·ε·τ < π (τ < 1 / (2ε): ≥ 2.5 s at 20 kHz, 39 s at 1.27 kHz), and it is τ(f + ε/2) to second order. (EQ.4b's difference of neighbouring unwrapped points aliased wherever 2π·Δf·τ > π — a 23-stage Q 5 allpass at 1.27 kHz drew −36 ms for its 59.6 ms peak.) Cost: one more evaluation of what changed while the delay area (or unwrapped phase) is open — a 32-band drag frame 0.82 ms → 1.3 ms in check.
- **`bandtype`**: a value block's Type as the filter glyph + short code (LP HP BP NO PK LS HS AP) in the band's colour (`band` style field: its index), a menu on click; the EQ strip's columns. Each column also has a `shape` bound to its band by a Panel binding (`embeddedGroup` counts Panel bindings of kind `panel` only), lit while that band is selected.
- **Selection and view state** (`PanelTarget::selectGroup` / `viewValue`): kept by the target, so they survive the panel's rebuilds (a band op relays the strip). **Mode**: the doc target sets a value block whose `eqRole` is Mode through `ops::setEqMode` (the first split copies A into B), whatever control shows it.
- **Curves** (`views::GroupResponses`, one per ViewContext). EQ.4b: the lowering is the APP'S OWN COMPILE: `ProjectLink::attach` sets `ViewContext::setLoweringProvided`, and each compile hands its `LoweredProject` (now a `shared_ptr<const>`: shared, no copy) to the context right after lowering, before the build (`ViewContext::setLowering(serial, snapshot, lowered)`; posted to the UI thread with a compile thread; `serial` = `ViewContext::docSerial()` of the compiled snapshot, stamped when the compile is queued); `currentLowering()` is the last one while no structural change (or new device library) came after its serial. After a structural edit the groups prepared before keep their responses (their controls still re-read per snapshot) and a group new in the edit passes through in a series (`waiting()`) until the compile of the new structure arrives (`view_change::kResponses`: the Device view's panel hosts and the circuit's canvas and panel windows repaint); another compile of the same structure keeps what is prepared. Without an app (tests, scenes) one `doc::lower` of the document per structural revision (`ViewContext::structuralRevision`: a kChangeStructural edit, its undo / redo, a reset, a new device library), made on the UI thread when a display first asks. The curve cache also keys on the sample rate. An embedded panel's forwarding target forwards `responses()` and `run()`. `want(groups)` prepares the groups a display draws together (`GroupResponse::prepareMany`: one inference per track patch); `get` re-reads the controls once per snapshot (`update`, `stamp()` changes only when a value it reads changed); `curve(group, hz, key)` evaluates and caches per group (the response and phase displays share one evaluation; every display uses one 768-point grid). A chain that is a PURE SERIES of its bands (In → b1 → … → bn → Out by main links, nothing else touching them) is drawn as the matrix product of the bands' own curves (`series`; an off band passes through), exact, so a drag re-evaluates the one band it moves: on a 32-band EQ (check preset) a drag frame (control re-read, band + product, the response display's geometry) ≈ 1.0 ms; the frame after a structural edit (a 32nd band added) was ≈ 9 ms with the lowering on the UI thread (≈ 13 ms in EQ.4's run) and is ≈ 2.5 ms with the app's (the curve as it was); the frame the compile's lowering arrives in prepares and evaluates the 32 bands, ≈ 7.8 ms (EQ.4b). Evaluating the 32-band chain group whole was ≈ 8.5 ms (stereo: every svf rule per channel per point). Other chains (a user's parallel wiring) use the chain group's own response. TX.mod-bands: per paint the response display gives every drawn response's driven controls their live ring position (`PanelTarget::modulation(binding)->current`, the host's 30 Hz poll: the midpoint of what the modulation swept since the last poll; the tap's voice for polyphonic modulation; none when stopped → the knob) and draws each node at its modulated frequency / gain (a faint ring marks the knob's place): the band curve, the total and the node move with the modulation, values only — no lowering, no prepare, no allocation.

### Waveforms (T5.5 / T5.6: `pq/ui/waveform.h`, `pq/views/waveforms.h`, `src/views/src/arrangement/audio_paint.cpp`, `pq/views/audio_clip/audio_clip_view.h`)
- **Drawing** (`ui::drawWaveform(dl, data, {frame0, frame1, rect, gain, reverse, colour, clipX0/1})`): `WaveformData` = the overview's levels as plain spans (+ optional sample window), so `ui` does not depend on `media`. Picks the coarsest level whose bucket ≤ frames per device px (≤ 5 buckets per column), below 256 frames per px the samples when the window covers the columns (min / max, then the line between samples), else level 0 stretched (`approx`). Only the columns inside the clip rect / [clipX0, clipX1) are generated: cost ∝ visible px. One strip per channel per run of columns: two vertices at each column centre plus a pair at each end, no feather (pixel-centre sampling gives each column exactly its rows). `ui::rasterWaveform` writes the same columns straight into an 8-bit coverage tile (no triangles: the arrangement's tiles).
- **Store** (`views::Waveforms`, owned by the `ViewContext`): `overview(sampleId)` / `window(sampleId, a, b)`; without a UI poster synchronous (tests, scenes), with one on two `media::AnalysisWorker`s (overviews; windows), results back through the poster (one pending post), `view_change::kWaveforms`. Cache: `<project>/cache/waveforms` when `setProjectCache(true)` (the app), else the shared directory the app sets (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\pqdaw\waveforms`, LRU 1 GiB), else none — tests never write a cache unless they enable one. Dropped on new / open; entries whose file changed are dropped on re-resolution.
- **Arrangement**: an audio clip = title strip + name (shared with note clips: `paintClipChrome` / `paintClipBorder`), badges, the waveform (`Model::audioLayout`: per clip snapshot + tempo map, the speed ratio resolved once; `forEachAudioPiece` maps clip-local px to source frames: warp Off one linear piece under one tempo, else 32-px pieces exact at their ends; Re-Pitch one piece per content pass, mirrored when reversed), fade shading + curves, the crossfade X and the handles in `Model::overlay` (untextured geometry appended after the row's tile quads by `flushTiles`). Waveform tiles as the T3.8-fix1 note tiles (key: clip snapshot, device px per tick, body height, the overview's `builtFrames`; built in `onFrame` within the texel budget once the zoom settles; stale tiles stretched meanwhile); direct drawing for drag previews, clips wider than a tile and deep zoom. The editor treats both clip kinds through `doc::ClipSlot` (move / copy / trim with the file-end limits / split / range carve = split + delete / clipboard by kind) and adds the handle drags (`DragKind::Fade`, `Gain`: one store gesture each).
- **Audio Clip View** (`AudioClipView`, in the ClipDetail slot next to the piano roll; shown when the detail clip is an audio clip): box controls bound to `doc::ops::setAudioClip*` (box drags = one store gesture), the whole sample (`SampleDisplay`) with the region, start marker (`SetAudioClip`) and Re-Pitch loop brace drags.

### Renderer, textures and glyph atlas (ui, T0.7 / T0.8; T0.8-fix1, T0.7-fix4: `pq/ui/renderer.h`, `atlas.h`, `text.h`)
- **Frame protocol** (Swapchain::endFrame, OffscreenTarget::submit): wait the slot's fence → `Renderer::upload(slot, layers)` → begin the command buffer → `recordTransfers(cb, slot)` → begin rendering → `record(cb, slot, frame)` → submit. Slots are used round-robin. Nothing in the renderer waits for the GPU (no `vkDeviceWaitIdle`, no `submitAndWait`); the slot fence wait is the only CPU–GPU sync.
- **Partial redraw (T0.7-fix1)**: a frame is a `FrameDesc` = ordered `DrawLayer`s + `full` or a list of disjoint whole-pixel damage rects (`Damage` keeps ≤ 8, merging overlapping/tiling ones) + the clear colour. Targets keep a persistent colour image (OffscreenTarget's own image; the Swapchain renders into its own persistent image and copies it whole into the acquired swapchain image, since swapchain images are not guaranteed to keep their contents). Full frames CLEAR; partial frames LOAD, `vkCmdClearAttachments` the rects and draw every command once per rect it reaches, scissored to clip ∩ rect, so each damaged pixel sees the clear then exactly the primitives covering it in list order: bit-identical to a full redraw (tested over 100 random sequences each for draw lists, a retained layer and a widget tree). A transient layer is copied into the slot's buffers each frame; a retained layer (`retained` id < 4, `version`) stays on the GPU per slot and is re-copied only when its version changes, and is indexed in runs of 64 triangles with vertex bounds so a partial frame draws only the runs reaching the damage. `Swapchain::preserved()` is false after a recreate (resize): the next frame must be full. The app draws in full when the damage covers > 50 % of the target, on resize/scale/theme changes and for scenes that cannot report damage.
- **Staging ring**: texture bytes are copied into persistently mapped 1 MiB chunks when they are handed over (`createTexture`, `updateTexture`, atlas dirty rects at `upload`), queued, bound to the next frame at `upload(slot)`, recorded as buffer → image copies between two batched barriers, and recycled when that slot comes round again. Overlapping copies to one texture in one frame are ordered (a covered copy is dropped, a partial overlap goes into a later batch behind a barrier).
- **Textures** (`TextureId`): `kAtlasTexture` (0) = the shared sprites (white texel, glow blob), valid on any atlas page; `atlasPageTexture(p)` (1..kMaxAtlasPages) = glyph page p; user textures from `kFirstUserTexture` (widgets reach them through `Renderer::images()`, a `TexturePool`: TX.spectrogram). `createTexture` (async), `updateTexture(id, rect, rgba, rowPitch)` (async), `destroyTexture(id)` (deferred until every frame slot in flight at destroy time has been recycled; the id is then reused, so a draw list must not use a destroyed id). The draw list merges `kAtlasTexture` with any atlas page, so fills and labels stay one draw command.
- **Glyph atlas**: up to 4 pages of 1024² R8 coverage (each also holds the shared sprites), sampled through the view swizzle (1, 1, 1, R) so one pipeline/shader serves glyphs, sprites and RGBA images. Pages open on demand; when all are full the least recently used page not touched by the current frame is cleared (generation++); glyphs are re-rasterised on next use. If every page is in use this frame the glyph is dropped, counted (`Stats::atlasFull`) and reported once on stderr; its run is rebuilt on a later frame.
- **Tile atlas (T3.8-fix1, `pq/ui/tile_atlas.h`)**: cached raster tiles that a widget rasterises once on the CPU (off its paint path) and draws as one textured quad per frame instead of re-emitting the geometry. `Renderer::tiles()` owns one `TileAtlas` (also `SceneTextures::tiles`, `PaintResources::tiles`; null without a GPU, so CPU-only paints draw directly): up to `kMaxTilePages` = 4 R8 pages of 2048² (16 MiB GPU, the bound; no CPU copy of the pages), sampled like glyph pages through the (1, 1, 1, R) swizzle, so a coverage mask drawn with vertex colour c works for any colour. Page texture ids are the table's last four (`tilePageTexture(p)`; user ids end below them). `allocate(w, h)` = shelves (heights rounded to 4) with a transparent one-texel border all round; per-page LRU eviction by frame stamp, never a page touched in the current frame; a cleared page's generation goes up and its tiles read `!valid()`. `write(tile)` returns the zeroed texels (incl. border) queued for upload; `upload()` creates newly opened pages' images, stages the queue in one copy, then `clearUploads()` + `beginFrame()` (`TestDriver::step` calls `beginFrame()` only). A tile drawn 1:1 with uv on texel boundaries is texel-exact. Users: clip note previews (T3.8-fix1), automation envelopes (T3.x-fix-paint) and circuit symbol bodies (T4.2). **CPU coverage rasteriser (T4.2, `pq/ui/raster.h`)**: `rasterizeCoverage(dl, dst, w, h, pitch, origin)` renders untextured draw-list triangles into an R8 tile as the GPU would (pixel centres, vertex alpha interpolated, positions snapped to 1/256 px with exact integer edge functions and the top-left rule, coverage accumulated as alpha over), so a tile drawn in colour k equals the geometry drawn directly in k (tested to 1/255 at 1×/1.5×/2×).
- **Code editor** (T4.4, `pq/ui/code_editor.h`): a multi-line UTF-8 editor widget — caret / selection at code point boundaries, keyboard and mouse editing (docs/HOTKEYS.md), Enter keeps the indent, its own scrolling, line numbers, syntax colours through a `CodeHighlighter` (per line, with a carried state such as "inside a block comment"), `CodeMark`s (wavy underline, gutter marker, the message after the line). User edits change its text at once and are reported through `onEdit(offset, removed, inserted, typing)`; undo belongs to the owner (Ctrl+Z bubbles up), which applies changes back with `replace`. Paint shapes only the visible lines (one run per coloured token, through the text system's run cache); no caret blink (idle when focused).
- **Text frames**: `TextSystem::beginFrame()` once per frame (the app loop and `TestDriver` call it). A `TextRun` stays valid until the next `beginFrame()`/`clearRunCache()`: the run cache is trimmed only there (M8). `TextRun` carries per-cluster caret stops (`carets`, `caretX`, `hitTest`, LTR) and `shapeTruncated`/`drawTruncated` cut at a cluster boundary and append "…" (`truncated`, `visibleBytes`).

## 4. Data and file formats
- **Project**: a directory: `project.pqd` (JSON, UTF-8, `"schemaVersion"` first, currently 7 (3 = device chains (T4.1: every track's `"devices"`; 2 → 3 gives every MIDI track the default instrument with new ids); 4 = modulation wires (T4.7); 5 = user devices (T4.4-fix1: a device's own patch text, `"source"`); 6 = racks (T4.8: a device's `"rack"`); 7 = audio (T5.3: `"kind": "audio"` tracks, audio clips tagged `"kind": "audio"`, the top-level `"samples"` table; golden `tests/document/fixtures/audio/project.pqd`)) and ticks at PPQ 221,760, older files migrate on load (1 → 2 multiplies every tick by 8; `tools/fixtures/migrate_pqd_schema1.py` does the same to fixture text); layout in `pq/document/serialize.h` and the golden `tests/document/fixtures/full/project.pqd`: ticks and ids as integers, rationals as `"n/d"`, floating point as shortest round-trip numbers, embedded tuning definitions), `patches/*.pqp`, `samples/` (T5.8: samples are referenced where they are; Collect All and Save copies the used ones into `samples/imported/`), `cache/`.
- **JIT cache entries** (T1b.4, `src/jit/src/cache.cpp`): `<32 hex digits of the key>.pqjit` in the user cache or the AOT seed: a 64-byte header (magic `PQJITCCH`, format version, key, payload size, xxh3-128 of the payload), then the stage function names, the pointer-table description (host slots, kernel offsets into the executable), the IR hash (verify), statistics and the COFF object. Valid only for the executable that wrote it (its file hash is part of the key). Deletable at any time.
- **Browser index** (T5.9, `pq/library/index_file.h`): `%LOCALAPPDATA%\pqdaw\library\index.pqi` (64-byte header: magic `PQLIBIDX`, version, snapshot sequence, counts, payload size and hash; then the places and every column of the index as raw 8-byte-aligned arrays and the string pool; written atomically at most every 5 s after changes) and `index.pqj` (the journal since that snapshot: `PQLIBJNL` header with the snapshot's sequence, then hashed records of relisted directories; a torn tail is dropped). Both are a cache: deletable, rebuilt by the next scan. `tags.json` next to them is user data (colour collections and free tags keyed by volume serial + NTFS file ID, path + size as the fallback).
- **User library files** (T5.11, `pq/document/library_files.h`): one JSON format per kind, written and read by the project serializer's own device / clip / sample / tuning code (same strict reader, same `"schemaVersion"`; library files start at 7), each `{"format": "<kind>", "schemaVersion", "name", …}`: `.pqpreset` (`"devices"`: one device — a stock device's values, a user device with its `"source"`, or a rack with its nested chains, mappings, macro names and variations; the device object comes right after the name, so `"kind"` / `"rack"` sit in the first 4 KiB the indexer sniffs, Q153), `.pqdevice` (a user device: `"kind"`, `"patch"` (the stock patch it was forked from), `"origin"` (that stock file relative to `stdlib/`, so its imports resolve), `"source"` lines, optional `"panel"` lines), `.pqtuning` (`"colour"`, `"def"`: EDO / MOS / Scala exactly as a project embeds a main tuning), `.pqclip` (`"clip"`: a note or audio clip; an audio clip carries its one `"samples"` entry, its path relative to the .pqclip's folder when the file lies below it). A clip assigned to a main tuning is written with that tuning as a private one. Ids in a file are the saved entity's; loading gives fresh ids (`ops::cloneDevice`, `ops::placeClip`). The User Library (`Documents\pqdaw\User Library`) holds `Presets/<Device>/`, `Defaults/<Device>.pqpreset`, `Racks/`, `Devices/`, `Tunings/`, `Clips/` (+ `Clips/Samples/`), `Samples/`; `<Device>` is the device's display name, which also names a preset's device without reading the file (`views::Library::presetPatch`).
- **Drag-out files** (T5.12): audio clips dragged to Explorer are rendered to `%TEMP%\pqdaw\dragout\<clip name>.wav` (32-bit float, or 24-bit PCM with the `views.dragOut24Bit` preference; `PQ_DRAGOUT_DIR` overrides the folder for tests), emptied at start and at exit.
- **Patches**: `.pqp` text in the patch language ([PATCH_LANGUAGE.md](PATCH_LANGUAGE.md)). The visual layout is stored in the same file in a `layout { }` block (T4.3: keys for anonymous nodes, PATCH_LANGUAGE §2.2; written into hand-written files by `graph::spliceLayout`, which changes only that block).
- **Golden audio** for tests: 32-bit float mono/stereo WAV (`pq/media/wav_file.h` writes and reads them; `pq/engine/wav_file.h` forwards), 48 kHz, ≤ 1 MB each, in `tests/golden/audio/` from the patches in `tests/golden/patches/`, compared by `tests/golden/golden_audio.h` with a tolerance per test: absolute (max |Δ|, default 1e-4) AND spectral (Hann STFT of 4096, semitone bands from 20 Hz, dB re a full-scale sine floored at −80 dB, default 0.2 dB). Both backends must match the same goldens (T1.10; regeneration and pqc commands in `tools/README.md`).

## 5. Build configurations
- **Generator**: Ninja via `CMakePresets.json`. **Compiler**: MSVC v143 (cl.exe), x64. **Language**: C++20 (`/std:c++20`).
- **CRT**: match the prebuilt LLVM. All targets use the same `CMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY` (see `cmake/PqToolchain.cmake`). There is **no MSVC Debug configuration**, because the LLVM libs are release builds. The `dev` preset is `RelWithDebInfo` with optimisations reduced; `release` is fully optimised per translation unit (`/O2`, no `/GL` / `/LTCG`, so objects are cacheable and links fast: routine verification); `ship` is `release` plus whole-program optimisation (`/GL` + `/LTCG /OPT:REF /OPT:ICF`) for shipping builds and recorded benchmark numbers (Q91).
- **Flags**: `/W4 /permissive- /Zc:__cplusplus /utf-8 /arch:AVX2 /fp:precise`. Warnings are errors in our code (not third-party). SIMD kernels can be compiled with stricter or looser flags per file where measured.
- **CPU baseline check** (`pq/core/cpu_baseline.h`, T0.1-fix1): `/arch:AVX2` code may use the whole x86-64-v3 set (AVX, AVX2, FMA, BMI1/2, LZCNT, F16C, MOVBE). `pq_add_executable` links the OBJECT library `pq_cpu_check`: a CRT initialiser in `.CRT$XCB` that runs before every C++ static initialiser and `main`, and exits 1 with a message naming the missing features. It and the query it calls (`pq_cpu_baseline`, archived into `pq_core`) are C (no inline code shared with `/arch:AVX2` C++), compiled without `/arch:AVX2` and without `/GL` (`pq_target_baseline_defaults`). Test hook: `PQ_TEST_CPU_DISABLE=avx2[,fma,…]` forces features off in `pq_query_cpu_baseline()`, and so in `cpuFeatures()`.
- **Allocation trap** (`pq/core/alloc_trap.h`): the OBJECT library `pq_alloc_trap` replaces global `operator new/delete`. `pq_add_test` links it into every test executable (both presets), where an allocation or free inside an `RtScope` fails the running doctest case; use `pq::alloc_trap::countRtViolations([&]{ … })` to assert that an RT entry point does not allocate. `pq_add_executable` links it only when `PQ_ALLOC_TRAP_IN_APPS` is ON (default ON for dev, OFF for release), where a violation prints a stack trace and aborts. `malloc`/`free` and OS allocators are not interposable under `/MT` and are not caught.
- **Perf-labelled tests** (T0.x-flake): the default test run (`ctest --preset check|dev|release`, which `scripts/build.ps1` uses) must pass on a machine busy with other builds, so test assertions measure load-independent things: counts, deterministic or TSC-stamped timelines, the thread's own CPU time (`QueryThreadCycleTime`), and "eventually" waits of seconds. Wall-clock checks that need the scheduler to be on time live under the CTest label `perf`, which the test presets exclude: doctest cases tagged `* doctest::test_suite("perf")` in executables registered with `pq_add_test(<name> PERF …)` (they get a second entry `<name>_perf`; the default entry passes `--test-suite-exclude=perf`), and `pqdaw_window_selftest_perf` (the window self-test with `PQ_PERF_CHECKS=1`, which enforces the glyph-hitch interval and partial-redraw CPU-ratio criteria). Run them on a quiet machine with `./scripts/build.ps1 -Preset release -Perf` or `ctest --test-dir build/release -L perf`. A bare `ctest --test-dir build/<preset>` (no preset) runs both.
- **Compiler cache and test tiers** (T0.x-buildspeed): debug info is `/Z7` (embedded per object; dev only); C++20 module dependency scanning is off (`CMAKE_CXX_SCAN_FOR_MODULES`: pqdaw uses no named modules, and the scan is an extra uncached preprocessor run per TU). SYSTEM include directories are passed as plain `-I`, with third-party warnings silenced by `/external:anglebrackets /external:W0` (pqdaw's own headers are included with `"..."`, third-party ones with `<...>`): sccache hashes `-external:I<dir>` verbatim, which defeated sharing for every TU that sees a `_deps` include directory. Precompiled headers are disabled when the cache is in use (SDL3's PCH is not cacheable). When `scripts/get-sccache.ps1` has installed sccache it is the compiler launcher (`cmake/PqCompilerCache.cmake`), with one cache shared by every checkout and worktree; `scripts/build.ps1` sets it up. Since a cached object may come from another worktree, compiled code never contains a checkout's absolute paths: the path macros (`PQ_SOURCE_DIR`, `PQ_TEST_DATA_DIR`, `PQ_ASSETS_DIR`, `PQ_SOURCE_ASSETS_DIR`, `PQ_ALLOC_TRAP_PROBE`, `PQC_EXE`) name arrays defined in `pq_build_paths`, one generated TU compiled without the cache (`pq_use_build_paths`). `pq_add_test` registers each executable twice: the default entry excludes the doctest suite `slow`, and `<exe>_slow` (ctest label `slow`) runs only it; the test presets (so `build.ps1`) skip the `slow` and `perf` labels; `build.ps1 -Full` adds `slow`, while `perf` stays opt-in (`-Perf`). T0.x-buildspeed2: the cache's base directories are every checkout root (one shared server), and `build.ps1` rewrites the other checkouts' header paths that cache hits leave in `.ninja_deps` (`scripts/NinjaDeps.cs`). `SHARDS n` / `SLOW_SHARDS n` split an entry into n parallel ctest entries (`--pq-shard=k/n`: the shared test main keeps every n-th case of each doctest suite; each test process also gets its own `%TEMP%`). The configure step writes the target graph (`build/<preset>/pq_graph.json`: kind, source directories, link dependencies of every module, executable, test and bench) for `scripts/affected.ps1` / `build.ps1 -Affected`. Verification uses the `check` preset (release flags without `/DNDEBUG`); `PQ_OPTIMIZED` marks optimised builds for wall-clock checks.
- **Third-party code** comes in via `FetchContent` (through `pq_fetch_declare`, which shares verified archives between checkouts) as release archives pinned by SHA-256 (`URL_HASH`), so configuring needs no VCS, and is recorded in `docs/DEPENDENCIES.md`. All of it is linked statically (`BUILD_SHARED_LIBS` is forced OFF). Only permissive licences: MIT, BSD, zlib, Apache-2.0, Boost, MPL-2.0 (file-level), OFL for fonts. No GPL; LGPL only if dynamically linked and justified.

## 6. Conventions
- Files: `snake_case.h/.cpp`. Types: `PascalCase`. Functions and variables: `camelCase`. Constants: `kPascalCase`. Members: `trailingUnderscore_`.
- Headers: `#pragma once`; public headers in `src/<module>/include/pq/<module>/…`; private code in `src/<module>/src/…`.
- Errors: `pq::Result<T>` (value or `pq::Error{code, message, location}`) on non-RT paths. Exceptions are allowed only at the outermost non-RT boundaries (file I/O, parsing), and are caught there. Everything reachable from the audio thread is `noexcept`.
- No global mutable state except explicit singletons in `app/` (JIT compiler, audio device manager).
- Comments explain *why*. Every RT-path function says in its header comment that it is RT-safe.
